## Functional

### Tracker lifecycle & data model

- **FR-1** `[Ph-1]` — System creates a device tracker from one of three inputs: (a) a customer template `[Ph-1]`, (b) an Excel import conforming to the per-customer schema `[Ph-2]` (deferred alongside DEF-15 — requires Template Schema Ingestor), or (c) manual entry `[Ph-2]`.
- **FR-2** `[Ph-1]` — **Tracker creation flow**. Cross-cutting content lives in topical FRs / NFRs — see FR-5 (uniqueness invariant), FR-40 (template.yaml structure), FR-84 (SP-alert routing — Source A Deliverables + Source B Milestones), FR-88 (owner identity model), NFR-8 / NFR-21 (HILDA-SP runtime coupling), PROJECT.md (PM ≡ TPM role lock). **SP architecture** (per `[D-083]`): HILDA's runtime SP coupling has three SP lists:
  - **`Deliverables_<customer_id>`** — per-customer Work Items list (e.g., `Deliverables_MMK`, `Deliverables_VZW`); one row per DeliveryItem; carries denormalized customer / device / milestone IDENTITY columns + work-item-level + TG-denormalized fields.
  - **`Milestones`** — single global list (one shared across all customers; scoped by `carrier` column); one row per milestone; carries milestone-level state (`status`, `target_date`, button timestamps, `download_package_*`).
  - **`Projects`** — existing global list reused; `project_id` → `(project_model, TPM)` lookup, where `TPM` is a **SharePoint Person/Group column** resolved by HILDA to a 3-tuple `(assigned_pm_id, pm_display_name, pm_email)` per `[D-088]`. Reused as-is; not created by `setup_milestone`.

  Ph-2 normalization plan deferred until production telemetry justifies it. **Field scope inventory** (where each field lives):
  - **Customer-level** (denormalized on Deliverables rows): `customer_id`. (`customer_jira_url` is HILDA-config-only in template.yaml per `[D-083]` — NOT in SP.)
  - **Device-level** (denormalized on Deliverables rows + on Milestones row): `model` / `project_model` (= `device_id` in template.yaml), `project_id` (corp-assigned numeric like `2479`; SP UI engineer assigns at `setup_milestone` time per FR-40).
  - **Milestone-level** (Milestones SP list row only — NOT denormalized on Deliverables rows): `target_date` (sole authoritative deadline per `[D-085]`), `status` (4-value enum per FR-6), `milestone_collection_started_at`, `milestone_submission_triggered_at`, `closed_all_items_triggered_at`, `refresh_requested_at`, `download_package_request_timestamp`, `download_package_url`, `download_package_status`, `download_package_generated_at`. Milestone identity (`milestone_id` = Milestones row PK `Id`; `milestone_name` = `Title`) is denormalized on Deliverables rows for SP-alert routing per FR-84 Source A.
  - **TG-level fields** (denormalized per-work-item; same value across all rows sharing the same `tg_name` within a `(device, milestone)`): `tg_path_id`, `tg_name`, `tg_owner_corp_usa_email`, `tg_owner_corp_id`, `tg_owner_name`, `tg_email_group_alias`, `corp_id_list`, `folder_routing_enabled`, `ingress_nsd`. All identity columns are free-form text per `[D-086]`. No `tg_owner_corp_email` field — never existed. No `default_cc_list` / `tracking_enabled` at TG-level — dropped per FR-56 (a) lock.
  - **Work-item-level** (unique per Deliverables row): `item_no`, `item_name`, `item_path_id`, `item_type`, `delivery_state`, `prior_delivery_state` (HILDA-managed audit per FR-7 Delayed/Blocked exit paths), `tracking_modality`, `owner_corp_usa_email`, `owner_corp_email`, `owner_corp_id`, `owner_name` (all 4 free-form text per `[D-086]`), `last_owner_contacted`, `last_owner_response_at` (HILDA-managed; set by FR-12 inbound parsing — feeds FR-10 no-response detection), `last_reminder_triggered_at`, `reminder_count` (HILDA-managed), `actual_completion_date`, `pm_approval_at`, `pm_approval_pm_id`, `doc_count`, `review_required`, `milestone_gating`, `no_customer_upload`, `force_tracking_enabled`, `manual_triage_required` (HILDA-set on FR-12 path (c.2) below-threshold; TPM-clearable), `plm_id` (HILDA-written at collection kickoff per `(device, milestone, owner_corp_id)` tuple per FR-8 step 2), form-factor flags (`handset`, `tablet`, `wearable`, `ir`, `osmr`, `rmr`, `hmr_smr` per `[D-084]`), `comment`, etc. **`assigned_pm_id` is NOT a column on Deliverables rows** per `[D-083]` — looked up at runtime via Projects SP list by `project_id`; PM identity may vary per `(customer, device, milestone)` triple, which the Projects list encodes via its `(project_id, project_model, TPM)` rows. **`TPM` is a SharePoint Person/Group column** per `[D-088]` — HILDA resolves it to a 3-tuple `(assigned_pm_id = TPM.User_name preferred (else email-local-part of TPM.Work_email); pm_display_name = TPM.Name; pm_email = TPM.Work_email)`. **`expected_completion_date` removed** per `[D-085]` — `Milestone.target_date` is sole authoritative deadline read from the Milestones SP list.

  **Write target semantics** (per `[D-083]`): **Milestone-level writes target the single Milestones SP list row** (e.g., TPM clicks Start Collection → SP web part writes `milestone_collection_started_at` to the single Milestones row for the milestone → one SP-alert fires → HILDA dispatches per FR-84 Source B routing). No N-row cascade write; no `[D-082]` cascade-dedup for milestone-level button writes (obsoleted per `[D-085]`). **Per-item writes target single Deliverables rows** (Approve, Mark Closed, cell edits, etc.). **HILDA reads via filtered REST GET** on the appropriate per-list path — Deliverables row by `?$filter=Model eq 'SM-S901U' and milestone_id eq 'LE-2' and item_no eq 7`; Milestones row by `?$filter=carrier eq 'MMK' and milestone_id eq 'LE-2' and project_model eq 'SM-S901U'`; Projects row by `?$filter=project_id eq 2479 and project_model eq 'SM-S901U'`. **Role nomenclature**: PM ≡ TPM throughout this document (see PROJECT.md Users line). HILDA's runtime SP coupling discipline (per `[D-083]`: 2 SP lists + reused Projects; HILDA never creates rows; no Postgres mirror; SP canonical for all entity data) is locked in **NFR-21**. **Ops-editable-only fields**: **on Deliverables rows**: `customer_id`, `model` (= `device_id`), `project_id` (TPM MUST NOT edit — misconfig breaks FR-13 NSD path construction, FR-77 carrier-folder routing, FR-84 SP-alert routing). **On Milestones rows**: `carrier`, `project_id`, `project_model`, `milestone_id`, `milestone_name` (same restriction). **`customer_jira_url`** is HILDA-config-only in template.yaml (not in SP per `[D-083]`); **`assigned_pm_id`** is resolved via Projects list lookup (not editable on Deliverables/Milestones rows; the Projects list itself is ops-managed — see Projects list maintenance below). Per-row TPM-editable fields are listed in FR-56 column model.

  **R&R lock**: SP UI engineer's TPM-triggered `setup_milestone` task is the **SOLE path for SP row creation** in both `Deliverables_<customer_id>` AND the Milestones SP list. **Granularity**: `setup_milestone` is per `(customer_id, device_id, milestone_id)` triple — **one milestone at a time**. **Per-run scope** (per `[D-083]`): the web part writes (i) **1 row to the global Milestones SP list** with milestone-level fields (`carrier`, `project_id`, `project_model`, `milestone_id`, `milestone_name`, `target_date`, `status = "Not Started"`, button timestamps initialized null, `download_package_*` initialized null); (ii) **N work-item rows to `Deliverables_<customer_id>`** (one per template-defined item for the chosen triple), each pre-populated with denormalized customer/device/milestone identity + work-item-level + TG-denormalized columns using template.yaml values. The FR-78 default work-item is the (N+1)th Deliverables row. **First-customer provisioning**: when `Deliverables_<customer_id>` does not yet exist, the web part provisions the SP list itself (columns + alert subscription + role permissions per `[D-073]`) as the first step of `setup_milestone`, then writes the Milestones row + N+1 Deliverables rows. **SP row creation trigger**: `setup_milestone` — bulk, milestone-scoped, TPM-initiated at onboarding/launch — sole row-creation path in Ph-1 (per FR-3 [Ph-2] deferral / DEF-22; mid-milestone PM/TPM on-demand add returns in Ph-2). Both `setup_milestone` and (Ph-2) on-demand add are executed by the same actor (SP UI engineer's web part). **Why one milestone at a time**: per FR-40 template structure, work-items under a given (customer, milestone) are SHARED across all devices; milestone-scoped onboarding is the natural granularity. **Operational lifecycle**: (1) new customer onboarded → first `setup_milestone` run provisions `Deliverables_<customer_id>` + writes 1 Milestones row + N+1 Deliverables rows for the first milestone; (2) new device launched for an existing customer → device added to template.yaml + TPM runs `setup_milestone` for the first milestone of that device (web part writes 1 new Milestones row + N+1 new Deliverables rows into existing lists); (3) milestones progress one at a time across device lifetime — TPM runs `setup_milestone` for the next milestone once the prior milestone completes, until end of device life. **Button availability**: the `setup_milestone` button is shown to TPM only when the triple does NOT yet have rows in `Deliverables_<customer_id>` AND no Milestones row exists for the triple; once active, the button is hidden — **re-run on an existing triple is impossible by UI**. **Atomic commit boundary** (per `[D-083]`): success creates the Milestones row + all N+1 Deliverables rows (+ provisions the Deliverables list itself when applicable); failure (auth error, SP service down, validation error) creates none and surfaces in SP UI to TPM — no partial state on SP. SP-alerts fire only after commit succeeds; **HILDA never sees partial state**. Failure handling: TPM-visible only in SP UI (SP UI engineer's web part runs in browser context, separate from HILDA service module, no access to HildaOpsAlert queue); TPM retries from the still-available button. Template.yaml is the **shared structural contract** between SP UI engineer and HILDA — both teams read it; SP UI engineer uses it as the field-and-default reference at `setup_milestone` time; HILDA uses it as structural reference + primary-key value set per the Runtime role section below.

  **HILDA NEVER creates SP rows — at any time, under any condition**. HILDA's role is exclusively **state transitions + non-default field updates** on already-existing rows: per-item state advances on Deliverables rows (`delivery_state` advances, `plm_id` assignment, `last_owner_contacted`, `actual_completion_date`, `last_reminder_triggered_at`, `reminder_count`, `prior_delivery_state` audit, etc.); milestone-level updates on Milestones row (`status` compute writeback — single Milestones-row update per `[D-083]`; `download_package_url` / `download_package_status` / `download_package_generated_at` outputs — single Milestones-row update). All via `[D-064]` REST writeback to existing rows; never `SpCrud.create_item`. ("Non-default" = HILDA-managed runtime values that diverge from the column-level defaults set by SP UI engineer at `setup_milestone` time.)

  **Template.yaml change semantics**:
  - **(a) Template.yaml is not a retroactive sync source** — once `setup_milestone` has run for a `(customer_id, device_id, milestone_id)` triple, that triple's SP rows are SP-canonical — both (i) the Milestones row for the triple AND (ii) the N+1 Deliverables rows for the triple's work-items. Template.yaml edits never propagate to existing rows of either list. Template.yaml edits affect only FUTURE `setup_milestone` runs.
  - **(b) SIGHUP HILDA after template.yaml change has no row-creation effect** — HILDA re-reads the template at SIGHUP to refresh its in-memory structural reference (canonical field names + primary-key value set), but does NOT create or modify any SP rows from the diff. **HILDA's write path is structurally insulated from template.yaml** — HILDA never writes to template.yaml, never writes SP rows in response to template.yaml diffs.
  - **(c) Runtime add / remove / reassign of DeliveryItems on an ACTIVE milestone is deferred to Ph-2 via FR-3 (DEF-22)** — in Ph-1, the per-milestone row set is **static after `setup_milestone`** (both the Milestones row and the Deliverables row set); no mid-milestone add/remove/reassign. In Ph-2 (when FR-3 lands), SP UI engineer's web part will create / update / remove SP rows directly on PM/TPM action; HILDA learns via SP-alert per `[D-047]` and propagates downstream.

  **Runtime role of template.yaml for HILDA**: HILDA reads template.yaml **ONLY at startup and on SIGHUP** — never at SP-alert receive time (resolution uses cached in-memory state per FR-84). Two purposes: (i) **canonical field-name reference** matching `core/src/template_schema/models.py` Pydantic schema per FR-40 three-tier name model; `sharepoint_integration` translates canonical → SP-internal via `customizations/sharepoint_config/customers/<customer_id>.yaml` per `[D-065]`; (ii) **primary key value set** — the specific `customer_id`, `device_id`, `milestone_id` values in template.yaml define the universe of `(customer, device, milestone)` tuples HILDA tracks. Template.yaml is HILDA's "what to track" manifest at the structural level — even though SP is canonical for row state. **HILDA bootstrap behavior** (per `[D-083]`): at process start, HILDA reads template.yaml for the primary-key tuple set; reads the **global Milestones SP list** via `$filter` to project tuples where `milestone_collection_started_at IS NOT NULL AND status ≠ 'Completed'` — this is the active-tracking universe (used by FR-26 PLM polling, FR-55 NSD monitoring, FR-25 CustomerJIRA polling, FR-11 deadline evaluation). HILDA also reads the **Projects SP list** at startup (cached; SIGHUP-refreshed) to populate the `project_id → (project_model, assigned_pm_id, pm_display_name, pm_email)` lookup table per `[D-083]` + `[D-088]`. For each active tuple, HILDA reads `Deliverables_<customer_id>` lazily / on-demand at SP-alert receive (per FR-84 Source A routing). Tuples present in template.yaml but absent from Milestones list are NOT tracked at runtime (awaiting `setup_milestone`); tuples present in Milestones list but absent from template.yaml are flagged as `STATUS-W001` orphan and surfaced in the diagnostic report; HILDA continues processing alerts for orphan tuples but logs each as suspect.

  **SP UI engineer + HILDA service R&R summary**:
  - **SP UI engineer**: SP-list schema provisioning per `[D-073]` (`Deliverables_<customer_id>` columns + alerts + Choice values + role-based access); SP row creation via `setup_milestone` TPM task (1 Milestones row + N+1 Deliverables rows per `[D-083]`) using template.yaml defaults; SP column-level defaults (Choice enums, BOOL defaults); SP-side button-write atomicity per `[D-068]` discipline. **Permissions**: `setup_milestone` button is gated by SP-list-level write permissions — any AD-authenticated TPM in the per-customer SP write-group can trigger; corp standard SP role-based access controls apply. **Template.yaml schema validation at setup_milestone time**: SP UI engineer's web part validates template.yaml values against SP Choice list membership + SP column types before writing rows; on mismatch, fails loudly in SP UI with field-level error; does NOT partially write. **CommunicationLog at setup_milestone**: NOT written — SP UI engineer's web part runs in browser context with no access to HILDA's CommunicationLog table (HILDA-PC-local Postgres per `[D-071]`); HILDA does not write CommunicationLog for row creation either (HILDA did not perform the creation per R&R lock). NFR-6 attribution is satisfied via the SP-alert audit trail (SP UI engineer's identity is in the SP-alert sender field + SP's built-in `Created` / `Author` system columns).
  - **HILDA service**: runtime SP reads via the 2-list + Projects coupling per `[D-083]` (Deliverables_<customer_id> per-customer Work Items + global Milestones + reused global Projects); state transitions + non-default field updates on existing rows via `[D-064]`; runtime resolution of SP-alerts per FR-84 (Source A Deliverables 5-field key; Source B Milestones 4-field key). **Resolution path on SP-alert receive**: see **FR-84**. **Runtime SP coupling discipline**: see **NFR-21** (per `[D-083]` coupling model; HILDA never creates rows; no Postgres mirror; SP canonical). **At setup_milestone time, SP UI engineer's web part pre-populates all static fields on the new SP rows** using template.yaml values per FR-40 template structure (template.yaml uses **HILDA-canonical names** per FR-40 three-tier name model; SP-internal column names are translated via `customizations/sharepoint_config/customers/<customer_id>.yaml` per `[D-065]`; authoritative SP-side schema = `docs/sp_ui_engineer/SP_lists_authoritative.xlsx`). DeliveryItems are grouped within a milestone by `tg_name` (anchors `[D-028]`); TG-level fields are denormalized per-work-item in template.yaml per **FR-40** — see FR-40 for details and the `TSC-W005` equality validator.

  **PM identity lookup chain** (per `[D-083]` + `[D-088]`): from a Deliverables row's `project_id` column, HILDA queries the global Projects SP list by `project_id` to retrieve the row's `(project_model, TPM)` pair. **`TPM` is a SharePoint Person/Group column**; HILDA extracts the 3-tuple `(assigned_pm_id, pm_display_name, pm_email)` per `[D-088]`: `assigned_pm_id = TPM.User_name` (preferred — e.g., `y.vasilyev`), fallback to email-local-part of `TPM.Work_email` when `User name` is null; `pm_display_name = TPM.Name` (single field — e.g., `Yury Vasilyev`); `pm_email = TPM.Work_email` (e.g., `y.vasilyev@partner.samsung.com`). All other Person/Group sub-fields ignored. Used at FR-9 outreach attribution (`pm_display_name` in signature; `pm_email` informational), FR-19/FR-51 PM-credentialed dispatch (`assigned_pm_id` is the credential-path slug), FR-25 (b) CustomerJIRA self-outreach (`pm_email` is the recipient), FR-65 Send Reminder (`pm_display_name` in dashboard render), `CommunicationLog` attribution per NFR-5/NFR-6 (`assigned_pm_id` as `originating_pm_id`). The Projects list lookup is cached at HILDA startup (SIGHUP-refreshed); architecture-phase to confirm caching policy. Orphans (Deliverables row's `project_id` not found in Projects list, OR Projects row's `project_model` mismatches Deliverables row's `project_model`) flagged `STATUS-W004` at SP-alert receive; HILDA continues processing but logs each as suspect.

  **Projects SP list maintenance**: the Projects SP list is global and pre-existing per `[D-083]` (reused; NOT created by `setup_milestone`). Each row carries `project_id`, `project_model`, and `TPM` (SharePoint Person/Group column — assigned PM corp-AD identity; HILDA-side 3-tuple resolution per `[D-088]`). New `project_id → TPM` rows are added by SP UI engineer's separate provisioning workflow (or ops) at the time a new project_id is corp-assigned for a new customer/device launch — outside `setup_milestone` scope. The Projects list lifecycle is administered independently of per-customer Deliverables lists.

  **Cross-references** for runtime semantics of pre-populated fields:
  - **Owner identity** (per-item + TG-level free-form text per `[D-086]`; preference rule; change-event semantics): see **FR-88**.
  - **`Milestone.target_date`** (Milestones SP list row; sole TPM-editable deadline per `[D-085]`; no per-item override; HILDA reads at rule-evaluation time): see **FR-11**.
  - **`doc_count`** (gates `OwnerClosed` guard condition; test-report-only counter): see **FR-7**.
  - **`review_required`** (gates FR-53 LLM review; vacuous-true for Confirmation items): see **FR-7** + **FR-53**.
  - **TG-coordinator identity + ODF runtime behavior**: see **FR-71**.
  - **`email_cc_list` outreach behavior + `tg_email_group_alias` runtime semantics**: see **FR-9**.
  - **`corp_id_list` messenger broadcast semantics**: see **FR-10** + **FR-71**.
  - **`plm_id`** is NOT set at tracker creation; assigned per `(device, milestone, owner_corp_id)` tuple at collection kickoff (per FR-5 plm_id grouping + `[D-035]`): see **FR-8** step 2.
  - **`item_path_id`** + **`tg_path_id`** (NSD path components per FR-13) — both denormalized per work-item in template.yaml per FR-40; SP UI engineer's web part pre-populates onto every Deliverables row at `setup_milestone` time; `tg_path_id` value repeats across all Deliverables rows sharing the same `tg_name` per `TSC-W005` equality discipline. See **FR-13** for NSD path layout and **FR-40** for the denormalization rule.
- **FR-3** `[Ph-2]` — **Deferred to Ph-2 per DEF-22**. TPM can add, remove, or **reassign ownership of** DeliveryItems after instantiation without re-creating the tracker (PM ≡ TPM throughout per PROJECT.md). "Reassign" = owner-reassignment via the **free-form text owner identity columns** per `[D-086]` — TPM updates the relevant subset of `owner_corp_usa_email` / `owner_corp_email` / `owner_corp_id` / `owner_name` columns (typical pattern: `owner_corp_usa_email` + `owner_corp_id` + `owner_name` together for an R&D-engineer transfer; `owner_corp_email` for legacy non-USA addresses). No AD validation. The `OwnerReassigned` rule fires only when EITHER `owner_corp_usa_email` OR `owner_corp_email` changes per FR-88; identifier-only (`owner_corp_id`) or display-only (`owner_name`) edits propagate to SP but do NOT trigger the rule cascade. HILDA's owner-email preference rule per FR-9 + FR-88 (+ `[D-080]` ratified rule) applies for outreach attribution: `owner_corp_usa_email` preferred; `owner_corp_email` fallback. Owner reassignment is NOT item-to-different-milestone migration — items belong permanently to the milestone where `setup_milestone` instantiated them. **Cross-milestone item movement path**: TPM removes the item from milestone A (Ph-2 FR-3 remove), then waits for milestone B's `setup_milestone` run to instantiate the item under B per template.yaml structure. Mid-milestone cross-milestone migration is not supported. **Identity-asymmetry note** vs PM identity: owner columns are free-form text per `[D-086]` (R&D engineers may be cross-org / partner identities not in HILDA's corp AD scope); PM identity per `Projects.TPM` is SP Person/Group (corp-AD-bound) per `[D-088]`. The asymmetry is intentional and load-bearing. **Ph-1 capability gap**: the milestone row set is static once `setup_milestone` has run — TPM cannot add/remove items mid-milestone, and cannot reassign owners mid-milestone. If an R&D owner transfers, the row stays with the original owner until Ph-2. **Ph-1 workarounds**: (a) FR-14 field overrides apply ONLY to TPM-editable fields per FR-56 column model (`delivery_state`, `tracking_modality`, `review_required`, `email_cc_list`, `comment`, `manual_triage_required` per FR-56 (a) lock) — NOT owner identity columns; owner change mid-milestone in Ph-1 is genuinely unsupported. (b) ops-mediated edits to template.yaml affect FUTURE `setup_milestone` runs only — existing Deliverables rows are not rewritten on SIGHUP. (c) for row-level rule changes in Ph-1, ops edits `customizations/rules/<customer_id>/per_item_overrides.yaml` per FR-30 SIGHUP reload. (d) outreach to the new owner happens out-of-band — PM/TPM sends ad-hoc email from their personal mailbox; HILDA's state machine, FR-10 reminder cadence, `last_owner_contacted`, and `reminder_count` are NOT updated; the original-owner DI continues to receive HILDA reminders until milestone close or Ph-2 ships. TPM workaround to suppress original-owner reminders: use FR-14 to set `delivery_state = Blocked` or `Delayed` on the affected DI (per FR-7 transient-states-out-of-cadence rule). **Ph-2 data flow**: TPM edits happen directly in SP (canonical store for DeliveryItem state); **SP UI engineer's web part creates / updates / removes SP rows on PM/TPM action — same actor as `setup_milestone` per FR-2 R&R lock; HILDA never creates rows in any path**. SP-side adds/removes/reassignments fire SP-alerts per `[D-047]` → HILDA's `sp_alert_parser` consumes the deltas → HILDA propagates to downstream effects (rule_engine triggers, workflow_engine task dispatch, storage's `DocumentItemAssociation` updates if a removed item had associated documents). HILDA does NOT write new DeliveryItem rows to its own Postgres per `[D-071]` — runtime queries always go through `sharepoint_integration` to SP. **Ph-2 SP UI engineer UX** for add/remove/reassign actions is specified in `docs/sp_ui_engineer/SP_UI_button_actions.md` (Ph-2 sections; deferred per this FR + DEF-22). **Ph-2 unblock criterion**: Ph-1 PLM + NSD workflow stable in production for at least 2 release cycles (operational confidence gating) + team capacity for SP UI form complexity + cascade testing. No fixed timeline; revisit at quarterly Ph-2 planning per PROJECT.md roadmap.

  **Per `[D-083]` 2-list architecture**: Ph-2 mid-milestone add / remove / reassign operations touch only the `Deliverables_<customer_id>` SP list — the Milestones SP list row for the milestone already exists from the initial `setup_milestone` run and is NOT modified by these per-item operations. **Add item**: SP UI engineer's web part writes a new Deliverables row (denormalized customer / device / milestone identity + work-item columns + TG-denormalized fields). **TG-denormalized field sourcing**: if the new item belongs to an existing TG within `(device, milestone)`, TG-denormalized fields (`tg_path_id`, `tg_name`, `tg_email_group_alias`, `tg_owner_*`, `ingress_nsd`, `folder_routing_enabled`, etc. per FR-2 line 45) are sourced from sibling Deliverables rows in the same TG; if the item belongs to a NEW TG not yet present in the milestone, TG-denormalized fields are sourced from template.yaml's TG definitions per FR-40 (assuming the new TG was added to template.yaml first via ops edit); if template.yaml does not yet define the TG, the SP UI form prompts TPM for TG-level values — Ph-2 detailed UX deferred to architecture phase. **Ph-2 add is intentionally OFF template.yaml's structural scope** — the new item is PM-supplied content (`item_no`, `item_name`, `item_type`, `doc_count`, `tracking_modality`, owner identity columns). Template.yaml is the source for STRUCTURAL constraints only (allowed `item_type` choices per FR-7 4-value enum, valid `tracking_modality` enums per FR-7, valid form-factor flag set per `[D-084]`, etc.); the SP UI engineer's add form validates against these constraints at write time. **Remove item**: hard-delete of the Deliverables row via SP REST DELETE. Audit trail: SP UI engineer's web part writes a `CommunicationLog` entry BEFORE issuing DELETE (`action_type = item_removed_by_pm` + full pre-delete row snapshot + PM/TPM attribution); HILDA also writes `CommunicationLog` on receiving the SP-alert (per the cascade below for items with docs; for items without docs, `action_type = item_removed_no_docs` + pre-delete snapshot from the SP-alert payload). Two complementary audit entries — SP UI side captures who/what; HILDA side captures cascade effects. **Reassign owner**: SP UI engineer's web part presents the 4 owner columns; TPM updates the subset that changes; HILDA's `sp_alert_parser` applies FR-88 fire rules at consumption (per the OwnerReassigned semantics above). None of these operations touch the Milestones row, the Projects list, or template.yaml. **SP-alert payload format**: per `[D-047]` + FR-84 Source A — `Alert_Deliverables_<customer_id> - <ItemTitle>` subject + action-verb sub-header + key:value body containing the row's current state (post-add or post-update; for delete, the row's pre-delete state). HILDA's `sp_alert_parser` routes per FR-84 Source A routing using the 5-field key `(customer_id, Model, ProjectID, MinorMilestone, ItemNumber)` to identify the row + action.

  **Item removal with associated documents** (per FR-79 + FR-78 + FR-83 cascade): when a Deliverables row is removed in Ph-2 AND that row has one or more `DocumentItemAssociation` rows pointing to it, HILDA's `sp_alert_parser` triggers a **reassignment cascade**: (i) for each associated document, HILDA reassigns the `DocumentItemAssociation` row from the removed `delivery_item_id` to the milestone's default work-item per FR-78 (unconditional). (ii) the document's NSD path is moved to the FR-86 `_unrouted/<inferred_tg_name>/` path — **conditioned on the file having NO remaining `DocumentItemAssociation` rows pointing to non-removed DIs**; if the same `file_hash` is still associated with other DIs in the milestone, the file stays at its current NSD path (still routed). `inferred_tg_name` is sourced from the removed DI's `tg_name` value captured in the SP-alert payload per `[D-047]` BEFORE the row vanishes from SP. (iii) TPM resolves via FR-83 reassignment + FR-87 step (A) to route each doc to a remaining work-item OR mark the default work-item `Closed` if the docs are obsolete. TPM-side surface: orphaned docs appear in the default work-item's per-row document list in SP UI (per FR-78 + FR-87 step (A)); the milestone cannot reach `Completed` until the default WI is `Closed` per FR-6 rung (1) + FR-78 — this naturally surfaces the work without a separate alert. **Cascade-delete option NOT used** — the `file_hash` itself + `DocumentIndexRow` survive (other associations may exist for the same file); only the `(file_hash, removed_delivery_item_id)` association tuple migrates. **Block-removal option NOT used** — TPM judgment on doc disposition happens post-removal via FR-83 / FR-87. Per `CommunicationLog`: `action_type = item_removed_with_docs_cascade` per orphaned doc, with old + new `delivery_item_id` recorded; for items removed without docs, `action_type = item_removed_no_docs` (per remove-item paragraph above) — symmetrical audit regardless of document scope.

  **Owner reassignment + `delivery_state` interaction** (per `[D-086]` + FR-62 / FR-83 cascade): owner reassignment via free-form text owner column edit does NOT revert `delivery_state` — the workflow position is independent of owner identity. Distinct from FR-62 upload-induced revert (where new doc content invalidates prior PM approval); owner change without new doc means PM's prior review stands. The new owner inherits the workflow position. HILDA fires `OwnerReassigned` rule per FR-28 (sends `NotifyNewOwner` per FR-29 + re-fires `StartItemCollection` if collection-phase state). **State-machine reset semantics for new owner**: `reminder_count` RESET to 0 (new owner starts fresh cadence per FR-10); `last_owner_contacted` set to `<now>` if `NotifyNewOwner` dispatched (FR-29 + FR-9 outreach to new owner counts as initial contact); `last_owner_response_at` cleared (new owner has not yet responded). The old owner's per-DI history is preserved in `CommunicationLog` (action_type history with prior `owner_corp_usa_email` captured per entry). **Completion-side timestamps are immutable to owner-change events**: `actual_completion_date` preserved if already set (owner who completed remains the historical record); `pm_approval_at` + `pm_approval_pm_id` preserved (PM approval not re-required). `OwnerReassigned` cascade preserves these regardless of state-machine re-firing in collection-phase; state-machine fields (`delivery_state`, `last_owner_contacted`, `reminder_count`) reset per the new owner's fresh cadence; completion-side timestamps are immutable to owner-change events. **Owner-change event semantics** (per FR-88): a write to EITHER `owner_corp_usa_email` OR `owner_corp_email` fires `OwnerReassigned`; `owner_corp_id` change alone does NOT fire (free-form identifier); `owner_name` change alone does NOT fire (display field only). **Multi-column event dedup**: multiple owner-identity column changes in a single SP edit fire ONE `OwnerReassigned` event (HILDA's `sp_alert_parser` deduplicates within the alert payload — single row update = single rule fire, regardless of how many columns were affected). `CommunicationLog` records the diff: old values → new values for each changed column.

  **PM identity reassignment is OUT OF SCOPE for FR-3** — `assigned_pm_id` per `[D-083]` lives in the Projects SP list (`project_id → TPM`), not on Deliverables rows. PM reassignment is performed by editing the Projects SP list row's `TPM` Person/Group column (per `[D-088]` 3-tuple resolution); HILDA's cache rebuild on SIGHUP re-resolves the 3-tuple `(assigned_pm_id, pm_display_name, pm_email)` for the affected `project_id` rows. All Deliverables rows referencing that `project_id` automatically reflect the new PM identity at next FR-9 outreach / FR-19 dispatch / FR-25 (b) self-outreach event — no per-row write is needed. **PM-vs-owner concurrent reassignment race**: HILDA's `sp_alert_parser` processes per-alert events serially per `[D-047]` ordering; the cascade for each event uses the current Projects-list cache snapshot at processing time. If PM reassignment SIGHUP happens between two owner-reassignment alerts, the second alert's cascade uses the new PM identity. No special concurrency handling needed beyond SIGHUP atomicity. FR-3 scope is OWNER (R&D engineer) reassignment via free-form text owner column edits on Deliverables rows.
- ~~**FR-4**~~ — *(deferred 2026-05-12 → DEF-15: Excel import schema validation — implementation phase TBD)*
- **FR-5** `[Ph-1]` — Hierarchical data is conceptually 4-tier (Customer / Device / Milestone / DeliveryItem) and **physically stored as one flat SP list per customer** named `Deliverables_<customer_id>` (e.g., `Tasks_MMK`). **`customer_id` value space**: stable, uppercase short carrier code (e.g., `MMK`, `VZW`, `TMO`) defined in template.yaml as the top-level YAML map key per FR-40; immutable post-onboarding. **Terminology lock**: HILDA-canonical name is `milestone_id` (snake_case, used in template.yaml + Pydantic schema + this document); SP column name is `minorMilestone` (camelCase as it appears in `Deliverables_<customer_id>`); SP-alert routing-key field is `MinorMilestone` (PascalCase as it appears in alert subject/body — see FR-84). Each row carries `customer_id` + `model` (= `device_id`) + `project_id` (SP-auto-generated when a new device is added to a carrier; example values like `2479`) + `minorMilestone` (= `milestone_id`; e.g., `LE-2`) + `item_no` + `item_name` + all TG / owner / state columns. **Uniqueness invariant**: `(model, minorMilestone, item_no)` is unique within a given `Deliverables_<customer_id>` list (one row per work-item). **Device identity within a list**: `model` is unique per device — two devices for the same customer cannot share `model`. The `model → project_id` functional dependency holds **per-list only**; the same `model` value may carry different `project_id`s across customers (across different lists), since each carrier auto-generates its own `project_id` independently. Both columns travel on every row for routing-key cross-validation per FR-84. **`item_no` definition**: positive integers (≥ 1) sourced from `item_no` in template.yaml; **immutable for the life of the row** per FR-84 routing-key immutability requirement (architecture-phase SP UI engineer confirmation pending per STATUS.md Flag — if `item_no` is re-numberable on row removal, an immutable `item_guid` column must be added and the routing key extended). `item_no` may repeat across milestones in the same list (e.g., `item_no = 7` valid in both `LE-2` and `LE-3` rows of the same device). The FR-78 default work-item carries a reserved `item_no` value (TBD at architecture phase; recommend `item_no = 0` reserved for default work-item with `item_no ≥ 1` for template-defined work-items). `item_name` is not required unique (work-items can share names across milestones). **TG identity within a `(model, minorMilestone)`**: keyed by `tg_path_id` — rows sharing the same `(model, minorMilestone, tg_path_id)` belong to the same TG; equality discipline enforced per FR-40 `TSC-W005` validator. `tg_path_id` (and `item_path_id`) are filesystem-safe slug strings (alphanumeric + `-` + `_`) used as NSD path segments per FR-13. Higher-tier "uniqueness" is implicit via column-value duplication: a (customer, device, milestone) tuple exists across N rows (one per work-item); a (customer, device) tuple exists across M rows; a (customer) tuple = the entire list. **HILDA reads via REST `$filter` on column values** (e.g., `?$filter=minorMilestone eq 'LE-2' and item_no eq 7`). **SharePoint's intrinsic `ID` column** (auto-Counter PK, per-list unique — NOT globally unique across `Deliverables_<customer_id>` lists) is opaque to HILDA's SP-coupling layer — it is NOT used as a foreign-key target or in cross-list joins. Only the `dashboard` module accepts `(customer_id, ID)` URL tokens from SP web part link-outs (View Documents / Upload Document per `[D-074]`) and resolves the target row via `Deliverables_<customer_id>` `?$filter=ID eq <item_id>` lookup (or equivalent `GetItemById(<item_id>)` REST call); `customer_id` co-passed in the URL path selects the list, `ID` selects the row within it. **`plm_id` semantic**: all rows in a list sharing the same `(model, minorMilestone, owner)` share the same `plm_id` — the PLM issue is one per `(customer, device, milestone, owner)` per `[D-035]` (`customer_id` is constant within a list, so the per-list key is the 3-tuple). **Owner identity for `plm_id` grouping** = `owner_corp_id` (the corp identifier — NOT the email fields; supersedes any earlier email-based framings). See FR-88 owner identity model for the full 3-field model and its preference rule for *outreach* (a separate concern from PLM grouping; outreach uses `owner_corp_usa_email` if set else `owner_corp_email`; PLM grouping uses `owner_corp_id` exclusively). The shared `plm_id` value is duplicated across all rows in the group. No Deliverable intermediate level (anchors `[D-028]`). **Identity-integrity check**: tuples present in `Deliverables_<customer_id>` but absent from template.yaml are flagged `STATUS-W001` at HILDA bootstrap per FR-2. (Ph-2 will normalize the per-customer flat list into N SP lists with FK chains per `[D-077]`.)
- **FR-6** `[Ph-1]` — Milestone status and `milestone_completion_pct` are computed from the states of their child DeliveryItems. **`Milestone.status` is a 4-value SP Choice column** (canonical schema in `docs/sp_ui_engineer/SP_lists_authoritative.xlsx`): `Not Started`, `In Progress`, `Completed`, `Delayed`. **`milestone_completion_pct` is a PERCENTAGE column** on the Milestones SP list (added 2026-06-19 per FR-6 I7 cascade — sheet row 82). Both columns live on the single Milestones SP list row per `[D-083]` and are HILDA-written via `[D-064]` REST. **SP-internal name mapping** (canonical → SP-internal per `customizations/sharepoint_config/customers/<customer_id>.yaml` per `[D-065]`): `customer_id` → `carrier` (Milestones list); `device_id` → `project_model` (Milestones + Deliverables lists); `milestone_id` → `Title` (Milestones list) and `milestone_name` (Deliverables list); the same template.yaml `milestone_id` value (e.g., `LE-2`) lands in both SP columns. **Precedence ladder for computation** (top-down short-circuit; first applicable wins): **(1) Completed** — all `milestone_gating = true` items are `Closed` (equivalent to `MilestoneAllClosed` per FR-28). **(2) Delayed** — `Milestone.target_date < today` (read from Milestones SP list per `[D-085]`) AND any DI's `delivery_state ∉ {ReadyForSubmission, SubmittedToCustomer, Closed}` (a single overdue gating-or-non-gating item triggers `Delayed`; overrides `In Progress`). The implicit overdue set technically includes `OwnerClosed` per FR-7 — but `OwnerClosed` is a transient state with zero observable duration; HILDA's compute does not special-case it. **(3) In Progress** — at least one DI past `Not Started` (DI `delivery_state ≠ Not Started`; `Open` qualifies) AND no higher rung applies. Once FR-8 step 1 transitions all DIs `Not Started → Open`, this rung qualifies — aligns with FR-8 step 6 outreach-stalled edge case. **(4) Not Started** — initial state; no DI past `Not Started` (all DIs in `Not Started`). After Start Collection has fired (FR-8 step 1), this rung is unreachable in practice. **Initial value**: `Not Started` is written by SP UI engineer's `setup_milestone` web part on the **Milestones SP list row** at row creation per FR-2 R&R lock + `[D-083]` (Milestones row is sole storage for `Milestone.status`); **hardcoded by `setup_milestone`** — the initial `Not Started` value is not template.yaml-sourced (template.yaml is the structural manifest; milestone status is a HILDA-state-machine value, not a template-author field). HILDA's first `Milestone.status` write fires at Start Collection per FR-8 step (6) when all DIs have transitioned `Not Started → Open` — at that point HILDA recomputes via the precedence ladder; result is `In Progress` per redefined rung (3) (any DI past `Not Started` qualifies); `[D-064]` REST writes the result back to the Milestones row. **HILDA-side `Not Started` discipline**: HILDA's compute may yield `Not Started` only in the impossible-post-Start-Collection case (all DIs in `Not Started`); combined with no-op suppression below, the `Not Started` value is therefore only ever the SP-UI-set initial value — once HILDA's `[D-064]` writes begin, the recomputed value is a different rung. **`milestone_completion_pct` formula**: `(count of milestone_gating = true DIs in Closed state) / (total milestone_gating = true DIs in milestone) × 100`, rounded to nearest integer. Computed on the same cadence as `Milestone.status`. **Default work-item interaction (FR-78)**: `setup_milestone` writes `milestone_gating = true` on the default work-item's Deliverables row per FR-78 + FR-2 R&R lock; HILDA's compute reads the SP column value uniformly — no implicit override. `Completed` requires the default work-item to be `Closed` via TPM's Mark Closed action after all unrouted docs are reassigned per FR-83; until then, the milestone cannot reach `Completed` even if all template-defined `milestone_gating = true` items are `Closed`. The default work-item also contributes to the `milestone_completion_pct` denominator. **Default work-item contribution to `Delayed` precedence**: the default work-item lifecycle (per FR-78) is `Open → Closed` via TPM Mark Closed (never traverses `ReadyForSubmission` / `SubmittedToCustomer`). If `Milestone.target_date < today` AND the default work-item has not yet been Marked Closed (i.e., still in `Open`), precedence ladder rung (2) Delayed triggers (default WI's `delivery_state = Open` is in the 'overdue' set). This is intentional — the milestone remains `Delayed` until TPM has resolved all unrouted docs via FR-83 + Marked Closed via FR-78. **Compute scope**: pre-Start-Collection milestones (`milestone_collection_started_at IS NULL`) are NOT in HILDA's compute scope — SP-side value remains `Not Started` as written by `setup_milestone`; HILDA neither reads nor writes their `status` until Start Collection fires per FR-8 step 6. **Compute cadence**: HILDA computes and writes `Milestone.status` + `milestone_completion_pct` to the single Milestones SP list row via `[D-064]` REST writeback (per `[D-083]` — Milestones row is sole storage for these fields) on every DI state change or daily tick (whichever fires first). **DI state-change trigger**: HILDA learns of DI state changes via SP-alert per `[D-047]` + FR-84 Source A routing on Deliverables row `delivery_state` column change; recompute fires after HILDA processes the alert in `sp_alert_parser`. **Daily tick role**: the daily tick is load-bearing for `Delayed` detection — `Milestone.target_date < today` flips at midnight without any DI state change, so the daily tick is the mechanism that catches deadline-driven status transitions. **Tick time + timezone are defined in a separate `daily_status_tick` AutomationRule** (default: 00:05 in the timezone given by `hilda_config.yaml` field `daily_status_tick.timezone`; default `Asia/Seoul` corp HQ; per-customer override Ph-2 deferred) — distinct from the `polling_schedule` deadline-tiered cadence per FR-23 cross-FR cadence consistency lock (which controls polling-channel intervals for FR-23 / FR-25 / FR-26 / FR-55, not status recompute timing). The `daily_status_tick` rule fires once per local day; on fire, HILDA recomputes `Milestone.status` + `milestone_completion_pct` for all active milestones (those with `milestone_collection_started_at IS NOT NULL AND status ≠ 'Completed'` on the Milestones SP list per FR-2 bootstrap). **No-op suppression**: HILDA reads the current Milestones row value before writing and **only writes when the recomputed value differs** — eliminates redundant writes (and the corresponding SP-alert bursts) for state changes that don't shift the milestone-level outcome. **Writeback failure handling**: if the `[D-064]` REST write to the Milestones row fails, HILDA logs `HildaOpsAlert` per FR-75 with a milestone-status-write-failed warning (error code candidate `STATUS-W009` — final ID ratified at architecture phase per error-code catalog policy) carrying `(milestone_id, computed_status, attempted_write_at)`; retry on next compute trigger (DI state change or daily tick); current Milestones row value is unchanged until a successful write. **Cascade ordering**: during multi-step cascade operations (FR-8 Start Collection batch DI activation, FR-64 Close All Items two-part cascade — Part 1 gating-cascade + Part 2 non-gating auto-advance), HILDA defers `Milestone.status` + `milestone_completion_pct` recompute until the cascade completes — single recompute + single Milestones-row write at cascade end, not per-DI mid-cascade. **Write pattern** per `[D-083]`: both fields live on the single Milestones SP list row — HILDA writes via `[D-064]` REST. **SP UI engineer rendering pattern** (per `[D-083]` cross-list join): when SP UI engineer's web part renders the milestone view, it reads the Milestones SP list row for milestone-level fields (`status`, `target_date`, button timestamps, `download_package_*`, `milestone_completion_pct`) by `?$filter=carrier eq '<customer_id>' and Title eq '<milestone_id>' and project_model eq '<device_id>'` (3-field tuple uniquely identifies a Milestones row per user-confirmed uniqueness; equivalently `(carrier, Title, project_id)` per user); separately reads `Deliverables_<customer_id>` rows by `?$filter=milestone_name eq '<milestone_id>' and Model eq '<device_id>'` (within a per-customer Deliverables list, this tuple uniquely scopes the milestone's DIs). SP-internal column names per the mapping above (`carrier` ← `customer_id`, `Model` ← `project_model`, `Title` ← `milestone_id` on Milestones list, `milestone_name` ← `milestone_id` on Deliverables list). Banner styling sources from Milestones row; per-row list display sources from Deliverables rows. Both reads happen on focus-aware refresh per `[D-074]`. No SP-side JOIN (SP lists don't support cross-list joins); 2 queries instead. **Start Collection button visibility is driven by `milestone_collection_started_at IS NULL`** on the Milestones row. **Naming-collision note**: Milestone `Delayed` (HILDA-computed from `Milestone.target_date`) is distinct from DI `delivery_state = Delayed` (FR-7 — owner-reported via status modality). A DI marked `Delayed` by owner does NOT make the milestone `Delayed` unless `Milestone.target_date < today`.
- **FR-7** `[Ph-1]` — Item types, tracking modalities, customer delivery modalities, `delivery_state` values, and `doc_count` are extensible via configuration without code change; `doc_count` is a per-DeliveryItem integer from the template (default 1) specifying how many `test_report` documents must be received before the item can advance to `OwnerClosed` — gates two transitions: (a) immediate advance to `DocumentReceived` (intermediate state) once the count is reached via document arrival; (b) guard condition (1) of the `OwnerClosed` transition triggered by owner "done" confirmation; applies only to artifact item types; `Confirmation` items have `doc_count = 0`; `item_completion_pct` is a per-DeliveryItem computed field representing the document-review completion percentage across all received documents (see FR-70); `tracking_modality` is a **multi-value field** (list) per DeliveryItem supporting the following values — `Email` `[Ph-1]` (status + documents via email reply), `CorporateMessenger` `[Ph-2]` (status only; no attachments; not document-capable), `CorporatePLM` `[Ph-1]` (documents only; not status-capable; owner uploads to PLM directly; HILDA polls PLM, downloads, and writes the document file to `<doc_type_slug>/<doc_id_slug>/rev1/`; document arrival advances item to `DocumentReceived` intermediate state — does **not** set `OwnerClosed`), `NetworkSharedDrive` `[Ph-1]` (documents only; not status-capable; owner drops in the milestone's NSD ingress folder at `\\NSD_X\<carrier>\<device>\<milestone>\<Folder-N>\` per FR-13 flat per-TG folder structure; HILDA polls; document arrival advances item to `DocumentReceived` intermediate state — does **not** set `OwnerClosed`), `CustomerJIRA` `[Ph-1]` (status only; not document-capable; HILDA polls customer JIRA API for waiver/issue closure status; no outbound; no attachments), `SPUI` `[Ph-2]` (documents only; not status-capable; TPM uploads via HILDA-rendered form per FR-62 / `[D-074]`; document arrival advances item to `DocumentReceived` intermediate state — does **not** set `OwnerClosed`; added 2026-06-14 per SP UI engineer review; `docs/sp_ui_engineer/SP_lists_authoritative.xlsx` — `tracking_modality` row of tab `DI field visibility`); **Valid `tracking_modality` combinations**: at least one status-capable modality (`[Ph-1]` Email or CustomerJIRA; `[Ph-2]` CorporateMessenger) is required for **ALL items**. At least one document-capable modality (Email, CorporatePLM, NetworkSharedDrive, or `[Ph-2]` SPUI) is required **ONLY for items with artifact deliverables** (`item_type ≠ Confirmation` AND `doc_count > 0`); `CorporateMessenger` escalation (FR-10) is automatic regardless of modality and is not a modality value itself; `OwnerClosed` transition is triggered when the owner explicitly confirms "done" via a status-capable modality (`Email` `[Ph-1]` or `CorporateMessenger` `[Ph-2]`) — the "done" confirmation is the **trigger** that evaluates the following two guard conditions, not a third condition itself: (1) `doc_count` reached (test-report count equals `doc_count`); (2) LLM review condition — vacuously satisfied when `review_required = false` for this item (no review triggered, no `AIReviewResult` awaited, item advances to `OwnerClosed` once condition (1) is met); when `review_required = true`: all received documents and all their revisions have been reviewed — for `test_report` documents: rule-based parser result (FR-16) AND non-null `llm_review_findings` (FR-53) present for every `rev_number` row in the document index; for `tech_report` and `waiver` documents: non-null `llm_review_findings` (FR-53) for every revision; if owner confirms "done" before condition (1) is met, HILDA sends an **email notification** to the owner (per FR-9 + FR-65 + FR-50 lock cascade 2026-06-17 — supersedes prior "corp messenger" framing; messenger is reserved for FR-11 escalation only per FR-50 outbound-only lock; this is an event-driven one-shot informational reply, NOT an outreach cadence event — does NOT update `last_owner_contacted` and does NOT reset FR-10 cadence) informing the owner that documents are still expected — `delivery_state` stays at its current value (likely `DocumentReceived` if partial docs received), no owner response is processed; action handled via `NotifyOwnerDocCountPending` rule action per FR-29; if owner confirms "done" before condition (2) is met and `review_required = true`, state advance is deferred until all reviews complete; document receipt alone via `Email` attachment, `CorporatePLM`, or `NetworkSharedDrive` advances to `DocumentReceived` only — explicit owner "done" confirmation is always required to trigger guard evaluation; `[Ph-2]` owner may self-close directly in the SharePoint UI (FR-56); **`OwnerClosed` is a transient state** — items spend zero observable time there. On entry: (a) `actual_completion_date` is auto-set to today (immutable thereafter per FR-15); (b) HILDA immediately advances the item out of `OwnerClosed` via one of two forks. **Ph-1 (single-revision)** — only `rev1` exists for each document → `is_final = true` auto-set on `rev1` → item advances directly to `UnderPMReview`. **Ph-2 (multi-revision)** — if multiple revisions exist for any `doc_id_slug` → `TriggerVersionSelection` fires (FR-66) → owner selects the final revision via corp messenger → `is_final = true` set on selected revision → item advances to `UnderPMReview` after all selections are confirmed; `delivery_state` enumeration — **11 total values, with a 10-vs-11 distinction clarified 2026-06-12**: the canonical Python `DeliveryState` enum in `core/src/template_schema/` has **10 values** starting at `Open` (HILDA's active state machine begins here when Collection Kickoff per FR-8 fires). The SP DeliveryItems list `delivery_state` Choice column has **11 values** — the 10 enum values PLUS `Not Started` as the SP-only pre-tracking state. **`Not Started` is the value SP UI engineer's `setup_milestone` writes at DI row creation** (via SP column-level default OR explicit template.yaml value per FR-40; HILDA does NOT write this value per FR-2 R&R lock — HILDA's first state-change write is `Not Started → Open` at Start Collection per FR-8 step (1)) (when DeliveryItem rows are created from template YAML and pushed to SP); HILDA's state machine is passive while items are in `Not Started` (no PLM issues, no outreach, no polling). When TPM clicks the **Start Collection** button in SP (per FR-56 / FR-8), HILDA receives the SP-alert and writes back `delivery_state = "Open"` — the HILDA state machine activates from that point forward. So: at tracker creation = `Not Started`; at Collection Kickoff = `Open`. The values: `Not Started` (SP-only; set by SP UI engineer's `setup_milestone` at row creation per FR-2 R&R lock; HILDA passive — not a HILDA write), `Open` (HILDA enum value 1; set by HILDA on receipt of Start Collection per FR-8), `OutreachSent` (initial outreach dispatched per FR-9), `DocumentReceived` (document arrived via any ingest channel per FR-28), `UnderPMReview` (active TPM review gate: `OwnerClosed` transient fork lands here once `is_final` is resolved per the fork logic above; TPM reviews document findings in the SP UI per FR-56; TPM may override `is_final` to select a different revision `[Ph-2]`; explicit TPM approval via the per-item **Approve** action (`PMApproval` trigger, FR-28) advances the item to `ReadyForSubmission`), `OwnerClosed` (owner confirmed done via status-capable modality; `actual_completion_date` auto-set to today's date on this transition — not overwritten by subsequent state changes), `Delayed` (owner-reported delay — set via status modality, any pre-approval state; **Ph-1**: Email modality only via FR-12 structured reply block; `[Ph-2]` CorporateMessenger reply per FR-54), `Blocked` (owner-reported blocker — set via status modality, any pre-approval state; **Ph-1**: Email modality only via FR-12 structured reply block; `[Ph-2]` CorporateMessenger reply per FR-54), `ReadyForSubmission` (PM approved per FR-28 `PMApproval` trigger), `SubmittedToCustomer` `[Ph-1]` (submission package dispatched to customer per FR-18), `Closed` (reached by one of four paths: (i) PM/TPM manual override per FR-14 once carrier acceptance is confirmed; (ii) bulk Close All Items via FR-64; (iii) **TPM-Mark-Closed manual path for items with `no_customer_upload = true`** per FR-80 — TPM clicks "Mark Closed" in SP UI after standard `OwnerClosed` guards are met; HILDA enforces guards at click time, no bypass; DEF-20 carve-out applies to this specific manual path only; (iv) **TPM-Mark-Closed manual path for the per-milestone default work-item** per FR-78 — TPM clicks "Mark Closed" after all unrouted docs have been reassigned to real work-items via FR-83; HILDA enforces the no-remaining-association guard at click time; DEF-20 carve-out also applies here; other automated transitions remain deferred per DEF-20); `Delayed` and `Blocked` are transient — **exit paths**: (a) owner reply via Email status modality with a state-change keyword (per FR-12 structured reply block) — HILDA writes the new value to `delivery_state`; (b) PM/TPM via FR-14 manual override directly picking the target state. The "prior active state" is stored on the row in the **`prior_delivery_state`** column (set when `Delayed`/`Blocked` is entered; cleared on exit) — captured in `docs/sp_ui_engineer/SP_lists_authoritative.xlsx` — `prior_delivery_state` row of tab `DI field visibility` (same 11-value Choice enum as `delivery_state`; HILDA-written, SP-UI-engineer does not read or write); **Items with `doc_count = 0` follow the Confirmation skip path** regardless of item_type — `Confirmation` items (`item_type = Confirmation`, which always have `doc_count = 0`) AND non-Confirmation items with `doc_count = 0` (template-author opt-in for waiver-only / tech-report-only cases per the Template Author Guidance below) both skip `DocumentReceived` and `OwnerClosed` entirely: owner "done" confirmation from `OutreachSent` advances directly to `UnderPMReview` (no document gate, no `OwnerClosed` transient, no `is_final` fork; `actual_completion_date` auto-set to today on this transition per FR-15-extended semantics). For non-Confirmation `doc_count = 0` items, documents may still arrive (and are stored + reviewed per `review_required`) but do NOT gate state advancement — owner-done confirmation alone advances the item. For Confirmation items, no document path exists (no document received, no FR-53 review); the PM Review section in SP UI for a Confirmation item shows no document list — surfaces only the owner's "done" confirmation timestamp + a TPM **Approve** action which advances to `ReadyForSubmission` via `PMApproval` trigger; **`CustomerJIRA`-only items** (`tracking_modality = [CustomerJIRA]` alone, no other modality) **must have `item_type = Confirmation`** — status-only modality is meaningless for an item expecting documents. State machine (CORRECTED 2026-06-17 per FR-25 (b) lock cascade — supersedes prior "Open → UnderPMReview directly" wording which incorrectly implied no email outreach): `Open → OutreachSent` (FR-8 initial outreach email fires for CustomerJIRA-only items per FR-25 (b) lock — recipient is the role-collapsed owner-PM identity per FR-25 (b) role-collapse template constraint; outbound reminder body is tailored for JIRA modality: "please review open JIRA tickets at `<customer_jira_url>` and reply to confirm milestone completion"). `OutreachSent → UnderPMReview` (close-intent reply email per FR-12 + FR-24 tag routing — same trigger as Email-modality Confirmation items, skipping `DocumentReceived` + `OwnerClosed` per FR-7 I8 Confirmation skip rule). Closing all customer JIRA tickets does NOT auto-advance the item — JIRA polling is informational per FR-25 (b) close-intent path exclusivity lock (close-intent comes EXCLUSIVELY via email reply, not via JIRA-close detection). In production, CustomerJIRA always appears as CustomerJIRA-only (no SPUI + CustomerJIRA combination, no CustomerJIRA mixed with other modalities — per FR-9 2026-06-16 lock); **`item_type` enumeration is 4-valued** (revised per `[D-053]` impl note 2026-06-08, supersedes the prior multi-valued enum): `Confirmation` (no documents — Yes/No reply only), `TEST_TECH_WAIVER_REPORT` (receives any of `{test_report, tech_report, waiver}`; `review_required = true`), `COMPLIANCE_CERTIFICATION_RELEASE_NOTES` (receives `compliance_certification_release_notes` documents; `review_required = false`), `Default` (auto-instantiated default work-item per milestone per FR-78; receives any document whose target work-item is not resolved). **`doc_type` enumeration is 5-valued**: `test_report`, `tech_report`, `waiver`, `compliance_certification_release_notes`, `unresolved`. **`doc_type` is NOT derived from `item.item_type`** (supersedes the prior 1:1 derivation framing per `[D-053]` impl note 2026-05-28b — withdrawn) — it is classified per inbound document via the FR-85 classification pipeline (filename regex + LLM for `{test_report, tech_report, waiver}`; `compliance_certification_release_notes` detected by filename regex only; `unresolved` on classification fail). **Alignment invariant** (anchors `[D-053]` impl note 2026-06-08): `TEST_TECH_WAIVER_REPORT` items hold doc_type ∈ `{test_report, tech_report, waiver}`; `COMPLIANCE_CERTIFICATION_RELEASE_NOTES` items hold doc_type = `compliance_certification_release_notes`; `Default` items hold any of the 5; mismatched (item_type, doc_type) pairs at ingest land on the `staged-not-classified` NSD path per FR-86 for TPM SP UI resolution per FR-87. **`review_required` defaults by item_type**: Confirmation = `false` (non-editable per FR-58); TEST_TECH_WAIVER_REPORT = `true`; COMPLIANCE_CERTIFICATION_RELEASE_NOTES = `false` (no LLM review per downstream-triggering rule); Default = `false` (default work-item is for unrouted docs; review pending until reassignment per FR-87). All TPM-editable per FR-56 column model except Confirmation. **`doc_type = unresolved` is transient** — a doc with `unresolved` at ingest lands on `staged-not-classified` NSD path per FR-86 regardless of item_type (since `unresolved` is never an alignment-permitted value for non-Default items, and even for Default items it indicates classification incomplete and warrants TPM review). TPM resolves to one of {test_report, tech_report, waiver, compliance_certification_release_notes} via FR-87 step (B); once resolved, the doc moves to `classified` (if revision is determined) or `staged-not-revision` (if multi-rev decision pending) per FR-86 storage matrix. `unresolved` is therefore never a steady-state value on a fully-resolved row. `doc_type` is used as folder organiser `<doc_type_slug>/` on shared drive (when set) and as document index key. Downstream triggering: `test_report` → FR-16 parser always + FR-53 LLM review when `review_required = true`; `tech_report` / `waiver` → FR-53 LLM review when `review_required = true`; `compliance_certification_release_notes` / `unresolved` → no parser, no review (FR-16, FR-46, FR-53 do NOT fire). **Post-resolution review (FR-87)**: if a doc was `unresolved` at ingest and TPM resolves to a reviewable doc_type ({test_report, tech_report, waiver}) via FR-87 step (B), the appropriate review pipeline fires after the doc reaches `classified` per FR-87's async-tail review trigger (`review_required=true` AND `classified` post-resolution). No post-resolution review fires for resolution to `compliance_certification_release_notes` (downstream-triggering rule unchanged). **`doc_count` counts `test_report` documents only** (intentional; if owner submits only waiver or tech_report, state remains in `DocumentReceived` and does not advance to `OwnerClosed` until a test_report arrives. **Template author guidance**: if the deliverable is satisfied without a test_report (waiver-only or tech_report-only cases), set `doc_count = 0` on the work-item — owner-done confirmation then advances state via the vacuous-guard path; the received document(s) are stored and reviewable but not required by the gating mechanism. Splitting into separate work-items does not help — all actionable items share the `TEST_TECH_WAIVER_REPORT` item_type and the same test_report-only doc_count semantic).

### Collection kickoff & ongoing tracking

- **FR-8** `[Ph-1]` — **Collection kickoff flow**. TPM clicks **Start Collection** in the SharePoint milestone view (FR-56 (e)) → SP UI engineer's web part writes `milestone_collection_started_at = <now>` to the **single Milestones SP list row** for the milestone (single-row write per `[D-083]`) → one SP-alert fires per `[D-047]` → HILDA's `email_service.sp_alert_parser` routes per FR-84 Source B routing (Milestones list alert; routing key = `(carrier, project_model, project_id, milestone_id)` resolved to the Milestones row → HILDA scopes the `start_collection` task by `(customer_id, project_id, project_model, milestone_id)`) → dispatches the `start_collection` Celery task for the milestone. **SP-alert → Celery dispatch reliability** per `[D-047]`: `sp_alert_parser` ACKs the IMAP message only after successful Celery enqueue; on enqueue failure the alert remains in the inbox for IMAP-IDLE re-delivery; TPM can also re-click Start Collection (re-trigger is idempotent — see below). **HILDA actions on `start_collection` task (executed in order)**:
  - **(1) Transition each `Not Started` DeliveryItem in the milestone to `Open`** via `[D-064]` SP REST writeback to existing DI rows per FR-7 state machine. HILDA enumerates via `$filter` on `(project_id, project_model, milestone_id)` against `Deliverables_<customer_id>` (`customer_id` known from SP-alert routing per FR-84 Source B). **Scope includes `no_customer_upload = true` items** — they transition `Not Started → Open` like everyone else; FR-9 outreach in step 4 skips them (modality-based outreach filter), and TPM later drives them `Open → Closed` via FR-80 Mark Closed; entry-point semantics for `no_customer_upload` rows is FR-7's standard `Open`. **The default work-item (FR-78)** also transitions `Not Started → Open` here and follows the same TPM-Mark-Closed path. HILDA does NOT create rows (per FR-2 R&R lock / NFR-21); state-transition writes only. **Per-DI writeback failure handling**: if any per-DI write fails per `[D-064]`, HILDA logs to `HildaOpsAlert` per FR-75 with `STATUS-W005` (partial step-1 transition) carrying the failed DI keys; subsequent steps (2-6) proceed only over DIs successfully transitioned; failed-write DIs retry via NFR-10 backoff on next `start_collection` invocation (idempotent per the re-trigger rule below).
  - **(2) Create one PLM issue per unique `(device, milestone, owner_corp_id)` tuple** within `Deliverables_<customer_id>` via the `IssueTracker` adapter (corp PLM target per FR-25 / FR-26). Owner key is `owner_corp_id` per FR-25 P1 + FR-52 I10 + FR-88 lock — **`owner_corp_id` is the corp-directory identifier (NOT an email address); owner email for any downstream outreach uses the FR-9 + `[D-080]` preference rule (`owner_corp_usa_email` preferred; `owner_corp_email` fallback) — these are distinct fields with distinct roles (batch grouping key vs. email recipient address)**. `owner_corp_id` for the tuple is read from any Deliverables row in the tuple (by FR-88 invariant all rows sharing `(device, milestone, owner_corp_id)` carry the same value; no cross-row reconciliation needed). Anchors FR-5 plm_id grouping + `[D-035]` (`customer_id` is constant within a list; per-list key is the 3-tuple). **Skip-rule (per-tuple, NOT per-row)**: when ALL items for a `(device, milestone, owner_corp_id)` tuple are `item_type = Confirmation`, skip PLM issue creation (no document path exists; PLM issue would never receive uploads); the tuple's rows have `plm_id = NULL` and FR-26 / FR-57 / FR-79 PLM-related paths skip these rows. **Mixed tuples** (some Confirmation, some non-Confirmation rows in the same `(device, milestone, owner_corp_id)`): PLM issue IS created and step 3 writes the same `plm_id` to ALL rows in the tuple including the Confirmation rows; PLM-skip semantics for the Confirmation rows in mixed tuples is enforced downstream by per-row `item_type = Confirmation` checks (FR-26 polling, FR-57 doc parsing, FR-79 closure) — NOT by `plm_id = NULL`. **PLM issue payload**: subject / body / labels / owner-identity-field templated from `customizations/issue_tracker/<corp_plm_slug>_adapter_config.yaml`; body includes the list of `(item_no, item_name)` pairs for items in the tuple + a HILDA-tab deep-link per `[D-074]`. Concrete schema deferred to architecture phase (STATUS Flag: `FR-8 PLM issue payload template`). **Adapter return contract**: the corp PLM gateway returns a canonical `plm_id` string (URL or system-native ID per adapter config); HILDA writes the returned string verbatim to the `plm_id` column. SP `plm_id` semantics: opaque to HILDA except as the display value rendered in FR-9 outreach body URLs and the FR-26 polling target. **Authentication**: HILDA calls the corp PLM gateway per FR-25 (a) + FR-51 pattern (d) — the gateway handles authentication via IP-allowlist + fixed-key infrastructure (HILDA's host IP is allowlisted). HILDA's adapter call **specifies two distinct corp human IDs**: (i) the **corp human id from `customizations/issue_tracker/<corp_plm_slug>_adapter_config.yaml`** (the human acting on behalf of HILDA — currently HUMAN; future BOT — this is the API caller identity); (ii) the **issue owner identity = `owner_corp_id`** (the R&D engineer expected to upload deliverables — this is the issue ownership field on the corp PLM side). HILDA does NOT call `credential_service.get_credential` for corp PLM (pattern (d) per FR-51 — no HILDA-stored credential). On gateway HTTP error: adapter raises `ITR-E002` per FR-25 P6 (gateway error; NOT credential issue); logged to `HildaOpsAlert` per FR-75. **CommunicationLog attribution** per NFR-5 / NFR-6 — the row carries **two identity columns**: `actor_corp_id` = the corp human id from adapter config (operational identity executing the gateway call); `originating_pm_id` = `assigned_pm_id` from Projects list lookup by `project_id` per FR-2 PM identity lookup chain per `[D-083]` + `[D-088]` (responsible PM for the milestone; `assigned_pm_id = TPM.User_name` preferred per `[D-088]`; NOT denormalized on Deliverables rows). HILDA caches the Projects list at startup (SIGHUP-refreshed); orphans flagged `STATUS-W004` per FR-2. **Idempotency + crash recovery**: HILDA logs the `(plm_id, customer_id, device, milestone, owner_corp_id)` mapping to `CommunicationLog` with `action_type = plm_issue_created` immediately after the `IssueTracker` call succeeds, **before** step 3 writeback. If the `CommunicationLog` insert itself fails after the PLM creation succeeded, HILDA fires `HildaOpsAlert` per FR-75 with `ITR-E005` (PLM-issue-created-but-log-failed) carrying `(plm_id, tuple_keys)`; HILDA does NOT proceed to step 3 until ops confirms log replay or manually inserts the audit row — prevents step-3 batch-write on an unjournaled PLM creation. On retry of `start_collection` (worker crash, network partition), step 2 checks BOTH the SP `plm_id` column AND recent `CommunicationLog` entries for the tuple before creating; if either source shows existing `plm_id`, skip creation and reuse. Closes the step 2 / step 3 atomicity gap on both sides.
  - **(3) Write `plm_id` back to all DeliveryItems** sharing the `(device, milestone, owner_corp_id)` tuple via `[D-064]` SP REST writeback — batch operation per `(device, milestone, owner_corp_id)`, not per item. Rows for tuples skipped in step 2 (all-Confirmation) are **not touched** (no NULL overwrite; Ph-1: `plm_id` is set once per row at first successful step 3 and never cleared; Ph-2 item-removal scenarios per FR-3 deferred — plm_id-clearing semantics deferred to FR-78 cascade design). **Batch-write failure handling**: on partial batch failure, HILDA logs to `HildaOpsAlert` per FR-75 with `STATUS-W006` (partial plm_id writeback) carrying `(plm_id, tuple_keys, rows_written, rows_failed)`; retry on next `start_collection` invocation (idempotent — already-carrying-`plm_id` rows skipped per the step-2 idempotency rule extended to step 3).
  - **(4) Fire FR-9 initial outreach** per `(device, milestone, owner_corp_id)` batch — one outbound email per `(device, milestone, owner_corp_id)` tuple with structured BATCH reply block per FR-9, OR one email per TG when `tg_email_group_alias` is set (TG is itself scoped within `(device, milestone)` per FR-40 denormalization; per-TG BATCH-id scope is `(device, milestone, TG, owner_corp_id)` per FR-9 path (a)). **Email recipient address** per FR-9 + `[D-080]` preference rule: owner's `owner_corp_usa_email` if set, else `owner_corp_email` — `owner_corp_id` is the tuple grouping key, NOT the email TO field. **All-Confirmation tuples** (PLM-skipped in step 2) still receive FR-9 outreach; body per FR-9 modality-specific templates (CustomerJIRA-only block per FR-25 (b); Confirmation+Email body with no `plm_id` URL — see STATUS Flag `FR-9 CorporatePLM + NSD outreach body format`). Each successful FR-9 outreach dispatch logs to `CommunicationLog` with `action_type = outreach_sent` + `(BATCH_id, recipient_email, tuple_keys, originating_pm_id = assigned_pm_id)` per NFR-5 / NFR-6 — symmetrical with step 2 audit. Each DI included in a successfully-dispatched batch advances `Open → OutreachSent` per FR-7 state machine; DIs in batches that fail dispatch remain in `Open` per FR-9 failure handling (logged to `HildaOpsAlert` per FR-75; retry policy per NFR-10).
  - **(5) Activate runtime polling channels at their natural granularity** (each channel's polling target reflects its upstream system's scope, NOT a one-size-fits-all per-milestone activation):
    - **Email** (FR-23): no per-milestone activation — HILDA's IMAP IDLE / short-poll mailbox monitor runs globally and covers all customers + milestones. Email polling Tier-3 (deadline-tiered escalation per FR-23 cross-FR cadence) is governed by the `polling_schedule` AutomationRule which reads `Milestone.target_date` per `[D-085]` at rule-evaluation time — no per-milestone register/deregister action in step 5.
    - **CustomerJIRA** (FR-25 (b)): no per-milestone activation — HILDA's CustomerJIRA poller runs per `customer_jira_url` (one URL per customer, read from template.yaml per `[D-083]`). Polling target is the customer-scoped JIRA project; if the URL is already being polled for any active milestone in this customer, this milestone's activation is a no-op.
    - **CorporatePLM** (FR-26): per PLM issue = per `(device, milestone, owner_corp_id)` — one polling target per PLM issue created in step 2; tuples skipped in step 2 (all-Confirmation) have no PLM polling target.
    - **NetworkSharedDrive** (FR-55): per `(customer, device, milestone)` — register one polling target for the milestone folder `\\NSD_X\<carrier>\<device>\<milestone>\` if **at least one DI in this milestone has `tracking_modality` containing `NetworkSharedDrive`**. The TG-level `ingress_nsd` field (`nsd1` / `nsd2` / `none`) on the DI rows determines WHICH NSD (`NSD_X = NSD1` or `NSD2`) is polled — both can be active in parallel for the same milestone when different TGs use different NSDs per FR-13.
    - **CorporateMessenger** (FR-50): no polling activation — messenger is outbound-only escalation channel per FR-50 + FR-9 lock; HILDA never polls messenger for inbound.

    Each channel adds its target(s) to the appropriate `polling_schedule` per the deadline-tiered AutomationRule. Polling ceases per FR-74 collection phase closure.
  - **(6) Recompute `Milestone.status` via the FR-6 precedence ladder** and write the result via `[D-064]` REST writeback to the **single Milestones SP list row** for the milestone per `[D-083]` (per the FR-6 cascade-ordering rule, recompute happens once at cascade end — not per-step intermediate). `Milestone.target_date` is read from the Milestones SP list row per `[D-085]` at recompute time. **Typical case**: at least one DI advanced past `Open` in step 4 (successful outreach) → result = `In Progress` per FR-6 rung (3). **Outreach-stalled edge case**: if ALL step 4 outreach failed (Email server down, alias broken, all dispatches errored) and no DI is past `Open`, DIs still sit in `Open` after step 1's transition — result is `In Progress` per FR-6 rung (3) (any DI past `Not Started` qualifies); HILDA additionally fires `HildaOpsAlert` per FR-75 with `STATUS-W007` (milestone-kicked-off-outreach-stalled) for ops triage. The status reflects collection-started; the alert flags the stalled-outreach concern. **Already-past-deadline edge case**: if `Milestone.target_date < today` at step 6 evaluation, FR-6 rung (2) Delayed supersedes — result = `Delayed`, and HILDA fires `HildaOpsAlert` per FR-75 with `STATUS-W008` (milestone-kicked-off-already-delayed) for TPM/ops awareness. HILDA owns this status compute and write per FR-6 + NFR-21 writeback scope.

  **For items added to the milestone AFTER Start Collection has fired** `[Ph-2]` (via FR-3 SP-side TPM add, deferred to Ph-2 per DEF-22): per-item `StartItemCollection` logic applies (FR-28 `ItemCreated` mid-collection trigger `[Ph-2]` → FR-29 `StartItemCollection` action). This covers steps (1)–(4) per-item (no Milestone.status change needed since milestone is already `In Progress`; no polling-channel re-activation needed since the milestone-scoped schedule already covers the new item). **Ph-1**: not applicable — the per-milestone row set is fixed at `setup_milestone` time.

  **Re-triggering Start Collection on an already-active milestone**: SP UI engineer's web part overwrites `milestone_collection_started_at = <now>` on every click — re-trigger overwrites the original kickoff timestamp. HILDA reads the timestamp at task dispatch but does not persist a separate audit trail of prior kickoffs (acceptable per Ph-1 R&R — `CommunicationLog` step 2 entries serve as the audit trail of when HILDA-side kickoff work executed). HILDA's 6-step task is idempotent across all steps — (1) is a no-op for DIs already past `Open` (failed-step-1 DIs from a prior run retry here); (2) is a no-op per the PLM idempotency rule above; (3) is a no-op for DIs already carrying their `plm_id` (failed-step-3 DIs from a prior run retry here); (4) re-fires outreach only for DIs still in `Open` state (already-`OutreachSent` DIs are skipped); (5) polling already active — no-op; (6) `Milestone.status` already at the recomputed value — no-op (per FR-6 no-op-suppression: HILDA reads current Milestones row value before writing and only writes when the recomputed value differs).
- **FR-9** `[Ph-1]` — Initial owner outreach is sent via the DeliveryItem's `tracking_modality` (see FR-7 for all values). **Outreach trigger**: FR-9 fires as step (4) of FR-8 collection kickoff (per `(device, milestone, owner_corp_id)` batch dispatch after PLM issue creation + plm_id writeback); also fires per-item for items added mid-collection via FR-28 `ItemCreated` → `StartItemCollection` action `[Ph-2]` (per FR-3 / DEF-22 — mid-milestone item add deferred to Ph-2; not applicable in Ph-1). **Outreach channel is always email** for items whose `tracking_modality` set yields any outreach — the MODALITY determines WHAT the email says (link to PLM issue, NSD path, BATCH reply block) NOT WHETHER an email is sent. **Outreach sender**: HILDA sends initial outreach on behalf of the assigned PM — `(assigned_pm_id, pm_display_name, pm_email)` resolved via the Projects SP list lookup by `project_id` per FR-2 PM identity lookup chain + `[D-083]` + `[D-088]`. **`pm_display_name`** (= `TPM.Name`) appears in the email signature; **`pm_email`** (= `TPM.Work_email`) appears as the informational PM-identity reference in the signature only — the stable From address is the HILDA team mailbox per FR-23. HILDA caches the Projects list at startup (SIGHUP-refreshed); orphans flagged `STATUS-W004` per FR-2. Reply-to header is set to the HILDA mailbox so owner replies route through FR-12 → `BATCH-id` resolution.
