The `[HILDA:<customer_id>/<device_id>/<milestone_id>/<item_id>/<pm_id>]` subject tag per FR-24 is prepended to outbound subjects so inbound replies route via FR-23 stream-(b) + FR-24 tag fallback. **TG-coordinator personal identity (`tg_owner_corp_usa_email` / `tg_owner_corp_id` / `tg_owner_name` per FR-88 + `[D-086]`) is NOT used for email outreach** — those personal fields are contacted only via FR-71 Owner Discovery Function `[Ph-2]` (corp messenger) for engineer reassignment. **`tg_email_group_alias`** (the TG's group distribution list — a separate field from the coordinator's personal identity) IS used for outreach when set (per path (a) below). **Owner email selection per FR-88 preference rule**: HILDA uses the owner's `owner_corp_usa_email` when set; falls back to `owner_corp_email` when `owner_corp_usa_email` is null. The same preference rule applies to TG-level outreach when `tg_email_group_alias` is not set (HILDA outreaches to each item's owner using the corp_usa_email-first rule). When `tg_email_group_alias` IS set, outreach goes to the alias (not per-owner emails) per (a) below — preference rule does not apply at the TG-alias case. For `Email` modality: **(a) when `tg_email_group_alias` is set for the TG** — one outreach email is sent to the alias containing all items in the TG grouped by owner; the email contains one structured BATCH reply block per owner (each block carries its own `BATCH-<id>`), so any TG member can fill in any owner's block and HILDA routes the response by the `BATCH-<id>` in the filled block; sender identity does not restrict which block a respondent may fill in. Each per-owner BATCH block additionally embeds that owner's `plm_id` URL when `CorporatePLM` is in the modality set — multiple owners in the same TG-alias email reference distinct PLM issues (one per `(device, milestone, owner_corp_id)` tuple per FR-5 / FR-8 step 2). **BATCH-id scope for path (a)**: `(device, milestone, TG, owner_corp_id)` — an owner with items spanning multiple TGs whose aliases differ within the same `(device, milestone)` receives multiple emails with distinct BATCH-ids; **(b) when `tg_email_group_alias` is not set** — multiple DeliveryItems owned by the same recipient within the same `(device, milestone, owner_corp_id)` tuple are consolidated into one outbound message per round identified by a stable `BATCH-<id>` (**BATCH-id scope for path (b)**: `(device, milestone, owner_corp_id)` — matches PLM issue grouping per FR-5 + `[D-035]` + FR-8 step 2; batches do NOT cross devices even when the same owner has items in multiple devices for the same milestone), with a per-item structured reply block grouped by `tg_name` `[Ph-1]` and per-item `mailto:` quick-update tap-links `[Ph-2]` in the body; the outbound email populates the CC field with the union of `email_cc_list` values across all items in the batch. **`email_cc_list` is set per-work-item in template.yaml** (FR-40 schema) — no TG-level `default_cc_list` cascade per FR-56 (a) lock 2026-06-12 (TG-level `default_cc_list` was dropped from the denormalized field set). Per-item `email_cc_list` is TPM-editable inline per FR-14. CC recipients receive the full outbound email for visibility — their inbound replies are processed by FR-12 sender-mismatch logic (CC address is not the registered owner for the BATCH-id) and surfaced as a `Sender mismatch` flag via `HildaOpsAlert` per FR-75 (TPM dashboard channel) for TPM review. **Outbound email encoding**: every outbound outreach (paths (a) + (b)) is sent `multipart/alternative` with both plain-text and HTML parts; the structured BATCH reply block is **ASCII-only** (no Unicode quotes / dashes / smart-formatting) to make regex parsing on inbound (per FR-12 path (a)) robust against MUA mangling. For `CorporateMessenger` modality, no initial outreach message is sent — CorporateMessenger is used exclusively as the **FR-11 escalation channel** per FR-50 + FR-65 lock 2026-06-17 when reminder cadence escalates past email-only thresholds (typically late-stage deadline-proximity rungs where owner has not responded to N successive email reminders). FR-10 reminders are email-only per FR-65 lead-in lock. CorporateMessenger is outbound-only per FR-50; inbound replies via messenger are NOT processed by the state machine — owner is directed to email in the escalation message body per FR-23 signature contract. For `CorporatePLM` and `NetworkSharedDrive` modalities, outreach informs the owner of the expected delivery path (PLM issue reference or shared drive path) via email (CC list construction is the same union-of-`email_cc_list` pattern as `Email` modality per (b) above) — outbound format definition pending architecture phase per STATUS.md Flag 'FR-9 CorporatePLM + NSD outreach body format'. **`CustomerJIRA`-only items** (per FR-7 must be `item_type = Confirmation`; `tracking_modality = [CustomerJIRA]` alone): HILDA DOES send outbound email outreach per FR-25 (b) lock 2026-06-17 — recipient is the role-collapsed owner-PM identity (the PM's own email per FR-25 (b) template constraint `TSC-W006`); body is tailored to JIRA modality (e.g., 'please review open JIRA tickets at `<customer_jira_url>` and reply to confirm milestone completion'). The `[HILDA:...]` tag per FR-24 is preserved; close-intent reply routes via FR-23 stream-(b) per FR-24 lock. **State machine**: `Open → OutreachSent` (FR-8 initial outreach fires) → `UnderPMReview` (close-intent reply per FR-12; skipping `DocumentReceived` + `OwnerClosed` per FR-7 I8 Confirmation skip rule). Close-intent comes EXCLUSIVELY via reply email per FR-25 (b) close-intent path exclusivity lock — closing all customer JIRA tickets does NOT auto-advance the item (JIRA polling is informational; updates `jira_open_ticket_count` + `jira_ticket_summary_json` columns on Deliverables row per FR-25 (b) lock). `customer_jira_url` is HILDA-config-only in template.yaml per `[D-083]` (NOT in SP). In production CustomerJIRA always appears as CustomerJIRA-only (no SPUI + CustomerJIRA combination, no CustomerJIRA mixed with other modalities — per FR-9 2026-06-16 lock). For **`SPUI` `[Ph-2]` modality**, no HILDA-initiated owner outreach — SPUI is not an owner-side delivery channel; TPM uploads via HILDA-rendered form per FR-62 / `[D-074]`. SPUI is always combined with at least one outreach-capable modality (Email / CorporatePLM / NSD) — those drive the `Open → OutreachSent` transition normally and SPUI is the supplementary upload path. **State transition on dispatch**: on successful dispatch, outreached DeliveryItems advance to `OutreachSent` delivery state. **Outreach failure**: items whose outreach dispatch fails (e.g., email bounce; alias rejection; corp messenger delivery error) do NOT advance to `OutreachSent` — they remain in `Open` state. Failure is logged to `HildaOpsAlert` per FR-75; retry policy per NFR-10 (transient backoff for retryable errors; non-recoverable: raise registered error code immediately). Exact backoff parameters (base / max delay / max retries) defined in NFR-10 — single source of truth. On retry success, the item advances per the normal dispatch path.
- **FR-10** `[Ph-1]` — **Scheduled reminders + cross-channel escalation**. **Reminder eligibility**: rule engine sends email reminders when EITHER (a) `delivery_state = OutreachSent` AND `days_since_last_owner_contact > N` (owner has been outreached, no response yet) OR (b) `delivery_state = DocumentReceived` AND `doc_count_received < doc_count` AND `days_since_last_owner_contact > N` (some docs received but `doc_count` not yet reached — waiting for remaining docs from owner). `N` is per-rule configurable. **Field sources**: `days_since_last_owner_contact = today − row's last_owner_contacted` (HILDA sets `last_owner_contacted = <now>` on every successful outbound — FR-9 initial outreach, FR-10 reminder, FR-10 escalation). **`reminder_count` is a HILDA-managed integer column** on the work-item row (captured in `SP_lists_authoritative.xlsx`); incremented atomically by HILDA when a reminder or escalation dispatches successfully; logged to `CommunicationLog` with `action_type = reminder_sent` or `escalation_sent`. **`last_owner_response_at` is a new HILDA-managed datetime column** on the work-item row (captured in `SP_lists_authoritative.xlsx`); set by FR-12 inbound email parsing when any status / comment update from the owner is processed — **NOT** set by HILDA-side writes (avoids the SP `Modified` system-column collision: every HILDA write would flip `Modified` and break "no response" detection). **FR-12 dependency + false-escalation guard**: rule engine waits ≥ FR-23 deadline-tiered upper bound (configurable; default = 1 hour) since `last_owner_contacted` before considering a row "no response" eligible for reminder / escalation count increment — prevents false escalation when owner has replied but FR-12 hasn't yet processed the reply. **Escalation trigger**: when `last_owner_response_at` is older than the escalation threshold (or NULL) AND `reminder_count ≥ M` (per-rule configurable), the rule engine additionally sends a cross-channel escalation via corp messenger. **Note on CorporateMessenger dual meaning**: HILDA's outbound corp-messenger escalation channel is a `[Ph-1]` capability and is **independent of the `CorporateMessenger` `tracking_modality` value `[Ph-2]`** (which is for owner-initiated status updates via messenger). FR-10 escalation fires for items stuck in `OutreachSent` or `DocumentReceived` regardless of which modality the item uses for status / documents (per FR-7 "CorporateMessenger escalation is automatic regardless of modality"). Inbound messenger response handling is `[Ph-2]` per FR-54. **Escalation routing — per-(device, milestone, TG, owner) scope** (matches FR-9 path (a) BATCH-id discipline): when `corp_id_list` (TG-wide messenger broadcast list — distinct from `tg_owner_corp_id` which identifies the single TG coordinator used by FR-71 ODF; clarified 2026-06-13) is set for the owner's TG, the escalation message is sent to all corp IDs in `corp_id_list`; when `corp_id_list` is not set, the escalation goes to the individual `owner_corp_id`. **Aggregation scope is per-(device, milestone, TG, owner)** — one message per TG-corp_id_list (or per individual `owner_corp_id` when `corp_id_list` is unset), containing all eligible items in that TG for that owner. If the owner spans multiple TGs in the same (device, milestone), the owner receives one escalation message per TG (matches FR-9 batching discipline; batches do NOT cross TGs or devices). **Ph-1 owner-UX guidance**: outbound escalation message includes an explicit instruction directing owners to reply via email (FR-12) for status updates — NOT via messenger. Sample wording in the outbound template: "Please reply via email to <hilda mailbox> with status. Direct messenger replies are not processed in Ph-1." (FR-54 inbound messenger processing is `[Ph-2]`.) **Escalation failure handling**: escalation dispatch failure is logged to `HildaOpsAlert` per FR-75; retry policy per NFR-10 (transient: exponential backoff base 30 s, max 30 min, max 5 retries; non-recoverable: raise registered error code immediately). On retry success, escalation is recorded in `CommunicationLog` with `action_type = escalation_sent` and `reminder_count` is incremented; on permanent failure, escalation is marked failed and the rule engine raises a `HildaOpsAlert` for TPM-assisted manual contact.
- **FR-11** `[Ph-1]` — **Deadline-proximity escalation**. **Eligibility**: rule engine fires deadline-proximity escalation when `Milestone.target_date - today ≤ N` AND `delivery_state ∈ {OutreachSent, DocumentReceived}` (same eligibility scope as FR-10 reminder; excludes `Open`, post-owner states `{UnderPMReview, ReadyForSubmission, SubmittedToCustomer}`, terminal `Closed`, and owner-reported `Delayed` / `Blocked`). `N` (days-before-deadline threshold) is configured in the `DeadlineProximity` rule under `customizations/rules/` (per `[D-062]` Global / Customer / Device 3-tier resolution); default `N = 3` days. Multiple escalation rungs supported (e.g., `escalation_l1` at `N = 7`, `escalation_l2` at `N = 3`, `escalation_l3` at `N = 0`). **Field model** (per `[D-083]` 2-list architecture + `[D-085]` target_date sole source): `target_date` is the sole authoritative deadline column on the **Milestones SP list** row for the milestone — TPM-editable; SP-UI exposes it via the milestone view; HILDA reads it directly at rule-evaluation time. NO per-item `expected_completion_date` column on `Deliverables_<customer_id>` (removed per `[D-085]`); all items in a milestone share the milestone's `target_date` in Ph-1; Ph-2 may add a per-item override column (`expected_completion_date_override` with NULL semantic) if operational need surfaces. **TPM edit flow**: (1) TPM edits the Milestones row's `target_date` via the milestone-view UI; (2) SP UI engineer's web part writes the new `target_date` to the single Milestones row in one SP transaction; (3) one SP-alert fires for the Milestones-row update; (4) HILDA receives the alert, re-arms `DeadlineProximity` evaluation per FR-28 for all items in the milestone (see Re-arm semantics below). NO N-row cascade write; NO `[D-082]` cascade-dedup logic needed for date propagation (obsoleted per `[D-085]`). **Escalation channel + content + aggregation** (Ph-1): channel = email (FR-12 outbound) to owner with PM CC (PM identified via Projects SP list lookup per FR-9 sender attribution — `assigned_pm_id` looked up by `project_id` per `[D-083]`). Aggregation scope is **per-`(device, milestone, TG, owner)`** — one email per TG-batch (matches FR-9 path (a) / FR-10 escalation discipline), containing all proximity-eligible items in that batch. Content includes days-until-deadline (or days-past-deadline), per-item status summary, and reply instructions per FR-12 structured reply block. Optional corp-messenger escalation when the rule has `escalate_via_messenger = true` (independent of FR-10's no-response trigger — FR-11 fires on deadline proximity regardless of response state). **Relationship to FR-10 (no-response escalation)**: FR-10 and FR-11 are independent rule engine triggers and may fire concurrently. **Combined-message rule**: if BOTH FR-10 and FR-11 fire within the same evaluation cycle for the same `(device, milestone, TG, owner)` batch, HILDA produces ONE combined email covering both concerns (subject prefixed `[Reminder + Deadline]`); CommunicationLog records both action_types (`reminder_sent` + `escalation_sent`). If they fire in separate cycles, owner receives separate messages. **Relationship to FR-6 `Milestone.status = Delayed`**: FR-11 fires PROACTIVE escalation at `N` days before deadline (multi-tier). FR-6 sets `Milestone.status = Delayed` RETROSPECTIVELY when `Milestone.target_date < today AND state ∉ {ReadyForSubmission, SubmittedToCustomer, Closed}` per FR-6 precedence ladder. Both are triggered by `Milestone.target_date`; FR-11 warns owner before deadline, FR-6 records milestone falling behind after. **Re-arm semantics**: on TPM `target_date` edit, HILDA invalidates any per-item "last DeadlineProximity escalation sent at rung `L_k`" state and re-evaluates the trigger at the next rule eval cycle against the new `target_date` value. **Cooldown + past-deadline behavior**: once FR-11 escalation has fired for an item at proximity rung `L_k`, no further FR-11 escalation fires for that item at the same rung until either (a) `target_date` is edited (re-arm above), or (b) the next proximity threshold `L_(k+1)` is crossed. For past-deadline items (`Milestone.target_date < today`) once all rungs have been escalated, FR-11 surfaces a daily one-line summary to the TPM dashboard via `HildaOpsAlert` per FR-75 (NOT additional owner emails). Architecture-phase: integrate with FR-6 `Milestone.status = Delayed` flag for unified TPM-dashboard surfacing.
- **FR-12** `[Ph-1]` — **Inbound email reply routing**. **Scope**: Email modality inbound replies (owner → HILDA mailbox per FR-23). Other modalities have separate inbound paths — corp messenger replies = FR-54 `[Ph-2]`; customer JIRA closure = FR-25 polling; PLM document upload = FR-26 polling; NSD document upload = FR-55 polling. **Three convergent paths, all keyed on `BATCH-<id>`**: **(a) `[Ph-1]`** structured reply block edited in place by the owner, regex-parsed from the body (format per FR-9 outreach spec); **(b) `[Ph-2]`** per-item `mailto:` tap-links that pre-compose tiny emails parsed from the subject (`[HILDA] BATCH-<id> ITEM-<n> <STATUS>`); **(c) `[Ph-1]`** free-text replies that match neither path are processed in two stages — **(c.1)** rule-based parsing attempts to extract status and comment against item-name tokens and `BATCH-<id>` context; above threshold, auto-apply; **(c.2)** if rule-based parsing fails, the runtime LLM infers item classification and status — above LLM confidence threshold, auto-apply; below threshold, record as comments on all batch items and raise the Manual triage flag (DEF-1 promoted 2026-05-13). When path (c) fires on an email that also contains attachments, a single fused LLM call processes email body, first-page attachment excerpts, and the batch DeliveryItem list together, covering both message classification and attachment routing in one pass (see FR-52, anchors `[D-034]`). **BATCH-id resolution**: HILDA generates BATCH-id on outbound dispatch (per FR-9) and persists the mapping `BATCH-id → (customer_id, device, milestone, TG, owner, item_list, outreach_target_email)` to a dedicated **`CommunicationBatch`** row in storage (architecture-phase: distinct table from `CommunicationLog`). On inbound, FR-12 looks up the BATCH-id in `CommunicationBatch` to obtain the batch context. BATCH-id scope per FR-9: path (a) outreach = `(device, milestone, TG, owner)`; path (b) outreach = `(device, milestone, owner)`. **Idempotency**: status applies are idempotent on `(BATCH-id, item-index, status)`. **Write semantics on successful inbound processing**: (1) **`last_owner_response_at = <now>`** on every row in the BATCH-id's `item_list` (per FR-10 dependency — flips FR-10's no-response signal); write via `[D-064]` REST writeback (failure to write `last_owner_response_at` is logged but does not block the status-apply — idempotent). (2) **State write discipline for `Delayed` / `Blocked`** (Ph-1 only via Email modality per FR-7): HILDA sets `prior_delivery_state = <current delivery_state>` BEFORE writing the new `delivery_state` value — both writes in one `[D-064]` REST batch (idempotent on retry). (3) **State write discipline for recovery from `Delayed` / `Blocked`** (owner replies with target state per FR-7 exit paths): HILDA clears `prior_delivery_state` (sets NULL) and writes the new `delivery_state` in one `[D-064]` REST batch. **Sender match semantic**: HILDA records the outreach target email (the address per FR-9 preference rule — `owner_corp_usa_email` if set else `owner_corp_email`) in the `CommunicationBatch.outreach_target_email` field at outbound dispatch. Inbound sender matches if it equals **(a)** the recorded outreach target, OR **(b)** the OTHER owner email field on the row (e.g., outreach went to usa_email but owner replies from corp_email — still a match; both are registered owner identities per FR-88), OR **(c)** the TG's `tg_email_group_alias` per the TG-response rule. The sender email address is captured in `CommunicationLog` for attribution on every inbound path. **Sender mismatch handling**: if sender matches none of (a)/(b)/(c), the update is applied (not a hard rejection) and recorded in `CommunicationLog` with `action_type = inbound_received` + `sender_match = false` + sender email; the PM dashboard (per `[D-074]` — HILDA-rendered web part accessing CommunicationLog via HILDA API) queries CommunicationLog for `sender_match = false` entries to surface a `Sender mismatch` flag for PM review. Stricter sender-enforcement rules are configurable via `AutomationRules` (e.g., reject inbound when `sender_match = false` for high-trust customers). **Path (c) auto-apply PM notification** (c.1 + c.2 above-threshold success): HILDA records the auto-apply event in `CommunicationLog` with `action_type = auto_applied_with_rule` (c.1) or `auto_applied_with_llm` (c.2) + confidence + original email reference. PM views via dashboard refresh (no separate email — PM was already CC'd on the original outbound per FR-9, so PM already saw the owner's reply). If the auto-apply causes a state transition the PM cares about (e.g., `Delayed` / `Blocked`), normal rule-engine notifications fire per FR-10 / FR-11 escalation cascade. **Manual triage flag** (path c.2 below threshold): HILDA writes `manual_triage_required = true` on every item in the batch — `manual_triage_required` is a row-level boolean column (captured in `SP_lists_authoritative.xlsx`; HILDA-written, TPM-readable, TPM-clearable when triage resolved). PM dashboard query filters on this column to surface the triage queue. Original email + LLM confidence + parsed candidate status are stored in `CommunicationLog` with `action_type = manual_triage_required`. TPM resolves by reading the original email and writing the correct `delivery_state` (and `prior_delivery_state` if Delayed/Blocked) per FR-14, then clears the `manual_triage_required` flag. **Attachments**: inbound email attachments are routed to DeliveryItems per FR-52. **Attachments without BATCH-id match** (owner forgot the reply block; sender unknown to any active batch): HILDA processes attachments via FR-52 routing pipeline using sender's `owner_corp_usa_email` / `owner_corp_email` lookup to find candidate items; failing match, attachments land on the milestone default work-item's `unrouted` NSD path per FR-78 + FR-86. Original email + sender + attachment metadata are logged to `CommunicationLog` for traceability.
- **FR-13** `[Ph-1]` — HILDA manages owner deliverables and internal artifacts across a **dual NSD topology** (anchors `[D-013]` impl note 2026-05-28). **NSD path source-of-truth for `customer_id` + `device_id`**: the canonical primary-key value set originates in `template.yaml` (which defines the universe of `(customer, device, milestone)` tuples HILDA tracks per FR-2 Runtime role + FR-40); at runtime HILDA reads the SAME values directly **from the work-item row in `Deliverables_<customer_id>`** on SP-alert receive (via `sharepoint_integration.SpCrud.get_items` with `$filter`; cached per FR-84 resolution path). Each row carries `customer_id` + `model` (= `device_id` in template.yaml) + `minorMilestone` (= `milestone_id`) duplicated across rows. Template.yaml values + SP row values MUST match by virtue of the `setup_milestone` discipline (SP UI engineer's web part writes SP rows using template.yaml as the field/default reference per FR-2 R&R lock). All NSD-path source values (`customer_id`, `model` / `device_id`, `minorMilestone`) live as columns on every work-item row in `Deliverables_<customer_id>` per FR-2 — no cross-list joins required. Below NSD path templates use `<carrier>` = `customer_id` and `<device>` = `device_id`: **NSD1 hosts two SEPARATE top-level directories**: `\\NSD1\internal\` is HILDA's exclusive write target (the classified storage tree at `\\NSD1\internal\<carrier>\<device>\<milestone>\<tg_path_id>\`); `\\NSD1\<carrier>\` (no `internal` prefix) is one of two ingress drives where owners drop documents at `\\NSD1\<carrier>\<device>\<milestone>\<Folder-N>\`. The ingress + internal trees are independent — HILDA never writes to the `<carrier>/` tree on NSD1 (same write-discipline as NSD2). **NSD2** (ingress-only at `\\NSD2\<carrier>\<device>\<milestone>\<Folder-N>\`; HILDA never writes to NSD2; NSD2 originals stay forever, no archival). Both NSDs SMB-mounted on HILDA PC; both authenticate via the same `hilda-svc` NTLM credentials (cifs-utils `sec=ntlmssp` with `~/.smbcredentials` username + password — corp standard; clarified 2026-06-14); both polled identically per the same `polling_schedule` AutomationRule (per FR-55). Polling activation per `(customer, device, milestone)` tuple per FR-8 step 5 + FR-55; both NSD1 and NSD2 ingress folders for the same milestone tuple are polled in parallel as part of the same `polling_schedule` cycle. NSD2-sourced documents are classified into NSD1's `internal/` tree (cross-drive write). **Owner inbound flat folder structure** — owners drop documents at `\\NSD_X\<carrier>\<device>\<milestone>\<Folder-N>\...` (no per-item folders — items have varying counts and per-item folders were rejected by owners with 40+ items). **`<Folder-N>` is an arbitrary string chosen by the TG** (e.g., `WiFi_Tests`, `5G_RF_Tests`, `AGPS`) — HILDA does not enforce naming. The folder-to-TG mapping is captured in `template.yaml` per-TG `folder_routing` block when `folder_routing_enabled = true` per FR-2 TG-level fields; HILDA matches inbound folder names to TGs via FR-52 step 3. Folder names need not be unique across milestones. Item identification at HILDA-side via FR-52's 5-step pipeline (substring on tags → fuzzy on item_name → folder template if TG opted in → LLM → staged → default work-item). **HILDA internal tree path conventions** — under `\\NSD1\internal\<carrier>\<device>\<milestone>\<tg_path_id>\`, four areas at TG level (HILDA-managed special directories use `_` prefix per convention): **(1) `_zip_audit/<original_zip_filename>.zip` `[Ph-2]`** — archive of received zip; **(1a) `_zip_audit/<original_zip_filename>/<extracted-tree>/` `[Ph-2]`** — extracted contents co-located with the zip (audit-only; never uploaded to PLM or carrier portal). **Ph-1**: HILDA does not ingest zip archives; if an owner attaches a zip in Ph-1, HILDA logs `EML-W009` and routes the zip as an opaque attachment via FR-52 routing pipeline. **(2) `<item_path_id>/<doc_type_slug>/<doc_id_slug>/revN/<original_filename>`** — classified storage per item (post-FR-52 routing); `doc_id_slug` derived by slugifying the first-received filename, stable across revisions; `rev1/` = initial receipt; `revN/` (N ≥ 2) = subsequent revisions per FR-17; `[Ph-2]` `is_final = true` set on TPM-approved revision per FR-66; `download_url` = HILDA-mediated link (`https://hilda-proxy.corp/dl/<scoped_token>`) **computed on demand** per `[D-057]` + FR-61 (never persisted on `DocumentIndexRow`; token generated per-(file, item) at dashboard render time with 300 s TTL); resolves at request time to the file's current NSD path per `storage.DocumentItemAssociation.local_nsd_path`. Never exposes a direct UNC path to consumers (NFR-16). **(3) `<item_path_id>/<doc_type_slug>/_staged_revision/<original_filename>` `[Ph-2]`** (renamed 2026-06-16 from `staged/` for convention consistency with `_zip_audit/` and `_unrouted/`) — `[D-039]` Step 3 ambiguous new-vs-revision holding (item resolved but `doc_id_slug` assignment pending); **Ph-1 has only `rev1` per FR-7 single-revision fork — no docs land in this area in Ph-1**; surfaced as `Document classification ambiguous` flag on PM dashboard; on PM resolution HILDA assigns to an existing `<doc_id_slug>/revN/` or creates a new `<doc_id_slug>/rev1/`. **(4) `<item_path_id>/_outbound/`** (renamed 2026-06-16 from `outbound/` for convention consistency) — HILDA-generated artifacts only (QC reports, diagnostics, submission-review outputs); never owner deliverables; never written to PLM or carrier portal.

  **Cross-reference to FR-86 4-path matrix (added 2026-06-15)**: the 4 areas above describe the **classified path layout** under TG. The full NSD storage matrix is defined by **FR-86**, which enumerates 4 distinct path TYPES at the milestone level: `classified` (the layout above — fully-resolved documents), `staged_not_classified` (item resolved but doc_type misaligned with item_type per FR-86 alignment invariant), `staged_not_revision` (= the `<item_path_id>/<doc_type_slug>/staged/` area above; doc_id_slug ambiguous per `[D-039]` Step 3), and `unrouted` (item routing failed; default work-item per FR-78 — see G-1 path below). For the staged/unrouted layouts and TPM resolution flow, see FR-86 + FR-87.

  **Default work-item NSD path (added 2026-06-15 per FR-78 + FR-86)**: documents routed to a milestone's default work-item land at `\\NSD1\internal\<carrier>\<device>\<milestone>\<inferred_tg_name_slug>\_unrouted\<original_filename>` per FR-86 `unrouted` path type. `<inferred_tg_name_slug>` (slugified per `[D-013]` slug convention — alphanumeric + `-` + `_`) is the channel-resolved TG name at ingest time (NSD ingress_nsd / tg_email_group_alias / PLM-id reverse lookup per `[D-060]` impl note) — known even when work-item routing fails. This enables TPM browsing of unrouted docs grouped by TG. The default work-item path does NOT follow the standard `<tg_path_id>/<item_path_id>/...` convention since neither value is resolved at ingest.

  **NSD directory creation responsibility (added 2026-06-15)**: HILDA creates NSD1 `internal/...` directory paths **lazily on first write** per item / per TG / per milestone — no pre-creation step at `setup_milestone` time. NSD subdirectories (`_zip_audit/`, `<tg_path_id>/`, `<item_path_id>/`, `<doc_type_slug>/`, `revN/`, etc.) materialize on-demand via `hilda-svc` SMB write per FR-13 path conventions; missing intermediate directories are created in a single `mkdir -p`-style operation. NSD2 (ingress-only) directory creation is owner-side responsibility (HILDA never writes to NSD2 per FR-13 NSD2 invariant). **Cross-share write performance** (NSD2 → NSD1 internal): HILDA reads doc bytes from NSD2 + writes to NSD1 internal via the SMB layer; same-share writes (NSD1 ingress → NSD1 internal) are faster. If cross-share write throughput becomes a hot spot, a future optimization could mirror NSD2 ingress to NSD1 staging first so internal-tree write is same-share. Tracked in STATUS.md as deferred performance optimization (architecture phase). **Zip file handling `[Ph-2]`** (per FR-72 ZIP ingestion is Ph-2 only): zip files are audit-only — stored in `_zip_audit/`; never uploaded to PLM or carrier portal; version tree operates on individual files only (zip-level versioning is irrelevant). PLM receives extracted individual files as **flat attachments** (PLM is an issue tracker — no directory structure; each extracted file becomes one attachment with its `original_filename`; per-TG collision risk on duplicate filenames acknowledged per `[D-054]`). Carrier portal receives extracted individual files with **source-folder hierarchy preserved** per FR-77 Type-2 routing — the matched source folder + any sub-tree under it is preserved on the carrier side. **PLM and carrier portal content thus differ structurally for zip-extracted files**: PLM is flat; carrier is hierarchical. PLM-source zips remain on PLM unchanged (HILDA never modifies PLM); HILDA extracts locally for routing + carrier upload, writing extracts to NSD1 classified tree for audit consistency. **NSD classified path (`<item_path_id>/<doc_type_slug>/<doc_id_slug>/revN/`) is the source of truth for in-progress owner deliverables; PLM is source of truth for submitted deliverables only** (after OwnerClosed + TPM approval; anchors `[D-040]`); PLM issues created per FR-26. **Ingest write flows by source** — PLM upload timing is **phase-conditional** for Email/NSD/TPM-via-FR-62 sources and uploads only individual files (never zips): **Ph-1** — immediate (single revision per document; `rev1 = is_final = true` auto-set); **Ph-2** — deferred until `OwnerClosed` + TPM approval; only the `is_final = true` revision is uploaded; deferred upload fires post-dispatch via FR-68 sync verification (anchors `[D-040]`, `[D-041]`). PLM-polled documents already in PLM, never re-uploaded; FR-68 handles post-dispatch PLM↔NSD and PLM↔Carrier hash sync verification: for **Email**, HILDA classifies via FR-52 — if attachment is a ZIP `[Ph-2]`, extracts to `_zip_audit/` then routes each extracted file via FR-52; otherwise routes the individual file via FR-52, uploads individual files to the `(device, milestone, owner)` PLM issue, records the document in the HILDA document index per the **file-centric storage model** (`[D-055]` / `[D-056]` / `[D-072]` — split across two tables): **`DocumentIndexRow`** keyed by `file_hash` carries `doc_type, doc_id_slug, rev_number, original_filename, first_page_excerpt, is_final, parser_result, llm_review_findings, inferred_tg_name, nsd_path_type, from_zip, source_zip_filename`; **`DocumentItemAssociation`** keyed by `(file_hash, delivery_item_id)` carries `local_nsd_path, plm_attachment_id, plm_id, upload_timestamp, ingest_source`. **`download_url` is NOT persisted** per `[D-057]` — computed on demand via `make_download_token(file_hash, delivery_item_id)` per storage MODULE.md. `ingest_source` enum: `Email`, `CorporatePLM`, `NetworkSharedDrive`, `HILDA_DASHBOARD` (renamed from `SharePointUI` 2026-06-13 per FR-62 rewrite — upload form is HILDA-rendered, not SP; storage/template_schema cascade tracked in STATUS.md Flags). HILDA writes document file to `<item_path_id>/<doc_type_slug>/<doc_id_slug>/revN/`; for **PLM poll**, document already in PLM — if attachment is a ZIP `[Ph-2]`, extracts locally to `_zip_audit/`; HILDA applies FR-52 routing + D-039 classification, records `plm_attachment_id` and `local_classified_path`, writes extracted individual files to `<item_path_id>/<doc_type_slug>/<doc_id_slug>/revN/` (no PLM re-upload); for **NSD drop** (NSD1 or NSD2), HILDA detects per FR-55 — if file is a ZIP `[Ph-2]`, extracts to NSD1 `_zip_audit/`; routes individual files via FR-52 (folder path is one signal among many, not authoritative); uploads individual files to PLM, writes to NSD1 classified path. Items with `no_customer_upload = true` (FR-80) skip BOTH PLM upload AND carrier portal upload. All download links HILDA-mediated (`https://hilda-proxy.corp/dl/<scoped_token>`) — never direct UNC paths (NFR-16); `actual_item_info` holds the PLM issue URL for the `(device, milestone, owner)` tuple per FR-5 plm_id grouping + FR-57.
- **FR-55** `[Ph-1]` — For DeliveryItems whose `tracking_modality includes NetworkSharedDrive`, HILDA polls **both NSD1 (`\\NSD1\<carrier>\<device>\<milestone>\<Folder-N>\`) and NSD2 (`\\NSD2\<carrier>\<device>\<milestone>\<Folder-N>\`)** per the dual-NSD topology in FR-13 (anchors `[D-013]` impl note 2026-05-28). When a new file is detected, **item routing uses the full FR-52 5-step pipeline** — folder path is one signal among many (consulted at step 3 if the TG has `folder_routing_enabled = True`), NOT the authoritative item identifier as in the prior per-item-folder model. The file is recorded in `CommunicationLog` with `ingest_source = NetworkSharedDrive` (filesystem identity attribution deferred to Ph-3 — see DEF-16). **Zip files `[Ph-2]`** (per FR-72 / FR-13): extracted recursively to `_zip_audit/`; each extracted file routed via FR-52. **Ph-1**: HILDA does not ingest zip archives from NSD; if an owner drops a zip in Ph-1, HILDA logs `EML-W009` and routes the zip as an opaque single file via FR-52. **doc_type**: classified per FR-85 stage 1 (filename regex + LLM ladder; runs first in the FR-85 sequential pipeline before FR-52 work-item routing per FR-85 lock 2026-06-16; supersedes the prior 1:1 derivation per `[D-053]` impl note 2026-06-08). HILDA uploads each individual file (never zips) to the `(device, milestone, owner)` PLM issue per FR-5 plm_id grouping + FR-8 step 2 / `[D-035]` (per FR-13's phase-conditional rule: **Ph-1** immediate; **Ph-2** deferred until `OwnerClosed` + TPM approval, only `is_final = true` revision uploaded per `[D-040]`) + FR-80 `no_customer_upload` gating, writes the document file to `<item_path_id>/<doc_type_slug>/<doc_id_slug>/revN/` in the NSD1 internal tree (**Ph-1**: always `rev1/` per FR-7 single-revision fork; **Ph-2**: `revN/` for subsequent revisions per FR-17), updates the HILDA document index, updates the DeliveryItem record in SharePoint and sets `actual_item_info` to the `(device, milestone, owner)` PLM issue URL per FR-57 + FR-5 + FR-13 QF4 (multiple items sharing the tuple share the same URL; set on first document arrival for the tuple and stable thereafter). Both phases: advances item to `DocumentReceived` when `doc_count` reached per FR-28 `AttachmentReceived` — **except for `doc_count = 0` items** (Confirmation or non-Confirmation), which skip `DocumentReceived` entirely per FR-7 I8 unified skip rule (docs arriving on these items are stored + reviewed per `review_required` but do NOT gate state advancement; owner-done confirmation alone advances). Triggers LLM quality review per FR-53 (only when `review_required = true` AND `doc_type ∈ {test_report, tech_report, waiver}`; never for `default`); on review completion the `AIReviewResult` trigger (FR-28) notifies the PM with findings on the dashboard — does **not** advance `delivery_state` (LLM review completion satisfies `OwnerClosed` guard condition (2) when `review_required = true`; condition (2) is vacuously satisfied when `review_required = false`; `OwnerClosed` still requires explicit owner "done" confirmation plus both guard conditions per FR-7). **Polling target**: per `(customer, device, milestone)` per FR-8 step 5 + FR-13 — one polling target per milestone folder (`\\NSD_X\<carrier>\<device>\<milestone>\`). HILDA registers the target only if at least one DI in the milestone has `NetworkSharedDrive` in its `tracking_modality`; HILDA does **not** poll for milestones with no NSD-modality items. **Polling frequency**: determined at the milestone level from the milestone's `target_date` (single milestone-level deadline per FR-11 + FR-2 flat-table model — all items in the milestone share the same `target_date`; `target_date` is the sole TPM-editable date per FR-11 / FR-14 lock — per-item override is NOT exposed); the same configurable `polling_schedule` used by FR-26 (defined in AutomationRules at Global / Customer / Device tier): `{days_before_deadline, interval_minutes}` breakpoints evaluated against `days_to_deadline = target_date − today`; baseline tier with `days_before_deadline: null` is always required as fallback; example: baseline 60 min, ≤ 3 days → 15 min, ≤ 1 day → 5 min. Polling ceases per FR-74 collection phase closure.
- **FR-52** `[Ph-1]` — When one or more documents arrive from any inbound source (email attachment via FR-12, NSD via FR-55, PLM poll via FR-26, FR-62 SP UI upload Ph-2), the system routes each document to its corresponding DeliveryItem via the following **5-step pipeline (Routing Type 1 — document to work-item)** (anchors `[D-053]`): **(1) Strict substring match (tags)** — `item_description` is a comma-separated tag list per item; every tag must appear as substring in filename (AND logic); top match wins; multi-item: if multiple items match, all matched items receive association entries (symmetric M:M per `[D-055]` — no primary/secondary distinction; PLM fan-out happens at upload time per FR-79). **(2) Fuzzy match (item name)** — rapidfuzz similarity between filename and `item_name` per item; top scorer above threshold wins. **(3) Source folder → work-item** (TG opt-in via `folder_routing_enabled = True`): for NSD-direct files, use outermost folder name relative to milestone root; for zip-extracted files `[Ph-2]`, use outermost folder name relative to fully-extracted zip root; substring match against entries in the per-TG `folder_routing.yaml` template; longest-substring-wins on multi-match. **(4) LLM `ROUTE_ATTACHMENT`** — first-page excerpt + candidate item list within TG scope; above-threshold matches are committed. **(5) Staged → default work-item** — when steps 1–4 fail, the document is associated with the milestone's default work-item (`item_type = Default` per FR-78); `DocumentIndexRow.unrouted_source_path` captures the originating context (NSD parent path; `[Ph-2]` zip-internal path); surfaced on PM/TPM dashboard for manual reassignment via FR-83. **doc_type classification** runs per FR-85 stage 1 — completes BEFORE the 5-step work-item routing pipeline above (which is FR-85 stage 2). Per FR-85 sequential lock 2026-06-16, routing does NOT use `doc_type` as an input (filename tags / fuzzy match / folder template / LLM operate independently of doc_type). Classification produces `doc_type ∈ {test_report, tech_report, waiver, compliance_certification_release_notes, unresolved}` per inbound document. **Alignment + storage**: the `(item_type, doc_type, revision-determination)` tuple is evaluated against the FR-86 storage matrix once the full FR-85 sequential pipeline completes — classification (stage 1) → routing (this FR, stage 2) → revision determination (stage 3 / `[D-039]`) → FR-86 matrix (stage 4). Aligned tuples land on the `classified` NSD path; misaligned land on one of `{staged_not_classified, staged_not_revision, unrouted}` per which stage's outcome triggers the misalignment (per FR-86 4-path matrix), for TPM SP UI resolution per FR-87. (Supersedes the prior 1:1 derivation framing per `[D-053]` impl note 2026-05-28b — withdrawn 2026-06-08; the prior `EML-W005` item-type-mismatch warning is replaced by the storage-matrix landing rule.) **Fused vs. standalone path**: when the triggering email also fires FR-12 path (c), email attachment routing is subsumed into a single fused LLM call for that path — path (c) delivers both a free-text message body and attachments simultaneously, so one LLM call resolves both message classification and document routing together for efficiency and shared context (anchors `[D-034]`); **the FR-52 5-step standalone routing pipeline is bypassed for those attachments — the fused call's output is authoritative**; PLM-polled documents always use the standalone path because they arrive with no concurrent message body to fuse with. **Zip handling `[Ph-2]`** (per FR-72 / FR-13): if an attachment is a `.zip` archive, HILDA routes it to the ZIP ingestion flow (FR-72) — item routing is deferred to FR-72 extraction; each extracted individual file flows through FR-52's 5-step pipeline as a standalone attachment. **Ph-1**: HILDA does not ingest zip archives; if an owner attaches a zip in Ph-1, HILDA logs `EML-W009` and routes the zip as an opaque single file through the 5-step pipeline (zip filename matched against tags / item_name / folder template like any other file). **D-039 new-vs-revision detection** fires AFTER work-item resolution (per `[D-039]` impl note 2026-05-28): **Step 0 `[Ph-1]`**: `file_hash` matches existing document index row for `(delivery_item_id, doc_type)` → exact duplicate, skip — logged in `CommunicationLog`, PM notified. **Step 1 `[Ph-2]`** (no `revN` in Ph-1 per FR-7 single-revision fork — Ph-1 always treats non-duplicate docs as `NEW_DOCUMENT`): slugify `original_filename` → `candidate_slug`; if matches existing `doc_id_slug` → revision (`revN`). **Step 2 `[Ph-2]`**: no slug match → if no existing `doc_id_slug` entries exist for this `(delivery_item_id, doc_type)`, classify as `NEW_DOCUMENT` immediately (no LLM call); otherwise LLM `CLASSIFY_DOC` TaskKind compares `first_page_excerpt` against existing entries — returns REVISION:`<doc_id_slug>` or NEW_DOCUMENT. **Step 3 `[Ph-2]`**: LLM ambiguous → document held in `<doc_type_slug>/_staged_revision/` on NSD per FR-13 area (3) (renamed 2026-06-16 from `staged/` for HILDA-managed-special-directory underscore-prefix convention); `Document classification ambiguous` flag surfaced on PM dashboard for manual assignment. **Ph-1 D-039 effective scope = Step 0 only**: hash match → duplicate (skip); else → `NEW_DOCUMENT` directly (single-revision per FR-7). **Default-work-item-associated docs skip D-039 entirely** — no revision tracking on default work-item since they're inherently unrouted; D-039 resumes when TPM reassigns the document via FR-83. For each document confirmed as a new document or revision, the following actions fire: **(1) PLM upload** via the IssueTracker adapter — **fans out per FR-79 across distinct `(owner_corp_id, plm_id)` pairs** (one PLM upload per pair, replicated `plm_attachment_id` + `upload_timestamp` across all M:M rows sharing the pair; key is `owner_corp_id` per FR-5 plm_id grouping — NOT email); phase-conditional per FR-13: **Ph-1** immediate for Email/NSD/TPM-via-FR-62 sources (single revision; `rev1 = is_final = true` auto-set); **Ph-2** deferred until `OwnerClosed` + TPM approval, only `is_final = true` revision uploaded post-dispatch via FR-68 (anchors `[D-040]`); skipped for CorporatePLM-polled documents (already present in PLM); skipped entirely when `no_customer_upload = true` (FR-80); for PLM-sourced documents the `plm_attachment_id` is already known from the poll and written directly to the document index. **(2)** the individual file written to `<item_path_id>/<doc_type_slug>/<doc_id_slug>/revN/` under the item's shared drive path per FR-13 — **Ph-1**: always `rev1/` per FR-7 single-revision fork (`rev1 = is_final = true` auto-set); **Ph-2**: `rev1/` if new, `revN/` if revision per FR-17. **(3)** the HILDA document index updated per the **file-centric storage model** (`[D-055]` / `[D-056]` / `[D-072]` — split across two tables): **`DocumentIndexRow`** keyed by `file_hash` carries `doc_type, doc_id_slug, rev_number, original_filename, first_page_excerpt, is_final, parser_result, llm_review_findings, inferred_tg_name, nsd_path_type, from_zip, source_zip_filename, unrouted_source_path`; **`DocumentItemAssociation`** keyed by `(file_hash, delivery_item_id)` carries `local_nsd_path, plm_attachment_id, plm_id, upload_timestamp, ingest_source`. `download_url` is NOT persisted per `[D-057]` — computed on demand via `make_download_token(file_hash, delivery_item_id)` per storage MODULE.md. (Note: `delivery_item_id` uniqueness is **per-`Deliverables_<customer_id>` list** — Ph-1/Ph-2 single-carrier scope means no cross-carrier collision; Ph-3+ multi-carrier scope will require `(customer_id, delivery_item_id)` composite key — matches the dashboard URL composite token per FR-5.) **(4)** `delivery_state` advanced to `DocumentReceived` when `doc_count` reached per FR-28 `AttachmentReceived` — **except for `doc_count = 0` items** (Confirmation or non-Confirmation), which skip `DocumentReceived` entirely per FR-7 I8 unified skip rule (docs stored + reviewed but do NOT gate state advancement); LLM quality review triggered per FR-53 (only when `review_required = true` AND `doc_type ∈ {test_report, tech_report, waiver}` — never for `default`); on completion the `AIReviewResult` trigger (FR-28) notifies the PM — does **not** advance `delivery_state`. **(5)** the DeliveryItem record updated in SharePoint and `actual_item_info` set to the `(device, milestone, owner)` PLM issue URL per FR-5 + FR-13 QF4 on first document arrival for the tuple (multiple items sharing the tuple share the same URL) — stable thereafter per FR-57. **No carrier portal upload from default work-item** — TPM must resolve to a real work-item via FR-83 SP UI first; then HILDA re-runs FR-77 Type-2 routing for the document and uploads.
- **FR-14** `[Ph-1]` — **PM/TPM manual override of DeliveryItem fields**. PM/TPM can override TPM-editable fields directly in the SharePoint UI (column-level cell edit on the SP list view; SP UI engineer's web part surfaces TPM-editable columns per FR-56 column model). HILDA's HTML dashboard at `hilda-proxy.corp` per `[D-074]` is the document/review surface — NOT the column-edit surface; column edits are pure SP UI. **Editable fields scope** per FR-56 column model: **owners `[Ph-2]`** (per FR-3 / DEF-22 deferral — mid-milestone owner change is Ph-2; in Ph-1, owners are fixed at `setup_milestone` time per FR-2 R&R lock); `comment` (advisory); `delivery_state` (per FR-7 state-machine, see scope below); `review_required` (with retroactive FR-53 enqueue per below); `manual_triage_required` (TPM clears the flag once FR-12 path c.2 triage resolved); `email_cc_list` (per FR-9 path b CC list); `tracking_modality` (per FR-7 enum); other per FR-56 column model. **Exclusions**: ops-editable-only fields per FR-2 (`customer_id`, `customer_jira_url`, `model`, `project_id`, `assigned_pm_id`); YAML-only fields like `milestone_gating` per `[D-078]`. **`delivery_state` override scope**: TPM may write any value from the 11-value delivery_state enum on a row, with HILDA-side guard verification: (a) `Closed` per FR-7 path (i) once carrier acceptance is confirmed; (b) recovery from `Delayed`/`Blocked` to any pre-approval state per FR-7 I2 exit path (b) (HILDA clears `prior_delivery_state`); (c) roll-back of an automated transition (e.g., `ReadyForSubmission` → `UnderPMReview` if PM approval was premature). HILDA enforces FR-7 state-machine validity at SP-alert receipt — invalid transitions are rejected with `[D-064]` REST revert + `HildaOpsAlert` per FR-75. **`prior_delivery_state` discipline** (parallel to FR-12 inbound discipline): when TPM-via-FR-14 writes `delivery_state = Delayed` or `Blocked` on a row currently in another state, HILDA's `sp_alert_parser` sets `prior_delivery_state = <current value>` BEFORE writing the new `delivery_state` — both writes in one `[D-064]` REST batch (idempotent on retry). When TPM-via-FR-14 writes recovery from `Delayed`/`Blocked`, HILDA clears `prior_delivery_state` (sets NULL) and writes the new `delivery_state` in one batch. **Per-item date override is NOT exposed in TPM SP UI** (per `[D-085]`); `Milestone.target_date` is the sole authoritative deadline. TPM edits Milestones row's `target_date` only via the milestone view; HILDA reads at rule-evaluation time. No per-item `expected_completion_date` column exists on `Deliverables_<customer_id>`. **`review_required` retroactive enqueue**: when TPM sets `review_required` from `false` to `true` on an item that already has received documents in the document index, HILDA retroactively enqueues FR-53 for all existing unreviewed documents on that item so that the `OwnerClosed` guard condition (2) can be satisfied. **Ad-hoc reminders** (SharePoint UI surface: FR-65): PM/TPM can trigger ad-hoc reminders independent of the scheduled rule cadence via the Send Reminder per-row button. **Interaction with FR-10 escalation count**: TPM Send Reminder increments `reminder_count` per FR-10 + xlsx `reminder_count` row Notes (both rule-engine + TPM-initiated reminders count). High-frequency TPM-initiated reminders can therefore cross FR-10's escalation threshold `M` and trigger the FR-10 corp-messenger escalation cascade — intentional behavior (TPM-initiated escalation works the same as rule-engine escalation downstream). **SP-alert routing + write semantics**: PM/TPM SP UI overrides propagate to HILDA via the SP-alert email channel per FR-84 (SP HTTP-to-HILDA is firewall-blocked); HILDA's `sp_alert_parser` sub-module parses the alert email, routes to the DeliveryItem via the **5-field SP-alert routing key** per FR-84 (`customer_id, Model, ProjectID, MinorMilestone, ItemNumber`), and applies the override via `[D-064]` REST writeback to the SP row (column-level write; per NFR-21 write scope). **Idempotency**: HILDA reads current SP column value before applying the override; if the value already matches the incoming alert payload, no-op (skip the `[D-064]` write + skip rule-engine triggers). Read-then-write-if-changed parallels FR-6 no-op suppression. `CommunicationLog` `manual_field_override` entry written once per actual value change (not per duplicate alert). **Attribution + audit** (NFR-5 / NFR-6): HILDA logs every TPM-via-FR-14 override to `CommunicationLog` with `action_type = manual_field_override` + `field_name` + `old_value` + `new_value` + TPM AD account (captured from SP's built-in `Editor` system column on the SP-alert payload — SP UI engineer's web part doesn't author the alert; SP itself does, with the editing TPM as the Editor). NFR-5 audit + NFR-6 attribution satisfied without separate SP UI engineer overhead. **Downstream effects**: TPM-via-FR-14 state changes are real state-machine transitions — they fire FR-28 rule-engine triggers normally. Examples: setting `delivery_state = Closed` on the last remaining non-Closed gating item in a milestone fires `MilestoneAllClosed` per FR-28 (**triggers only when ALL `milestone_gating=true` DIs + the default work-item per FR-78 are `Closed`** — TPM-via-FR-14 single-item close on a milestone with still-open gating items does NOT fire `MilestoneAllClosed`) → `MilestoneStorageCleanup` per FR-76; setting `delivery_state = Delayed` re-arms `DeadlineProximity` per FR-11 (TPM-set Delayed is treated as owner-reported override; subsequent deadline-proximity escalation eligible per FR-11 ladder). **Comments override** does NOT fire any FR-28 trigger — `comment` column is advisory. **Scope**: FR-14 is per-item override (TPM edits one row's column at a time). Bulk state changes are handled by FR-64 Close All Items (multi-row `delivery_state = Closed` cascade); no bulk path exists for other fields in Ph-1 — TPM iterates per-item if needed.
- **FR-15** `[Ph-1]` — **HILDA-managed timestamp + audit columns on DeliveryItem rows**. **`last_owner_contacted`** (HILDA-managed datetime; xlsx `last_owner_contacted` row): updates on every successful HILDA-initiated outbound contact to the owner. **Write paths**: (a) **FR-9 initial outreach** at FR-8 Start Collection step (4); (b) **FR-10 scheduled rule-engine reminders**; (c) **FR-10 corp-messenger escalations**; (d) **FR-65 TPM-initiated ad-hoc reminders** — TPM clicks Send Reminder button → SP web part writes `last_reminder_triggered_at = <now>` on the delivery-item row → SP-alert per `[D-047]` / FR-84 reaches HILDA → HILDA dispatches reminder via item's status-capable modality. All four paths write via `[D-064]` REST writeback on **successful dispatch only** — on dispatch failure, `last_owner_contacted` stays unchanged and the failure is logged to `HildaOpsAlert` per FR-75 (consistent across all four paths). **Exclusion**: TPM-via-FR-14 `delivery_state` overrides do NOT update `last_owner_contacted` — this column is reserved for HILDA-initiated outbound contact to the owner. TPM column edits are administrative writes — they fire FR-28 rule-engine triggers per FR-14 "Downstream effects" but do not advance the owner-contact timestamp. **`actual_completion_date`** (HILDA-managed date; xlsx `actual_completion_date` row): set once on first applicable transition; never overwritten by subsequent state changes. **Set timing covers three transition types** (collectively referred to as **"FR-15-extended semantics"** by FR-7): (a) **Standard fork**: `OwnerClosed` entry (transient state per FR-7 fork rule); (b) **Confirmation / `doc_count = 0` skip path** per FR-7 I8: `OutreachSent → UnderPMReview` direct transition; (c) **CustomerJIRA-only items** per FR-7 I4 + FR-9 lock: `Open → UnderPMReview` direct transition on JIRA-closure detection. **`Modified`** (SP built-in system column; the custom `last_updated` column was dropped 2026-06-12 per SP UI engineer review — use SP's native `Modified` field instead): auto-managed by SP itself on any row write — HILDA doesn't write to it explicitly. **`Modified` is NOT a reliable "no owner response" signal** — it flips on every row write (HILDA writeback, TPM edits, SP UI engineer's web part writes). FR-10 escalation uses **`last_owner_response_at`** (below) instead. `Modified` is for SP-internal audit only. **Write owner** (clarified 2026-06-12 per `[D-073]` + `[D-064]`): HILDA writes `last_owner_contacted`, `actual_completion_date`, and the other HILDA-managed audit columns below to the appropriate row in **`Deliverables_<customer_id>`** via `sharepoint_integration` REST writeback per `[D-064]` (SP is the canonical store for DeliveryItem state per `[D-071]`; HILDA does NOT mirror these fields in its own Postgres). **HILDA-managed audit columns index** (FR-15 serves as the pointer to the authoritative FR for each column): **`last_owner_contacted`** (outbound; this FR); **`last_owner_response_at`** (inbound; FR-12 / FR-10 I4) — HILDA-managed datetime set by FR-12 inbound email parsing when ANY status / comment update from the owner is processed; NOT set by HILDA-side writes (decouples from SP `Modified` column); NULL until first owner response; used by FR-10 escalation trigger as the "no response" signal; **`last_reminder_triggered_at`** (FR-65 TPM trigger) — SP-UI-written by SP web part on Send Reminder button click → SP-alert wakes HILDA to dispatch the reminder; **`reminder_count`** (FR-10 I3) — HILDA-managed integer; incremented atomically on every successful reminder or escalation dispatch (rule-engine + TPM-initiated); **`manual_triage_required`** (FR-12 I4) — HILDA-set on FR-12 path c.2 below-threshold classification; TPM-clearable when triage resolved; **`prior_delivery_state`** (FR-7 I2) — set when `delivery_state` enters `Delayed` or `Blocked` from another state (captures pre-transition state); cleared (set to NULL) on exit; discipline lives in FR-7 I2 + FR-12 inbound + FR-14 TPM-via-SP; **`actual_completion_date`** (this FR; FR-15-extended per FR-7) — set on first applicable transition; never overwritten; **`Modified`** (SP system column; advisory only — see warning above).

### PM review & resolution path (Stage 4)

- **FR-16** `[Ph-1]` — **Test-report parser firing**. When a document **lands on the FR-86 `classified` NSD path** AND `doc_type = test_report` — i.e., the full FR-85 sequential pipeline (stage 1 classification → stage 2 routing → stage 3 revision determination → stage 4 matrix) has completed with aligned `(item_type, doc_type)` — the `TriggerParser` rule action (FR-28 / FR-29) fires. For documents on `staged_not_classified`, `staged_not_revision`, or `unrouted` paths, parser does NOT fire until TPM resolves the document to `classified` via FR-87 (see post-resolution trigger below). **Ingest channels**: email attachment (FR-52), NSD drop (FR-55), PLM poll (FR-26), or HILDA dashboard upload (FR-62 `[Ph-2]`). **Parallel firing with FR-53 LLM review**: when a document lands `classified`, the rule engine fires BOTH `TriggerParser` (this FR, for `doc_type = test_report` regardless of `review_required`) AND `TriggerLLMReview` (FR-53, for `doc_type ∈ {test_report, tech_report, waiver}` AND `review_required = true`) — parallel execution; no dependency between them; results persist independently to `DocumentIndexRow.parser_result` and `DocumentIndexRow.llm_review_findings` respectively. **Parser output**: the parser extracts per-test-case `(test_case_id, status ∈ {passed, failed, non-applicable, waived, not-started}, [waiver_ref])` tuples. The **FR-46 classification rule** then determines `final | interim` from the parser output (rule: `final` iff every test case is `{passed, non-applicable, waived}` AND every `failed` test case carries a `waiver_ref` (reclassified as `waived`); otherwise `interim`). **Persistence**: parser writes its output to **`DocumentIndexRow.parser_result`** keyed by `file_hash` per `[D-055]` file-centric storage model + FR-57 (NOT to the DeliveryItem row — DocumentIndexRow is the canonical location). The persisted structure carries the per-test-case status grid + the `final | interim` classification flag per FR-46 (schema = Pydantic model in `storage` MODULE.md; architecture-phase detail). **Per-revision firing**: parser fires once per revision row (**Ph-1**: always `rev1` per FR-7 single-revision fork — one parser fire per document; **Ph-2**: each `revN` triggers its own parser fire, writing its own `parser_result` to the corresponding `DocumentIndexRow`; prior revisions' `parser_result` is preserved unchanged). **Parser failure handling**: if the parser fails to extract test cases (malformed document, unexpected format, profile mismatch), `parser_result` is written with status `parser_failed` + error code (`TST-W001` malformed structure / `TST-W002` profile mismatch / etc.) + raw text excerpt for TPM triage; logged to `HildaOpsAlert` per FR-75; FR-53 LLM review still fires when `review_required = true` (operates on the raw document independently of parser success — provides degraded-mode review). **Post-resolution parser fire** (FR-87 cascade): when a doc was `unresolved` at ingest and TPM resolves to `test_report` via FR-87 step (B), `TriggerParser` fires after the doc reaches the `classified` NSD path per FR-87's async-tail. Same applies when TPM reassigns a doc from `Default` work-item to a real work-item via FR-83 + resolves doc_type to `test_report`. **PM presentation surface**: PM views the classification + per-test-case status grid + resolution-path picker for unresolved failures **via HILDA's rendered document section per FR-60** (HILDA-rendered HTML at `hilda-proxy.corp/docs/<customer_id>/<item_id>` per `[D-074]` Variant A — NOT SP UI; SP UI surfaces only the View Documents link-out anchor per FR-56 (c)). Resolution-path determination per FR-47 `[Ph-2]`. **Execution location**: parser runs as a `workflow_engine` Celery task in `hilda-worker` per `[D-022]` (architecture-phase detail). Per-customer parsers loaded from `customizations/test_report_parsers/<customer_id>.py` (generated by Test Report Document Profiler per `[D-011]`).
- **FR-53** `[Ph-1]` — **LLM quality review**. **Trigger gating**: fires only when `review_required = true` on the DeliveryItem row at evaluation time. `review_required` is a TPM-editable per-item column per FR-7 I5 + FR-56 column model — defaults `true` for `TEST_TECH_WAIVER_REPORT`, `false` for `COMPLIANCE_CERTIFICATION_RELEASE_NOTES` / `Default` / `Confirmation`. **Confirmation is non-editable per FR-58** (`review_required = false` always — no document path). For other item_types, TPM may flip the value via FR-14 (with retroactive enqueue for docs already received — see lifecycle below). **Firing condition**: when a document **lands on the FR-86 `classified` NSD path** AND `doc_type ∈ {test_report, tech_report, waiver}` AND `review_required = true` for the linked DeliveryItem — i.e., the full FR-85 sequential pipeline has completed with aligned `(item_type, doc_type)` — `TriggerLLMReview` (FR-28 / FR-29) fires. For documents on `staged_not_classified`, `staged_not_revision`, or `unrouted` paths, LLM review does NOT fire until TPM resolves the document to `classified` via FR-87. **Ingest channels**: email (FR-52), NSD (FR-55), PLM poll (FR-26), or HILDA dashboard upload (FR-62 `[Ph-2]`). **Parallel firing with FR-16 parser**: when a document lands `classified`, the rule engine fires BOTH `TriggerParser` (FR-16; for `doc_type = test_report` regardless of `review_required`) AND `TriggerLLMReview` (this FR; for `doc_type ∈ {test_report, tech_report, waiver}` AND `review_required = true`) — parallel execution; no dependency between them; results persist independently to `DocumentIndexRow.parser_result` and `DocumentIndexRow.llm_review_findings`. **Review action**: the runtime LLM performs an initial quality review against the per-customer checklist generated by the Test Report Profiler `[D-011]`. **Persistence**: writes findings to **`DocumentIndexRow.llm_review_findings`** keyed by `file_hash` per `[D-055]` file-centric storage model + FR-57; the row is uniquely identifiable via the secondary partial unique index `(milestone_id, doc_id_slug, rev_number)` per `[D-072]`. Updates `review_status` on the work-item row in `Deliverables_<customer_id>` via `[D-064]` REST writeback. Surfaces findings on the PM dashboard for TPM review **via HILDA's rendered document section per FR-60** (HILDA-rendered HTML at `hilda-proxy.corp/docs/<customer_id>/<item_id>` per `[D-074]` Variant A — NOT SP UI). **Per-revision firing**: LLM review fires once per revision row (**Ph-1**: always `rev1` per FR-7 single-revision fork — one review fire per document; **Ph-2**: each `revN` triggers its own LLM review, writing its own `llm_review_findings` to the corresponding `DocumentIndexRow`; prior revisions' findings preserved unchanged). **Review failure handling**: if the LLM call fails (timeout, gateway error, low-confidence output), HILDA retries per NFR-10 (transient: exponential backoff base 30 s, max 30 min, max 5 retries; non-recoverable: raise registered error code immediately). On persistent failure, `review_status` is written as `failed` (4th enum value added 2026-06-16) + error code (`LLM-W001` LLM gateway timeout / `LLM-W002` low-confidence / etc.); logged to `HildaOpsAlert` per FR-75; FR-16 parser still fires independently when `doc_type = test_report` (parallel execution above — degraded-mode review still possible from parser output). **Post-resolution review fire** (FR-87 cascade): when a doc was `unresolved` at ingest and TPM resolves to a reviewable doc_type ({test_report, tech_report, waiver}) via FR-87 step (B), `TriggerLLMReview` fires after the doc reaches the `classified` NSD path per FR-87's async-tail (gated on `review_required = true`). Same applies when TPM reassigns a doc from `Default` work-item to a real work-item via FR-83 + the new work-item has `review_required = true` AND doc_type is reviewable. **Execution location**: LLM review runs as a `workflow_engine` Celery task in `hilda-worker` per `[D-022]`. LLM backend selection per `[D-052]` (tri-backend: Ollama A4000 / vLLM DGX / corp LLM); per-customer checklists loaded from `customizations/test_report_parsers/<customer_id>.py` (generated by Test Report Profiler `[D-011]`). **`review_status` lifecycle** (per-item column; HILDA-managed; 4-value Choice enum `pending / complete / not_required / failed`; xlsx `review_status` row Notes carry the full lifecycle): default `pending` at row creation when `review_required = true`. Transitions: (a) `review_required` toggles `true → false` via FR-14 → HILDA writes `review_status = not_required`; (b) `review_required` flips `false → true` via FR-14 with existing received docs → HILDA retroactively enqueues FR-53 for all received docs + writes `review_status = pending`; (c) all received-doc LLM reviews complete successfully → HILDA writes `review_status = complete`; (d) any review fails persistently → HILDA writes `review_status = failed`; (e) new doc arrives mid-flow on an item already `complete` → HILDA writes `review_status = pending` for the new review cycle. **Per-item aggregation**: `review_status` reflects the aggregate state across ALL received docs on the item. `pending` = at least one received doc has review not yet complete; `complete` = ALL received docs have review complete (each doc's `DocumentIndexRow.llm_review_findings` is non-null) — required to satisfy FR-7 `OwnerClosed` guard condition (2) for items passing through `OwnerClosed`; `not_required` = `review_required = false` on the item; `failed` = at least one received doc has review persistently failed. **Interaction with `OwnerClosed` guard condition (2)**: when `review_required = false`, FR-53 does not fire — `review_status` is set to `not_required`, `llm_review_findings` remains null. **For items that pass through `OwnerClosed`** (standard fork per FR-7), this means guard condition (2) is vacuously satisfied. **For `doc_count = 0` items** (Confirmation OR non-Confirmation with `doc_count = 0`), `OwnerClosed` is skipped entirely per FR-7 I8 unified skip rule — guard condition (2) does not apply (the state machine bypasses `OwnerClosed` directly to `UnderPMReview`). **Ph-1 scope**: initial review, findings persistence to document index, and dashboard display only — PM response tracking, owner revision communication, and multi-version re-review are deferred to `[Ph-2]` (DEF-2 remainder, FR-17).
- **FR-17** `[Ph-2]` — **Multi-revision handling**. When a document arrives for a DeliveryItem and `[D-039]` revision-determination (FR-52 stage 3) identifies it as a REVISION (not new, not duplicate, not ambiguous), the system stores the revision at `<item_path_id>/<doc_type_slug>/<doc_id_slug>/revN/<original_filename>` under `\\NSD1\internal\<carrier>\<device>\<milestone>\<tg_path_id>\` per FR-13 (where `<carrier>` = `customer_id`, `<device>` = `device_id` (= `model` SP column), `<milestone>` = `milestone_id` (= `minorMilestone` SP column) — all sourced from template.yaml per FR-13; N = next revision number for the matched `doc_id_slug`). **FR-7 Ph-2 fork rule cross-reference**: FR-17 implements the Ph-2 multi-revision fork per FR-7 — when multiple revisions exist for any `doc_id_slug` at owner-done time, `TriggerVersionSelection` (FR-28) fires for FR-66 owner final-revision selection workflow. Once `is_final = true` is written on the selected revision's `DocumentIndexRow` via FR-66 (and `is_final = false` on all others for that `doc_id_slug` — atomic write), the item advances `OwnerClosed → UnderPMReview` per FR-7 Ph-2 fork. **Per-revision actions** (fire after revision is written to NSD `revN/`): **(1) Storage** — new revision's `DocumentIndexRow` keyed by its own `file_hash` per `[D-055]` file-centric storage model — prior revisions' rows (keyed by their own `file_hash` values) are preserved unchanged. Per-revision rows are uniquely identifiable via the secondary partial unique index `(milestone_id, doc_id_slug, rev_number)` per `[D-072]`. **(2) Parser + LLM review fire** — per FR-16 per-revision firing rule (each `revN` triggers its own `TriggerParser` fire) + FR-53 per-revision firing rule (each `revN` triggers its own `TriggerLLMReview` fire when `review_required = true`) — parser + review run in parallel on the new revision per FR-16 / FR-53 parallel-firing semantic; results persist independently to the new revision's `DocumentIndexRow.parser_result` and `llm_review_findings` respectively. **(3) SP write-back** — updates the work-item row in `Deliverables_<customer_id>` via `[D-064]` REST writeback (`review_status` per FR-53 lifecycle; `actual_item_info` already set from first document arrival per FR-57 — does not change). **(4) PLM upload deferred** per FR-13 phase-conditional rule + `[D-040]` — only the `is_final = true` revision is uploaded post-dispatch via FR-68 sync verification; intermediate revisions are never uploaded to PLM (Email/NSD sources upload individual files only — never zips); NSD classified path remains source of truth for in-progress revisions. **Version tracking operates on individual files only** — zip-level versioning is irrelevant; `[D-039]` + `[D-048]` apply per individual file; if `Q3_reports_v1.zip` and `Q3_reports_v2.zip` arrive sequentially, each extracted file is processed independently through `[D-039]` 4 steps per FR-52; zip archives accumulate in `_zip_audit/` regardless of version sequence. **Revision ingest sources** (all 4 sources run the FR-85 sequential pipeline — `[D-039]` is part of stage 3, NOT a separate "revision flow"): **(a) Email (FR-12 / FR-52)** — document arrives as email attachment; FR-85 stage 1 classifies doc_type; FR-52 stage 2 routes to work-item; `[D-039]` stage 3 determines new vs revision per FR-52's authoritative Step 0–3 description (Ph-2 effective scope = Steps 0–3); revision result drives storage path + per-revision actions above. **(b) NSD (FR-55)** — owner drops the revised document into the milestone's NSD ingress folder `\\NSD_X\<carrier>\<device>\<milestone>\<Folder-N>\` per FR-13 (flat per-TG folder structure — no per-item folders; same drop zone as initial submission per FR-55). HILDA detects the new file via FR-55 polling; same FR-85 sequential pipeline + `[D-039]` Step 0–3 path as (a). **(c) CorporatePLM (FR-26)** — during each PLM poll cycle, HILDA downloads every document attached to the PLM issue and applies the FR-85 sequential pipeline + `[D-039]` Step 0–3 path; `plm_attachment_id` already known from the poll; no PLM re-upload (per FR-13). **(d) HILDA dashboard upload (FR-62)** — TPM uploads a revision via the HILDA-rendered upload form at `hilda-proxy.corp/upload/<customer_id>/<item_id>` per `[D-074]` Variant A; the form's New/Revision radio + revision dropdown lets TPM select the existing `doc_id_slug` being revised — **bypasses `[D-039]` classification entirely per FR-62** (no hash dedup, no LLM CLASSIFY_DOC, no `_staged_revision/` holding); HILDA writes the file directly to `<item_path_id>/<doc_type_slug>/<doc_id_slug>/revN/` where N = (current max `rev_number` for the selected `doc_id_slug`) + 1; per-revision actions (1)–(4) above fire. **`_staged_revision/` ambiguous holding** (per FR-52 `[D-039]` Step 3 + FR-13 area (3)): when `[D-039]` Step 2 LLM is ambiguous (sources (a)/(b)/(c)), the document is held in `<item_path_id>/<doc_type_slug>/_staged_revision/<original_filename>` per FR-13 area (3); HILDA sets `manual_triage_required = true` on the row per FR-12 I4 lock; PM dashboard query filters on this column to surface the triage queue. TPM resolves via FR-87 step (C) — on resolution, HILDA writes the file to `<item_path_id>/<doc_type_slug>/<doc_id_slug>/revN/` and per-revision actions (1)–(4) fire.
- **FR-46** `[Ph-1]` — **Test report final|interim classification rule**. **Rule**: a test report is classified `final` iff every test case is in `{passed, non-applicable, waived}` AND every `failed` test case carries a `waiver_ref` (which reclassifies it as `waived` for this rule's logic — see waiver-reclassification scope below); otherwise the report is `interim`. (Note: `not-started` test cases — equivalent to "no test result available" for that test case — are NOT in the qualifying set, so any `not-started` test case forces `interim`.) **Trigger timing**: FR-46 fires immediately after FR-16 parser produces `(test_case_id, status ∈ {passed, failed, non-applicable, waived, not-started}, [waiver_ref])` tuples for a `test_report` document (synchronous; sub-second; part of FR-16's `TriggerParser` task). Operates on the parser output tuples — does not re-read the source document. **Persistence**: the `final | interim` classification is written into `DocumentIndexRow.parser_result.classification` (top-level field of the `parser_result` Pydantic model alongside the per-test-case status grid) per `[D-055]` file-centric storage model — keyed by the document's `file_hash`. **Per-revision classification**: each revision's parser output is classified independently — **Ph-1**: always `rev1` per FR-7 single-revision fork (one classification per document); **Ph-2**: each `revN` produces its own `parser_result.classification` written to its own `DocumentIndexRow`; prior revisions' classifications preserved unchanged. **Waiver reclassification scope**: the rule **logically** treats `(status = failed, waiver_ref = non-null)` as `waived` for the FR-46 final|interim check. The underlying parser tuple is **not mutated** — `parser_result.test_cases[i]` retains `status = failed` + `waiver_ref = X` as the source-of-truth. Downstream consumers that filter on `status = failed` (e.g., FR-47 surfacing for resolution-path determination) must additionally check `waiver_ref` to exclude waived failures from their surface. **OwnerClosed guard (2) interaction (per FR-7)**: `interim` classification does NOT block `OwnerClosed` — per FR-7 guard condition (2), only the *presence* of `parser_result` (and `llm_review_findings` per FR-53) is required, not its content. `interim` reports proceed through the normal owner-done flow to `UnderPMReview` for TPM review; failed test cases without `waiver_ref` are surfaced for resolution-path determination per FR-47 `[Ph-2]` — but this happens at `UnderPMReview` stage, not as a blocker on the `OwnerClosed` transition. **Downstream consumer FR-47** `[Ph-2]`: for `interim` reports, FR-47 surfaces each failed test case **without** a `waiver_ref` in **HILDA's rendered document section per `[D-074]` Variant A** (NOT SP UI — the FR-47 resolution-path picker lives in HILDA-rendered HTML at `hilda-proxy.corp/docs/<customer_id>/<item_id>` per FR-60; SP UI surfaces only the View Documents link-out anchor per FR-56 (c)). PM selects `resolution_path ∈ {fix_pre_launch, tech_report, waiver}` per FR-47. `final` reports trigger no FR-47 action (no unresolved failures). `failed` test cases WITH `waiver_ref` are treated as `waived` per the reclassification rule above and do not appear in FR-47's surface. **Ph-2 multi-revision + `is_final = true`**: per FR-7 Ph-2 fork, TPM selects the final revision via FR-66 → `is_final = true` is set on the selected revision's `DocumentIndexRow`. The **selected revision's `parser_result.classification`** drives FR-47 surfacing — `interim` → resolution-path determination; `final` → no action. Non-`is_final` revisions' classifications are preserved (for audit) but don't drive FR-47. **Parser-fail handling**: if FR-16 parser produces `parser_result.status = parser_failed` (e.g., `TST-W001` malformed structure, `TST-W002` profile mismatch), FR-46 does NOT produce a `final | interim` classification — `parser_result.classification` is set to `not_classified` (3rd enum value added 2026-06-16). TPM triages via the FR-60 HILDA-rendered document section displaying the parser failure; resolution path is manual (TPM may use FR-14 to override `delivery_state` per the Confirm Carrier Acceptance flow if carrier accepts despite parser failure, or wait for owner-revised document). **Internal vs carrier acceptance**: FR-46 `final | interim` is HILDA's **internal** classification of report completeness per the rule above — it does NOT signal carrier acceptance. Carrier acceptance is a separate downstream event (FR-7 path (i) — TPM-Confirm-Carrier-Acceptance per SP UI engineer's Confirm Carrier Acceptance button when customer-side acceptance is received out-of-band). A `final` report may still be rejected by carrier; an `interim` report may eventually achieve carrier acceptance after FR-47 resolution paths complete. **Anchors**: `[D-011]` (Test Report Document Profiler — generates the per-customer parser that produces the test-case tuples this rule operates on); FR-16 (the parser firing context that invokes this rule).
- **FR-47** `[Ph-2]` — For every `failed` test case without a `waiver_ref` in a test report, the system surfaces the test case on the PM dashboard for resolution-path determination; the PM selects a `resolution_path` from the formal enum `resolution_path ∈ {fix_pre_launch, tech_report, waiver}` for each failed test case (the `tech_report` path covers both network-behavior and by-design rationale sub-cases described in `HILDA_Design.md` §5 Stage 4 — no separate enum value); `[Ph-2]` this selection is a PM annotation only: HILDA records the PM's chosen path against the `(delivery_item_id, test_case_id)` in the document index but takes no automated downstream action in Ph-2; waivers are owner-created — HILDA tracks the `waiver_ref` once supplied by the owner and does not auto-create Waiver DeliveryItems; automated follow-up per resolution path (owner notification, Tech Report DeliveryItem creation, waiver_ref monitoring) is deferred to Ph-3 (DEF-17).

### SharePoint UI

- **FR-56** `[Ph-1]` — The SharePoint UI presents a per-milestone view (classic web part) listing all DeliveryItem rows for that milestone — SP UI reads `Deliverables_<customer_id>` with filter `model = <device_id> AND minorMilestone = <milestone_id>` per Ph-1 flat-table model + FR-5 (one row per work-item; milestone-level columns duplicated across all rows in the milestone per `[D-077]`). Rows grouped by `tg_name` (TG header values rendered from the denormalized TG columns on the first delivery-item row in each group per `[D-051]` impl note 2026-06-12 — no separate TGGroups list lookup). **Per-row column model** (locked 2026-06-12 — split into two buckets: visible columns + editability discipline):

  **(a) Mandatory display in list-view rows** — visible always (per-row scan); union of "key scan fields" + "TPM-inline-editable fields" (the editable fields MUST be visible since TPM edits them inline; they are not optional):
  - `item_no` (read-only; YAML-loaded; immutable per item lifetime per template_schema/MODULE.md Invariant 2026-06-12)
  - `item_name` (read-only; YAML-loaded)
  - `item_type` (read-only; YAML-loaded; drives View Documents link-out visibility per FR-58)
