Browser sessions are **persistent across dispatches within a worker** (login once, dispatch many files) and **recycled after N files** (default 50; configurable per-carrier) to prevent memory leaks; recycling does NOT block in-flight dispatches — new dispatches wait for the new session to log in (~5–10s). **`NotImplementedError` capability-flag handling**: per-carrier adapters declare capability flags in `<carrier_slug>_adapter_config.yaml` `capabilities: {submitItem: bool, getStatus: bool, postComment: bool, uploadAttachment: bool}`. **Caller behavior** for `NotImplementedError`: **(a) `uploadAttachment` MUST be implemented** (no Ph-1 carrier can lack this — FR-18 dispatch core); HILDA refuses to onboard a carrier whose adapter declares `uploadAttachment: false` (config validation at HILDA startup); validation failure → ops alert, carrier not loaded. **(b) `getStatus = false`** — FR-68 PLM-Carrier hash verification SKIPS the carrier-side check for this carrier (PLM-NSD comparison still runs; logged to `HildaOpsAlert` as `CSA-W001` informational so ops know that carrier doesn't support post-dispatch verification). **(c) `postComment = false`** — `[D-044]` Ph-2+ PM commentary flow surfaces "commentary not supported for this carrier" to TPM in PM dashboard; no error. **(d) `submitItem = false`** — Ph-1 default; no-op (file upload IS submission for Google Drive). Capability flag values are static per carrier (not runtime-discovered); changes require adapter config redeploy + HILDA restart. **Hash semantics for FR-68 verification** (cross-ref FR-68): HILDA's pre-upload hash (`DocumentIndexRow.file_hash` per `[D-039]` Step 0) is the canonical hash; the adapter logs it as the "dispatched-hash" in `CommunicationLog` per FR-18. **Post-upload carrier-side hash is NOT computed by FR-19 adapters** in Ph-1 — browser-automation can't reliably download-and-rehash without massive overhead (the very flow that would verify integrity would also re-trigger the carrier's audit logs as a download event). FR-68 PLM-Carrier verification therefore compares **PLM-uploaded hash** (read via `IssueTracker.getAttachmentHash` or equivalent) to **HILDA's pre-upload hash** (which was also dispatched to carrier) — proving HILDA dispatched IDENTICAL bytes to both PLM and carrier, but NOT proving the carrier-side file arrived intact (a stronger guarantee deferred to Ph-3+ if carrier exposes a server-side hash API). FR-19 just supplies the pre-upload hash to the comparison; FR-68 owns the verification scope. **Reference base class in `core/`** — `core/src/customer_adapter/google_drive_base.py` provides a `GoogleDriveBaseAdapter` class with the shared selenium machinery: Chromium driver lifecycle, headless mode configuration, common UI navigation patterns, selector-version loading from per-carrier config, retry/backoff on stale-element exceptions, hash computation (pre-upload only per above) for FR-68 PLM-Carrier sync, structured `CarrierAdapterError` raising helpers, worker-pool integration, and standard `getStatus` / `postComment` skeletons (default raises `NotImplementedError`; subclass overrides if supported). Per-carrier adapter implementations live at `customizations/customer_adapter/<carrier_slug>_adapter.py` + `customizations/customer_adapter/<carrier_slug>_adapter_config.yaml` — each subclasses `GoogleDriveBaseAdapter` and provides only carrier-specific overrides: selectors (versioned in the `_adapter_config.yaml`), folder path semantics, authentication flow (if SAML/SSO varies), session storage strategy, capability flags (which Protocol methods are implemented vs. raise `NotImplementedError`), `max_concurrent_sessions` override. The base class contains **no proprietary identifiers** — it is generic Google Drive browser-automation infrastructure. The `CustomerAdapter` Protocol surface lives at `core/src/customer_adapter/protocol.py`; **no per-carrier concrete implementations in `core/`** — only the generic `GoogleDriveBaseAdapter` (mirrors the IssueTracker pattern where `core/src/issue_tracker/jira_adapter.py` is a public reference and per-carrier proprietary adapters live in `customizations/`; anchors `[D-054]`).
- **FR-20** `[Ph-1]` — **UPDATED 2026-06-17 — alignment with FR-19 `CSA-E001` lock + FR-18 per-item-atomicity lock; pre-flight credential check via new `CSA-E000` code; manual recovery path via FR-63 multi-round semantic**. Submission of an item is **not retried by HILDA** when the PM's credential for the target customer system is missing or expired. The item remains in `ReadyForSubmission` state per FR-18 per-item-atomicity lock 2026-06-17 — successfully-dispatched siblings advance to `SubmittedToCustomer` independently; only the credential-failing item is held back. A **`HildaOpsAlert` is logged per FR-75** with the PM identifier + carrier_slug; **credential material is NEVER logged per NFR-2 + FR-19 lock**; the alert surfaces in TPM dashboard for ops intervention. **Failure mode discrimination** (distinguishes the two paths into this state): **(a) Pre-flight credential check at dispatch coordinator (`CSA-E000` — `retryable = false`)**: before invoking the customer adapter for an item, HILDA's dispatch coordinator verifies the existence of `customizations/credentials/<assigned_pm_id>/<carrier_slug>.env.sops` (the encrypted credential file per `[D-038]`); if missing, the coordinator fails the item with `CSA-E000` BEFORE invoking the adapter; `HildaOpsAlert` message: `"PM <pm_id> has no credentials for <carrier_slug> — ops must configure via [D-038] sops-encrypted env file at customizations/credentials/<assigned_pm_id>/<carrier_slug>.env.sops"`. **(b) Adapter-session credential expiry (`CSA-E001` per FR-19 — `retryable = false`)**: file exists but credentials no longer valid (session cookie expired, OAuth refresh failed, SAML re-auth required); adapter raises during the session; `HildaOpsAlert` message: `"PM credentials expired for <carrier_slug>; ops must re-encrypt sops env file"`. Both codes lead to the same item state (`ReadyForSubmission`) and require ops intervention; the codes differentiate root cause for ops triage. **Recovery path after credential restoration**: HILDA does NOT auto-resume blocked items — there is no credential-watcher background task. After ops re-encrypts the sops env file (per `[D-038]`) and HILDA's adapter pool refreshes (next dispatch attempt picks up the new credentials), the TPM (or PM) must manually re-click **FR-63 Submit to Carrier** in the SP milestone view to retry the blocked items. Per FR-63 multi-round semantic, only items still in `ReadyForSubmission` will be dispatched; items that succeeded in the prior round are skipped (already in `SubmittedToCustomer`). The `HildaOpsAlert` raised on the credential failure remains in the TPM dashboard until manually acknowledged after successful resubmission. Cross-refs: FR-19 (`CSA-E000` / `CSA-E001` codes + adapter contract) + FR-51 (PM-credentialed calls) + `[D-038]` (sops-encrypted env files) + FR-75 (`HildaOpsAlert` channel) + NFR-2 (no credential material in logs) + FR-63 (manual re-trigger via Submit click) + FR-18 (per-item-atomicity).

### Customer follow-up & closure (Stage 6)

- ~~**FR-21**~~ — *(deferred 2026-05-19 → DEF-19: automated customer feedback capture from carrier tracking system and email — TPM manually monitors the carrier portal; revised documents are uploaded via FR-62 and resubmitted via FR-63 — revisit: Ph-3)*
- ~~**FR-22**~~ — *(deferred 2026-05-19 → DEF-20: automated `Closed` transition requiring carrier-acceptance signal detection + PM confirmation gate — TPM manually sets items to `Closed` via FR-14 per-item override or FR-64 bulk Close All Items action — revisit: Ph-3)*

### Communication adapters — Email Service

- **FR-23** `[Ph-1]` — **UPDATED 2026-06-17 — outbound mailbox + signature contract per `[D-038]`; bounce handling per FR-9/FR-12/FR-75; inbound routing discrimination per FR-84/FR-12/`[D-047]`; tier transition triggers + IMAP IDLE RFC ceremony; cross-FR cadence consistency; per-environment mailbox isolation**. Email Service owns a dedicated mailbox and emits outbound on behalf of PMs with the PM's name in the signature and a stable From address. **Outbound mailbox identity + signature contract**: outbound emails are sent from HILDA's **dedicated team mailbox** (`hilda-team@corp.com` or equivalent — exact address configured per deployment in `customizations/email_service/<env>.yaml`); the From header carries this stable address (NOT the PM's personal email — Email Service does NOT impersonate PMs at the SMTP envelope level). **Signature**: outbound emails include a signature block stating the PM's name + role + a polite note that replies go to the HILDA team mailbox (which routes back to the PM via `[D-044]` Ph-2+ inbound-reply routing). **SMTP credentials**: stored in `customizations/credentials/hilda-team-mailbox.env.sops` (sops-encrypted per `[D-038]`); decrypted at Email Service startup; NEVER logged per NFR-2 (parallel to FR-19 PM-credential discipline). Outbound failures on SMTP-auth surface as `EML-E001` (SMTP credential expired — `retryable = false`) logged to `HildaOpsAlert` per FR-75. **Per-environment mailbox isolation**: each HILDA environment (dev / staging / production) uses a DISTINCT dedicated mailbox to prevent cross-environment SP-alert / owner-reply routing pollution — e.g., `hilda-team-dev@corp.com` / `hilda-team-staging@corp.com` / `hilda-team@corp.com`. Mailbox address + SMTP credentials are env-scoped per `customizations/email_service/<env>.yaml` + `customizations/credentials/hilda-team-mailbox-<env>.env.sops`. SP-alert subject pattern stays the same across environments (Email Service routes based on the receiving mailbox, not the subject's environment prefix); SP UI engineer's per-environment SP list config writes alerts to the env-matching mailbox. **Bounce handling** (cascade with FR-9 owner email preference + FR-12 owner-reply parser + FR-75): Email Service monitors the dedicated mailbox for **DSN (Delivery Status Notification) bounces** on outbound. On hard-bounce: **(a) `owner_corp_usa_email` bounce** — Email Service logs `EML-W001` to `CommunicationLog` (`action_type = bounce_primary_email`) + retries the same outreach via `owner_corp_email` per FR-9 fallback (if set); items where only the primary email bounces continue receiving outreach via the secondary email. **(b) BOTH emails bounce OR `owner_corp_email` is null and primary bounced** — Email Service logs `EML-E002` to `HildaOpsAlert` per FR-75 with the item + bounce reason + owner identifier; **flips `manual_triage_required = true`** on the item per FR-12 parallel (TPM must fix owner email via FR-3 / FR-88 owner reassignment Ph-2 OR manually contact owner out-of-band). Outbound retries for that owner pause until `manual_triage_required` is cleared. **Soft-bounce** (4xx SMTP code — temporary): retry per NFR-10 backoff; no immediate triage flag. **Inbound consumption is three-tier** (anchors `[D-047]`): **(1) IMAP IDLE on Exchange (primary, ~1–2 s latency)** — long-lived connection delivers push semantics server-side; **IMAP IDLE RFC ceremony**: IDLE connection re-issues `DONE` + new `IDLE` every 25 minutes (RFC 2177 max-idle ~29 minutes; 25-min margin avoids server-side disconnect race); this is internal Tier-1 plumbing, not a tier transition. **(2) Short-interval polling (5–10 s fallback)** — used when Exchange admin disables IDLE; **(3) Deadline-tiered `polling_schedule` AutomationRule (third-tier fallback)** — same schedule used by FR-26 (PLM) and FR-55 (NSD); `{days_before_deadline, interval_minutes}` breakpoints evaluated against `days_to_deadline` across all active items in the milestone; effective interval is the shortest tier applicable across any active item in the milestone at that moment; example: baseline 60 min, ≤ 3 days → 15 min, ≤ 1 day → 5 min. **Cross-FR cadence consistency**: this deadline-tiered `polling_schedule` definition is shared across FR-23 (Email Tier 3) + FR-25 (CustomerJIRA + CorporatePLM polling) + FR-26 (PLM polling) + FR-55 (NSD polling); the breakpoint structure + shortest-tier-applicable semantic is canonical here — FR-25 / FR-26 / FR-55 reference this contract. If a future review modifies the shape, all four FRs must be updated together. **Tier transition triggers** (Email Service's `tier_manager` decides per-mailbox tier in real time): **Tier 1 → Tier 2 fallback fires when**: (a) IDLE command returns server error (`BAD` or `NO` response); (b) network drops mid-IDLE 3 consecutive times within 10 min (Exchange unreachable); (c) admin-disabled-IDLE check (HILDA periodically probes IDLE capability via `CAPABILITY` command every 1 hour); first failure-to-detect drops to Tier 2. **Tier 2 → Tier 3 fallback fires when**: (d) short-poll IMAP login fails 3 consecutive times (mailbox auth broken); (e) IMAP server returns persistent 5xx errors > 5 min. **Tier 1 / Tier 2 re-attempt**: Email Service re-attempts the higher tier every 1 hour (configurable) — failure conditions are typically transient; auto-promote back to Tier 1 on successful IDLE re-establishment. Each transition logs `EML-W003` (downgrade) or `EML-W004` (upgrade) to `CommunicationLog` for ops visibility. The IMAP IDLE / short-poll path is the primary mechanism for **both** SP alert emails (per FR-84) and owner-reply emails (per FR-12); the deadline-tiered cadence is the third-tier fallback used only when both IDLE and short-poll are unavailable (owner-reply emails where minutes-of-latency is acceptable). **Inbound routing discrimination** (in `email_service.inbound_dispatcher`): every inbound email is classified into one of three streams: **(a) SP-alert stream** — subject matches `Alert_Deliverables_<customer_id> - <ItemTitle>` per FR-84 + the configured SP-alert sender domain; routed to `email_service.sp_alert_parser` for 5-field key extraction per FR-84. **(b) Owner-reply stream** — `In-Reply-To` header references a HILDA outbound `Message-ID` (HILDA tracks outbound `Message-ID` → item mapping in storage per `[D-047]`); routed to `email_service.owner_reply_parser` for FR-12 inbound parsing. **(c) Unclassified stream** — neither pattern matches; logged to `CommunicationLog` with `action_type = inbound_unclassified` + raw headers; surfaced as informational `EML-W002` to TPM dashboard for periodic review (likely spam, ops mail, or misdirected human reply); NOT processed by the state machine. The three streams are mutually exclusive — `In-Reply-To` check fires BEFORE subject-pattern match (an owner reply CC-ing the SP-alerts mailbox is routed to owner-reply, not SP-alert). Per-stream rate limits prevent any single stream from starving the others under burst conditions.
- **FR-24** `[Ph-1]` — **UPDATED 2026-06-17 — tag structure + format locked (`[HILDA:<customer_id>/<device_id>/<milestone_id>/<item_id>/<pm_id>]`); customer_id added for FR-5 row resolution; routing precedence with FR-23 stream-(b) (In-Reply-To primary; tag fallback); FR-12 sender mismatch anomaly handling; HILDA-bypass reply mitigation; reply-all deduplication**. **Outbound subject tag format**: HILDA prepends a bracketed reference tag to outbound email subjects: `[HILDA:<customer_id>/<device_id>/<milestone_id>/<item_id>/<pm_id>]` — pipe-style hierarchical path with `HILDA:` prefix for unambiguous detection. Same 4 routing fields as FR-84's SP-alert routing key (`customer_id, device_id (=Model), milestone_id (=MinorMilestone), item_id (=ItemNumber)`) plus `pm_id` for attribution — note: ProjectID per FR-84 is captured implicitly via `(customer_id, device_id)` resolution and NOT included here to keep subject compact. **Tag position**: ALWAYS at the start of subject (before any PM-customizable subject text); on inbound replies, HILDA strips any `Re:` / `Fwd:` / `RE:` / `FW:` prefix before tag extraction (regex: `^(Re:|Fwd:|RE:|FW:)*\s*\[HILDA:...\]`). The `item_id` here is the SP system `ID` column per FR-5 (per-list unique), routed via `(customer_id, item_id)` to the canonical row. Tag is generated by Email Service at outbound-send time and stored against the item's outbound `Message-ID` in storage per `[D-047]` (enables `In-Reply-To` fallback per FR-23 stream-(b) routing). **Outbound senders** (which FR triggers the outbound send): FR-8 initial outreach, FR-10 reminder cadence, FR-11 escalation, FR-65 TPM ad-hoc Send Reminder — all use the FR-9 body format and the FR-24 subject tag here; transport per FR-23 outbound. **Routing precedence with FR-23 stream-(b)** (cascade with FR-23 inbound routing discrimination lock 2026-06-17): FR-23 owner-reply stream-(b) uses the `In-Reply-To` header as the **primary** routing key (HILDA-tracked `Message-ID` → item mapping in storage per `[D-047]`). The FR-24 subject tag operates at TWO layers as **secondary fallback**: **(Layer 1 — stream classification)** when `In-Reply-To` is missing or doesn't match any tracked outbound `Message-ID`, the presence of a valid `[HILDA:...]` subject tag still classifies the email as stream-(b) owner-reply (otherwise it would fall through to stream-(c) unclassified). **(Layer 2 — item routing)** when classified as stream-(b) but `In-Reply-To` doesn't resolve to a tracked Message-ID, the 4 routing fields inside the tag identify the canonical row directly. **Both header + tag present and CONSISTENT**: `In-Reply-To` wins (more reliable than user-editable subject); subject tag verifies. **Both present and INCONSISTENT** (different items resolved by header vs tag) — Email Service logs `EML-W005` (header-tag mismatch) to `CommunicationLog` + `HildaOpsAlert` and routes via `In-Reply-To` (trusts header); also flips `manual_triage_required = true` on the resolved item per FR-12 anomaly handling. When neither resolves → routed to FR-23 stream-(c) unclassified per FR-23 lock. **Sender attribution + anomaly detection** (per FR-12 lenient sender match): Email Service captures the inbound `From` header alongside the routed item; FR-12's owner-reply parser performs lenient comparison against the item's `owner_corp_usa_email` + `owner_corp_email` (per FR-9 + `[D-080]`). **Match outcomes**: **(a) Exact match** on either email — sender is the expected owner; parser processes normally; `last_owner_response_at` set per FR-15-extended. **(b) Lenient match** (case-insensitive; trailing-dot normalization; corp-domain-equivalence) — same as (a) with informational `EML-W006` logged (audit trail of normalization). **(c) Mismatch** — sender is NOT the expected owner (possible forwarding, delegate-send, owner change without HILDA awareness, or spoofing). Per FR-12 lock: `manual_triage_required = true` is flipped on the item; `EML-W007` logged to `CommunicationLog` with the actual sender + expected owner emails; TPM dashboard surfaces for triage. Parser STILL processes the email content (the body may contain relevant owner-delegate information) but the state-machine actions are gated on TPM triage clearance. **Forwarded / delegate-sent emails are NOT auto-trusted as owner responses** — TPM must explicitly confirm the delegation by clearing `manual_triage_required` after reading the original email thread. **Replies bypassing HILDA**: HILDA only routes inbound emails received at its dedicated mailbox (per FR-23 mailbox isolation). If an owner replies DIRECTLY to the PM's personal email (bypassing HILDA's `Reply-To: hilda-team@corp.com`), HILDA does NOT see the reply — `last_owner_response_at` won't update; FR-10 escalation cadence continues firing reminders the owner has already answered. **TPM mitigation path**: PM forwards the reply to HILDA's dedicated mailbox preserving the original subject tag (or with `[HILDA:...]` tag manually prepended); HILDA processes per FR-24 tag-based routing as a fallback; `EML-W008` informational logged for audit. PMs are educated to include the team mailbox in CC on any direct owner correspondence to keep HILDA in the loop (per FR-23 signature contract — outbound emails carry a polite note: "Please reply to this thread; replies go to the HILDA team mailbox"). **Reply-All + duplicate-message handling**: when owner uses Reply-All and HILDA's dedicated mailbox is in multiple recipient fields (To + CC), HILDA may receive ONE message OR MULTIPLE copies (depends on SMTP server deduplication). Email Service deduplicates inbound by `Message-ID` header (canonical RFC-2822 identifier — unique per send); a second arrival with the same `Message-ID` is logged `EML-W009` informational and dropped. Per-item state-machine actions fire ONCE per `Message-ID`. CC'd third parties (PM, TG-coordinator, customer_jira) are captured in the message context but do NOT trigger separate routing — only the primary owner reply matters for FR-12 parsing.

### Communication adapters — IssueTracker (internal)

- **FR-25** `[Ph-1]` — **UPDATED 2026-06-17 — comprehensive PLM + JIRA cascade-drift lock pass; IssueTracker Protocol surface specified; multi-scope grouping per FR-57; upload phase split per FR-18; tracking-not-messaging per FR-65; adapter location discipline per NFR-2; per-role authentication; lifecycle; close-intent skip OwnerClosed per FR-7 I8 + FR-9; role-collapse template constraint; SP-side ticket-count rendering surface; per-person credentials for customer JIRA**. The `IssueTracker` Protocol per `[D-008]` serves two distinct roles in HILDA. **Both roles use the identical Protocol surface distinguished only by adapter and configuration; no internal-JIRA tracking in Ph-1/Ph-2.**

**IssueTracker Protocol surface** (`core/src/issue_tracker/protocol.py` per `[D-008]`): `{createIssue, getIssue, updateIssueStatus, postComment, uploadAttachment, listIssues, searchIssues, getAttachmentHash, downloadAttachment}`. **Method usage per role**: (a) Corp PLM: all methods used (full CRUD + attachment + search). (b) Customer JIRA: read-only — only `listIssues` / `searchIssues` / `getIssue` invoked; write methods raise `NotImplementedError` per per-customer capability config (parallel to FR-19 `NotImplementedError` handling). `getAttachmentHash` is invoked by FR-68 PLM-Carrier verification per FR-19 I7 hash semantics lock.

**(a) Corp PLM adapter** — document storage and source of truth for owner deliverables. **Issue identity** (per FR-52 I10 + FR-88 + FR-57 multi-scope lock): one PLM issue per `(device_id, milestone_id, owner_corp_id)` tuple — `owner_corp_id` is the stable corp identity (`owner_corp_usa_email` / `owner_corp_email` are the messaging-channel addresses per `[D-080]`; either can change without changing PLM identity). One owner's PLM issue covers ALL TGs they own within the same `(device_id, milestone_id)` per FR-57 multi-scope (TG is sub-tuple within owner). **Multi-scope PLM grouping** (per FR-57 multi-scope lock 2026-06-12): (i) one owner across multiple TGs within the same `(device_id, milestone_id)` → SHARED PLM issue (all TGs' items roll up to one `plm_id`); (ii) one owner across DIFFERENT milestones for same device → DISTINCT `plm_id` per milestone; (iii) one owner across DIFFERENT devices same customer → DISTINCT `plm_id` per `(device, milestone)`. **PLM scope clarification** (per FR-65 modality lock 2026-06-17): PLM is a **tracking/issue-management system** — HILDA opens, updates (comment/status), and uploads documents to PLM issues for owner-deliverable tracking; HILDA does NOT send owner messages via PLM (no owner notifications). Owner outreach is email-only per FR-9 lead-in lock. PLM ingest channel per FR-26 detects owner-uploaded documents in PLM issues (when owner uploads directly to PLM). **Outbound email handling for CorporatePLM items**: HILDA sends outbound email reminders for items with `tracking_modality includes CorporatePLM` per FR-8 / FR-10 / FR-65 — outbound modality is email-only per FR-65 lock; PLM is the tracking/storage channel, not the messaging channel. Owner replies come back via FR-23 stream-(b) per FR-24 tag routing — same as Email-modality items. **PLM upload trigger phase split** (cascade with FR-13 + FR-18 + FR-62 + FR-68): **`[Ph-1]`** docs uploaded to PLM at ingest time per FR-13 (immediate on classified-path write); **`[Ph-2]`** PLM upload DEFERRED per FR-62 — intermediate revisions never go to PLM; only `is_final = true` revision uploaded post-dispatch per FR-68 sync verification. PLM upload SKIPPED entirely when `no_customer_upload = true` per FR-80. **Adapter location discipline** (parallel to FR-19 customer adapter pattern; per NFR-2): the proprietary corp PLM adapter lives at `customizations/issue_tracker/<corp_plm_slug>_adapter.py` (generated by `[D-003]` API Spec Ingestor from the corp PLM's API spec) + `customizations/issue_tracker/<corp_plm_slug>_adapter_config.yaml`. `core/src/issue_tracker/` contains only: (i) the generic `IssueTracker` Protocol per `[D-008]` at `core/src/issue_tracker/protocol.py`; (ii) the public `jira_adapter.py` reference (used by FR-25 (b) customer JIRA path). **No proprietary identifiers in `core/`** per NFR-2. **Corp PLM authentication** (CORRECTED 2026-06-17 per architect lock — supersedes prior "HILDA service account + sops-encrypted credentials" framing): HILDA accesses corp PLM via the **corp PLM gateway** (pattern (d) per FR-51 lock 2026-06-17 — gateway-mediated IP-allowlist + specified human id). **HILDA does NOT store any credential** for corp PLM — the gateway handles authentication via corp infrastructure (IP-allowlist + fixed-key managed by corp PLM gateway admins; HILDA's host IP is allowlisted at the gateway). HILDA's `customizations/issue_tracker/<corp_plm_slug>_adapter_config.yaml` specifies only: (i) the gateway URL; (ii) the **corp human id** HILDA uses in its API calls (currently a HUMAN account — the human acting on behalf of HILDA; future could be promoted to a BOT account). The adapter calls gateway APIs and specifies the corp human id as a parameter; the gateway authenticates the call via IP + fixed-key (HILDA-transparent) and acts on behalf of the specified human id on the actual corp PLM server. **No sops-encrypted env file for corp PLM**; HILDA does NOT call `get_credential` per FR-51. **Gateway error handling**: on gateway HTTP error (auth/connectivity/IP-allowlist removal/gateway-side fixed-key rotation lapse), adapter raises `ITR-E002` (corp PLM gateway error — `retryable = false`; reframed 2026-06-17 from prior "credential expired" semantic — NOT a HILDA-side credential issue); logged to `HildaOpsAlert` per FR-75 with gateway URL + HTTP status code + corp human id (NO credential material involved per NFR-2); polling + uploads pause until ops coordinates with corp PLM gateway admins to restore connectivity. **Why gateway-mediated here vs per-person sops for FR-25(b) customer JIRA**: corp PLM is HILDA's INTERNAL system (auditable via corp PLM gateway audit trail + the specified human id at API call time); customer JIRA is EXTERNAL (carrier expects identifiable individual accounts for audit per their governance, with HILDA's account credentials provisioned at the carrier side — see (b) below). **Polling cadence**: HILDA polls this issue when `tracking_modality includes CorporatePLM` (FR-13, FR-26 — using the deadline-tiered `polling_schedule` shared across FR-23 / FR-25 / FR-26 / FR-55 per FR-23 cross-FR cadence consistency lock 2026-06-17). **PLM issue lifecycle** (FINALIZED 2026-06-17 per architect Option B lock — single closure trigger at FR-76 MilestoneStorageCleanup; supersedes both prior "HILDA does NOT close" framing (commit 85dcade) AND the OwnerClosed-trigger framing (commit 848b8a6)): **HILDA owns the corp PLM issue lifecycle end-to-end** — creation through closure — with closure deferred to milestone-level cleanup so PLM stays available throughout the PM workflow + late-arriving doc paths. Sequence: **(i) FR-8 Start Collection**: HILDA creates the PLM issue with constant initial metadata (`status = Open` + standard HILDA-generated summary/description per adapter config). **(ii) FR-13 / FR-18 / FR-62 ingest**: HILDA uploads documents to the issue per phase split (Ph-1 at ingest; Ph-2 deferred per FR-62/FR-68 — both write to `status = Open` PLM throughout the lifecycle). **(iii) FR-26 polling**: HILDA polls the issue for owner-uploaded documents using shared `polling_schedule`. Polling continues throughout the entire item lifecycle (Open → ... → Closed) — does NOT stop at `OwnerClosed`; this preserves PLM-side reachability for late-arriving docs via owner-side upload (e.g., owner uploads to PLM after `OwnerClosed` realizing they missed one). **(iv) Late-arriving doc handling during PM workflow** (key Option B benefit): docs may arrive via FR-62 PM/TPM upload (causes FR-62 revert from `SubmittedToCustomer` / `ReadyForSubmission` / `UnderPMReview` back to `UnderPMReview`) OR via FR-83 TPM reassignment from default work-item to a target item past `OwnerClosed`. In both cases, the PLM issue is still `status = Open`, so HILDA uploads the late-arriving doc to PLM naturally per FR-13 / FR-62 (no re-open required; no special-case state-handling). **(v) `[Ph-2]` post-dispatch is_final upload (FR-62 + FR-68)**: HILDA uploads the `is_final = true` revision to the still-Open PLM issue (no special status check needed; PLM remains Open through this point). **(vi) FR-76 MilestoneStorageCleanup — single closure trigger**: when MilestoneAllClosed fires (FR-28 — all gating items + default work-item Closed per FR-78), FR-76 cleanup performs a **PLM sweep** for the milestone: enumerate all PLM issues created by HILDA for `(device_id, milestone_id, *)` tuples (one per owner_corp_id in the milestone) and close each via `updateIssueStatus(plm_id, status='Closed', metadata={...constant set per adapter config...})`. Constant closure metadata is configured ONCE per-customer in `customizations/issue_tracker/<corp_plm_slug>_adapter_config.yaml` (typical fields: `resolution_status: "Completed"`, `closure_comment: "All deliverables received per HILDA workflow"`, `closed_by: hilda-team-plm-bot`); no per-issue resolution ceremony required. **Sweep ordering**: PLM closures fire BEFORE NSD cleanup (atomicity not critical — corp PLM and NSD are independent systems — but logical order preserves "close-then-delete" semantic). Sweep failures per PLM (network / auth issue) log `ITR-E004` to `HildaOpsAlert` per FR-75 (`retryable = true`) but do NOT block NSD cleanup (corp PLM closure can be retried out-of-band; NSD cleanup is independent). After sweep: corp PLM issue + attachments persist at corp PLM (no change at corp PLM side; status now `Closed`); HILDA's local NSD copy is deleted per FR-76. **(vii) Edge case — rare follow-up post-cleanup**: if a doc / question surfaces after milestone cleanup, HILDA's adapter supports re-opening via `updateIssueStatus(..., status='Open', ...)` — out of scope for FR-25 Ph-1; flagged as edge case for ops handling. **Why Option B (single FR-76 trigger) over OwnerClosed trigger**: (1) **Robust to all state-machine skip paths** — covers items that skip `OwnerClosed` via FR-7 I8 `doc_count=0` path, FR-64 Part 2 non-gating force-advance, TPM cell-edit `delivery_state = Closed`, FR-80 `no_customer_upload` Mark Closed, etc.; (2) **Late-doc-arrival friendliness** — FR-62 upload-induced revert from `SubmittedToCustomer` / `ReadyForSubmission` lands docs at items already past `OwnerClosed`; PLM stays Open to receive them naturally; (3) **Single sweep per milestone** — one closure action per owner_corp_id at end-of-milestone; simpler than per-item triggers; (4) **No semantic gap during PM workflow** — PLM stays "alive" while HILDA is still actively managing the milestone; closure marks complete-end-of-milestone, not just owner-done. **Cost trade-off accepted**: PLM stays Open longer (potentially weeks between `OwnerClosed` and milestone closure); polling cost is bounded by FR-26 deadline-tiered cadence. **Rationale for HILDA owning closure**: (1) **Symmetric ownership** — HILDA creates → HILDA closes. (2) **TPM workflow proxy** — automation purpose. (3) **File-repository semantic** — no audit requirements for closure ceremony, no compliance hold, no cross-team workflow dependencies. (4) **Constant closure metadata** — corp PLM gateway API supports. (5) **No reversal risk** — re-open supported via adapter for rare follow-ups.

**(b) Customer JIRA adapter** — applies to DeliveryItems with `tracking_modality: CustomerJIRA`; these items have `item_type: Confirmation` per FR-7 I4 + FR-9 CustomerJIRA-only lock — no documents, no AI review, `doc_count = 0`; technical issues and waiver requests raised by TPMs or carriers are tracked in the customer's JIRA instance. **`customer_jira_url` source** (per `[D-083]`): HILDA reads `customer_jira_url` from per-customer `template.yaml` at startup (NOT from SP; the URL is stable per customer and never changes at runtime). SIGHUP-reload mechanism per FR-2 captures URL changes if a customer onboarding adds a new JIRA endpoint. **Role-collapse template constraint** (per FR-2 + FR-88): at template.yaml authoring time, CustomerJIRA-only items MUST have `owner_corp_usa_email` set to the PM's email (the same person who will be `assigned_pm_id` at `setup_milestone`). The template_schema/MODULE.md validator enforces: when `item_type = Confirmation` AND `tracking_modality = [CustomerJIRA]` only, the `owner_corp_usa_email` field MUST match the milestone's `assigned_pm_id` resolved email at `setup_milestone` time — mismatch raises `TSC-W006` (CustomerJIRA-only owner email must equal PM email; role-collapse invariant). HILDA's `sp_alert_parser` does NOT enforce at runtime (validation is at template-load time per FR-2 SIGHUP reload). The `owner_corp_email` secondary field per `[D-080]` is optional but if set must also resolve to the same PM identity. HILDA treats `owner_corp_*` identity as authoritative; PM-approval gate becomes self-approval but NFR-5 audit still applies — the same person clicks Approve, recorded in CommunicationLog. **Outbound email handling for CustomerJIRA items**: HILDA DOES send outbound email reminders for CustomerJIRA items per FR-8 (initial outreach at milestone kickoff) + FR-10 (escalation cadence) + FR-65 (TPM ad-hoc Send Reminder). The outbound goes to the role-collapsed owner-PM identity (the PM's own email). The close-intent reply path per FR-12 + FR-24 IS the feedback mechanism — without outbound reminders, the PM/TPM has no prompt to check JIRA ticket status and reply. The reminder body (per FR-9 format) is tailored for CustomerJIRA-modality items: instead of "please deliver document X", it reads "please review open JIRA tickets at `<customer_jira_url>` and reply to confirm milestone completion". The `[HILDA:...]` tag per FR-24 is preserved; close-intent reply routes via FR-23 stream-(b) per FR-24 lock. **Customer JIRA query**: HILDA polls via `core/src/issue_tracker/jira_adapter.py` (public Jira REST API) at device-model scope — the query returns all open JIRA tickets for the `(carrier_slug, device_id)` pair; no per-milestone filter is applied (JIRA tickets are not tagged by milestone); TPM determines milestone relevance of open tickets — HILDA does not gate on ticket count or content. **Customer JIRA authentication** (per `[D-038]` cascade — clarified 2026-06-17): HILDA accesses customer JIRA via **individual person credentials** — either the PM's own account on the carrier's JIRA OR a HILDA OPS member's individual account (configured per customer; carrier expects identifiable individual accounts for audit per their governance — NOT a service account). Credentials stored in `customizations/credentials/customer_jira/<account_id>/<customer_id>.env.sops` (sops-encrypted per `[D-038]`) where `<account_id>` is the PM-id or HILDA-OPS-member-id resolving the account; the account-id-per-customer mapping lives in `customizations/issue_tracker/<customer_id>_jira_config.yaml` (`account_id: <pm_id | ops_member_id>` + `auth_method: pat | basic_token`). Decrypted at adapter instantiation; NEVER logged per NFR-2. On credential expiry / 401: adapter raises `ITR-E003` (customer JIRA auth expired — `retryable = false`; account_id + customer_id surfaced in `HildaOpsAlert` per FR-75 with the individual identifier; NOT the credential); polling for that customer pauses until ops re-encrypts the sops env file. **Polling cadence**: same dynamic `polling_schedule` as FR-26/FR-55 — `{days_before_deadline, interval_minutes}` breakpoints defined in AutomationRules (Global / Customer / Device tier) evaluated against `days_to_deadline = Milestone.target_date − today` (HILDA reads `Milestone.target_date` from the Milestones SP list per `[D-085]`); baseline tier (`days_before_deadline: null`) required as fallback; polling begins at milestone kickoff (FR-8); **shared `polling_schedule` definition** is canonical in FR-23 cross-FR cadence consistency lock (FR-23 / FR-25 / FR-26 / FR-55 must update together if shape changes). **Open-ticket list rendering surface (SP-side per-row display — option C lock 2026-06-17)**: HILDA polls customer JIRA per cadence → writes per-item `jira_open_ticket_count` (int) + `jira_ticket_summary_json` (JSON list: top-N ticket key / status / summary / last-update; configurable N per-customer; default 5) to the `Deliverables_<customer_id>` row via `[D-064]` REST writeback (parallel to other HILDA-managed columns). **SP UI engineer's web part** renders a compact ticket-count badge per row (e.g., "3 open tickets") with hover tooltip showing the top-N from `jira_ticket_summary_json`; each ticket key links out to the customer JIRA UI for detail. **No new HILDA-rendered `/jira/<customer_id>/<item_id>` endpoint** — JIRA-only items have no documents, so an HILDA-rendered surface would be overkill; the SP per-row display is sufficient. SP_UI_button_actions.md sync follow-up flagged in STATUS.md to add the per-row read paths + button visibility for CustomerJIRA items. **Close-intent flow** (corrected 2026-06-17 — skip `OwnerClosed` per FR-7 I8 + FR-9 CustomerJIRA-only lock): role-collapsed owner-PM sends a close-intent reply email → HILDA performs a fresh JIRA re-query at processing time (out-of-cycle poll) → current open-ticket list written to SP per `[D-064]` REST → item advances DIRECTLY to `UnderPMReview` per FR-7 I8 unified skip rule (Confirmation items skip `OwnerClosed`); HILDA continues polling during `UnderPMReview` so the SP ticket-count badge stays live; PM (same person as owner) reviews the open-ticket list in the SP row + tooltip and determines milestone relevance → Approve action fires `PMApproval` trigger (FR-28) → `ReadyForSubmission` → `SubmittedToCustomer` → `Closed`; polling ceases per FR-74 collection phase closure (milestone-level threshold supersedes the per-item `SubmittedToCustomer` condition). **Close-intent path exclusivity**: close-intent for CustomerJIRA items comes EXCLUSIVELY via owner-PM reply email per FR-12 + FR-24 (close-intent body phrase per FR-12 parsing — typically "please mark complete" / "all tickets resolved" / similar; LLM classifier per FR-12 path (c) detects). Closing all JIRA tickets does NOT auto-advance the item — JIRA polling is informational (live ticket list display); state-machine transitions require explicit close-intent reply. **Rationale**: (i) carrier may close tickets out-of-band without HILDA-side coordination; (ii) auto-advance on JIRA-close would race with PM judgment; (iii) explicit close-intent preserves audit trail (`CommunicationLog` records reply Message-ID + content).
- **FR-26** `[Ph-1]` — **UPDATED 2026-06-17 — owner_corp_id key per FR-25 P1 + FR-52 I10 + FR-88; NSD path FR-86 canonical; FR-86 4-path landing matrix handling; PLM upload phase split independent of tracking_modality per FR-25 P8 + FR-13 + FR-62 + FR-68; cross-FR cadence consistency 4-FR roster (FR-23 lock); default-routed PLM-source docs per FR-78; polling cessation vs PLM closure timing reconciliation**. Corp PLM issues are keyed on `(device_id, milestone_id, owner_corp_id)` per FR-5 plm_id grouping + FR-8 step 2 / `[D-035]` + FR-25 P1 + FR-52 I10 + FR-88 owner identity lock 2026-06-17 — `owner_corp_id` is the stable corp identity (`owner_corp_usa_email` / `owner_corp_email` may change per FR-9 preference rule without changing PLM identity); not per DeliveryItem; `customer_id` is constant within `Deliverables_<customer_id>`, so the per-list key is the 3-tuple. For the initial item batch, issues are created at collection kickoff (FR-8); if a new DeliveryItem is added to a running milestone via `ItemStartCollection` `[Ph-2]` (per FR-3 / DEF-22) and its `owner_corp_id` has no existing PLM issue for the `(device_id, milestone_id, owner_corp_id)` tuple, a new issue is created at that point using the same FR-8 logic; if the owner already has a PLM issue for the tuple, no new issue is created and the new item's documents attach to the existing one; the PLM issue is **document-only** — corp PLM does **not** sync status back to HILDA. When `tracking_modality includes CorporatePLM`, HILDA polls each PLM issue for new owner-uploaded documents. **Polling target**: per `(device_id, milestone_id, owner_corp_id)` per FR-8 step 5 — one polling target per PLM issue (skipped tuples per FR-8 step 2 all-Confirmation rule have no polling target). **Polling frequency**: determined at the milestone level from the milestone's `target_date` (single milestone-level deadline per FR-11 + FR-2 flat-table model — all items in the milestone share the same `target_date`; `target_date` is the sole TPM-editable date per FR-11 / FR-14 lock — per-item override is NOT exposed); the same configurable `polling_schedule` shared across FR-23 (Email Tier 3) + FR-25 (CustomerJIRA + CorporatePLM) + FR-26 (PLM polling — this FR) + FR-55 (NSD polling) per FR-23 cross-FR cadence consistency lock 2026-06-17 — defined in AutomationRules at Global / Customer / Device tier; ordered list of `{days_before_deadline, interval_minutes}` breakpoints evaluated against `days_to_deadline = target_date − today`; HILDA applies the interval of the first threshold where `days_to_deadline ≤ days_before_deadline`; baseline tier with `days_before_deadline: null` is always required as the fallback; example: baseline 60 min, ≤ 3 days → 15 min, ≤ 1 day → 5 min. **Routing**: PLM-polled documents (zip or individual) flow through **FR-52's 5-step routing pipeline** for work-item identification; zip-internal folder structure is consulted at FR-52 step 3 if the TG has `folder_routing_enabled = True` (TG3-style — see FR-77). **doc_type**: classified per FR-85 stage 1 (filename regex + LLM ladder; runs first in the FR-85 sequential pipeline before FR-52 work-item routing per FR-85 lock 2026-06-16; supersedes the prior 1:1 derivation framing per `[D-053]` impl note 2026-06-08 — `CLASSIFY_DOC_TYPE` TaskKind restored). On each poll cycle HILDA downloads attachments from PLM, applies FR-52 routing, writes individual extracted files to `<NSD_root>/internal/<carrier>/<device>/<milestone>/<tg>/<item>/<doc_type>/<doc_id_slug>/revN/` per FR-86 canonical (`classified` path) when classification (FR-85 stage 1) + work-item routing (FR-52 / FR-85 stage 2) + revision determination (`[D-039]`) all succeed (PLM-source zips remain on PLM unchanged; HILDA extracts locally for routing + carrier upload + classified-tree write for audit consistency), records `plm_attachment_id` (already known from poll) directly in the document index. **FR-86 4-path landing matrix applies**: depending on FR-85 stage 1 (doc_type classification) + FR-52 / FR-85 stage 2 (work-item routing) + `[D-039]` (revision determination) outcomes, the PLM-polled doc lands at one of 4 FR-86 paths: (a) `classified` (all 3 succeed); (b) `staged_not_classified` (alignment-invariant violation between item_type + doc_type); (c) `_staged_revision/` (`[D-039]` ambiguous per Step 3); (d) `_unrouted/<inferred_tg_name>/` (routing landed on Default work-item per FR-52 step 5 — `[D-039]` is SKIPPED at ingest per FR-86 skip rule, deferred to FR-83 reassignment). HILDA's `inferred_tg_name` for PLM-polled docs is resolved via reverse-lookup on `plm_id` per `[D-060]` impl note (the polled PLM issue's `(owner_corp_id, plm_id)` key resolves to TG context via `tg_owner_corp_id` / `tg_email_group_alias` matching on the owner's TG memberships within the milestone). TPM resolution per FR-87 moves docs from staged/unrouted paths to `classified` when needed. **PLM-source default-routed docs (FR-78 cascade)**: when an owner uploads a doc to their PLM issue that fails work-item routing (FR-52 step 5 → Default), HILDA writes to `_unrouted/<inferred_tg_name>/` per FR-86. TPM resolves via FR-83 reassignment + FR-87 step (A); the doc moves from `_unrouted/` to `classified/` once routed. The doc is still recorded in the document index with `plm_attachment_id` (PLM-source provenance preserved). **PLM upload semantic** (independent of `tracking_modality` per FR-25 P8 Option B cascade): **(a) PLM-source docs** (this FR's polled docs) — already in PLM; HILDA records `plm_attachment_id` in document index — **no PLM re-upload** (would duplicate). **(b) Non-PLM-source docs** (FR-12 email per FR-52, FR-55 NSD, FR-62 TPM upload) — HILDA uploads to PLM per phase split: `[Ph-1]` at ingest per FR-13 (immediate on classified-path write); `[Ph-2]` deferred to post-dispatch per FR-62 + FR-68 (is_final only). PLM upload SKIPPED when `no_customer_upload = true` per FR-80. The polling lifecycle (Owner → PLM → HILDA reads) and the upload lifecycle (HILDA → PLM) coexist on the same PLM issue throughout its lifetime — PLM stays `status = Open` until FR-76 sweep per FR-25 P8 Option B lock 2026-06-17. `[Ph-2]` `[D-039]` Step 1+ new-vs-revision classification applies per FR-17(c); `[Ph-1]` first-receipt path uses Step 0 hash dedup only (Step 1+ implied as new since no prior docs). After OwnerClosed + TPM approval the sync verification loop (FR-68) verifies all uploaded carrier-portal files hash-match against PLM contents; when stale or superseded PLM attachments need removal, FR-67 PLM stale deletion fires. There is no internal-Jira tracking — the Jira adapter is used only for customer-facing `CustomerJIRA` modality per FR-25. **Polling cessation vs PLM closure timing reconciliation**: PLM polling ceases at FR-74 collection phase closure (no more owner uploads expected via PLM). PLM issue itself remains `status = Open` until FR-76 MilestoneStorageCleanup sweep per FR-25 P8 Option B lock 2026-06-17 — this preserves PLM-side reachability for HILDA-initiated late uploads via FR-13 (Ph-1 buffered Email/NSD if any) + FR-62 (Ph-2 TPM upload) + FR-68 (Ph-2 post-dispatch is_final). The two timings differ by intent: polling-cessation = no-more-owner-input expected; closure = no-more-HILDA-activity expected (end of milestone).

### Communication adapters — Messenger

- **FR-50** `[Ph-1]` — **UPDATED 2026-06-17 — messenger scope locked to FR-11 escalations only per FR-65 cascade; Messenger Protocol surface enumerated; HILDA-bot service account authentication per `[D-038]`; Slack vs proprietary adapter scope distinction; outbound-only semantic clarified; structured `MessengerError` catalog; contract test pattern per `[D-016]`**. Messenger adapter implements the `Messenger` Protocol per `[D-009]`; Ph-1 targets are **Slack** (adapter at `core/src/messenger/slack_adapter.py`, Slack Web API via `slack_sdk`) and the **proprietary internal messenger** (adapter at `customizations/messenger/<proprietary>_adapter.py`, generated by the API Spec Ingestor per `[D-003]` as its first end-to-end exercise in Ph-1); both adapters must pass the same `Messenger` Protocol contract test suite (anchors `[D-016]`). **Messenger usage scope** (cascade with FR-65 email-only lock 2026-06-17): corp messenger is reserved for **FR-11 escalation cascade only** — fires when reminder cadence escalates past email-only thresholds (typically late-stage deadline-proximity rungs where owner has not responded to N successive email reminders). Routine outbound reminders + ad-hoc Send Reminder use email-only per FR-9 lead-in + FR-65 lock; PLM is tracking-only per FR-65; NSD / SPUI are ingest-only. Messenger is NOT a routine modality — it's the escalation tier-up channel. **Messenger Protocol surface** (`core/src/messenger/protocol.py` per `[D-009]`): `{sendStatusMessage, sendDirectMessage, sendChannelMessage, getChannel, resolveUser}` — concise outbound-only surface. **Method usage**: **`sendStatusMessage(target, message, context)`** — primary FR-11 escalation send (target = `owner_corp_id` resolved via `resolveUser` per per-customer adapter config); **`sendDirectMessage`** + **`sendChannelMessage`** — secondary surfaces for HILDA-OPS team notifications (FR-75 `HildaOpsAlert` channel mirror in Slack); **`getChannel` + `resolveUser`** — resolution helpers per-adapter. **No `receiveMessage` / `getReplies` / `getAttachments` methods** — messenger is outbound-only per outbound-only semantic below. **Adapter scope distinction**: **(a) Slack adapter** (`core/src/messenger/slack_adapter.py`) — used for HILDA-team ops notifications (FR-75 `HildaOpsAlert` mirror channel, deployment-pipeline notifications, ad-hoc TPM digest summaries). Recipients are HILDA-team members / TPMs, not owners. **(b) Proprietary internal messenger adapter** (`customizations/messenger/<proprietary_slug>_adapter.py`) — used for FR-11 escalations to owners + escalation CC to TPM/PM. Recipients include the role-collapsed owner-PM per FR-25 (b) for CustomerJIRA items and the R&D owner per FR-88 for other items. Per-customer adapter config in `customizations/messenger/<proprietary_slug>_adapter_config.yaml` resolves owner identity → messenger handle via lookup (`owner_corp_id` → corp directory → messenger user ID; configurable per-customer). **Messenger is OUTBOUND-ONLY** (corrected 2026-06-17 — prior 'attachment receipt not supported' wording was incomplete): HILDA does NOT consume inbound messages from messenger — no message-receive polling, no webhook receiver, no reply routing back to the state machine. If an owner replies to a HILDA escalation message via messenger, the reply is NOT processed (state-machine actions only flow from email replies per FR-23 stream-(b) + FR-12 + FR-24 tag routing). Messenger replies are visible to HILDA-team Slack-mirror observers per FR-75 but do NOT advance per-item state. **Why outbound-only**: (a) inbound parsing complexity duplicates FR-12 email-reply parser without commensurate value (escalation channel is rarely the primary feedback path); (b) corp messenger protocols vary widely (Slack API vs proprietary REST vs WebSocket); per-platform inbound parsers are non-trivial; (c) owner is explicitly directed in escalation messages to reply via email (template includes "reply to the HILDA team mailbox at hilda-team@corp.com" per FR-23 signature contract). Attachment receipt would be a separate concern even if inbound were supported — explicitly excluded. **Messenger authentication** (CORRECTED 2026-06-17 per architect lock — supersedes prior "HILDA-bot service accounts + sops-encrypted credentials" framing for corp messenger; Slack adapter scope clarified separately below): **Corp messenger** (proprietary internal — used for FR-11 owner escalations): accessed via the **corp messenger gateway** (pattern (c) per FR-51 lock 2026-06-17 — gateway-mediated no-key). **HILDA does NOT store any credential** for corp messenger — the gateway handles authentication via corp infrastructure (API key managed entirely by corp messenger gateway admins; HILDA-bot's gateway-registered identity is what HILDA uses). HILDA's `customizations/messenger/<proprietary_slug>_adapter_config.yaml` specifies only: (i) the gateway URL; (ii) HILDA-bot's gateway-registered identity (used as the From identity in send calls). The adapter calls gateway HTTP APIs directly; the gateway authenticates and routes. **HILDA registers with the corp messenger gateway** for both send and receive routing — received messages are delivered to HILDA's webhook/polling endpoint by the gateway but are NOT state-machine processed per FR-50 outbound-only lock (received messages may be logged for audit observation but do NOT trigger rule-engine actions). **No sops-encrypted env file for corp messenger**; HILDA does NOT call `get_credential` per FR-51. **Slack adapter** (used for HILDA-team ops notifications): the Slack adapter follows the same gateway-mediated pattern IF the corp Slack workspace is fronted by a corp gateway with the same infrastructure (verify per deployment); if Slack is accessed directly via Slack Web API with a bot token, the bot token follows pattern (a) service-account sops file at `customizations/credentials/messenger/slack.env.sops` per `[D-038]` (decrypted at adapter instantiation; NEVER logged per NFR-2). **Gateway error handling** (corp messenger): on gateway HTTP error (auth/connectivity/gateway-side API key rotation lapse), adapter raises `MSG-E001` (corp messenger gateway error — `retryable = false`; reframed 2026-06-17 from prior "credential expired" semantic — NOT a HILDA-side credential issue for the gateway-mediated path); logged to `HildaOpsAlert` per FR-75 with gateway URL + HTTP status code (NO credential material involved per NFR-2); the adapter pauses until ops coordinates with corp messenger gateway admins to restore connectivity. **Slack auth-failure handling** (when Slack uses pattern (a) sops bot token): `MSG-E001` retains its prior "auth expired" semantic for the Slack path — adapter pauses until ops re-encrypts the sops env file. **Why gateway-mediated for corp messenger vs sops for Slack**: corp messenger is fronted by a corp-internal gateway (corp infrastructure standard); Slack is an external SaaS where bot token auth is the standard. Both result in HILDA-bot identity to message recipients; the identity model is consistent (BOT account); only the credential-storage mechanics differ per platform infrastructure. **Failure semantics — structured `MessengerError`** (parallel to FR-19 `CarrierAdapterError`): adapter implementations raise `MessengerError(error_code: str, retryable: bool, message: str, cause: Exception | None)` on any failure. **Error codes**: **`MSG-E001`** (auth expired — `retryable = false`); **`MSG-E002`** (network / messenger platform unreachable — `retryable = true`); **`MSG-E003`** (recipient resolution failure — `resolveUser` returned no match for `owner_corp_id`; `retryable = false`; surfaces to TPM with "owner messenger handle not found — check directory mapping" advisory); **`MSG-E004`** (rate-limit hit — `retryable = true` with NFR-10 backoff per platform `Retry-After` header); **`MSG-E005`** (message-too-long / format-rejection — `retryable = false`; usually template misconfiguration); **`MSG-W001`** (informational — recipient is offline / DND; message still sent but may not be seen immediately). HILDA's escalation coordinator handles `retryable = true` with NFR-10 backoff; `retryable = false` → `HildaOpsAlert` per FR-75 + the FR-11 escalation rung continues with next-channel fallback (e.g., if messenger fails, FR-11 may fall back to email-only at higher cadence per FR-11 contract). **Contract test pattern per `[D-016]`**: `core/tests/messenger/test_messenger_contract.py` defines the canonical Protocol contract test suite (parameterized over adapter implementations). **(a) Unit tests** use `FakeMessenger` adapter (in `core/tests/messenger/fakes.py`) — fully in-process; no network; deterministic; the contract suite runs against `FakeMessenger` to verify the Protocol surface itself. **(b) Per-adapter integration tests** subclass the contract suite and parameterize over the real adapter (`SlackAdapter`, `<proprietary>Adapter`); run against a sandbox messenger workspace per-customer (configured in CI env vars; credentials sops-encrypted per `[D-038]` for CI access). **(c) End-to-end test** lives in `core/tests/integration/test_fr11_escalation.py` and exercises the full FR-11 escalation cascade through a real messenger (gated on `--integration` flag in pytest to avoid slow runs in default test pass).
- **FR-54** `[Ph-2]` — Inbound corp messenger messages from owners are captured on receipt, attributed by sender handle to the owner, recorded as comments on all open DeliveryItems in the owner's active batch, and surfaced as a `Messenger reply — Manual triage` flag on the PM dashboard; if the owner has multiple open items across milestones, flags are grouped by milestone to minimise PM triage actions; the runtime LLM classifies the message content to extract status (closed / open / delayed / blocked) and reason — above-threshold classifications are auto-applied; below-threshold go to PM triage.

### Communication adapters — Customer systems (pluggable)

- **FR-27** `[Ph-2]` — Customer adapters are registered via configuration (AutomationRules + per-customer config) including endpoint URL, field mappings, and outbound templates; adding a new customer requires no code change in `core/`.

### Rule engine

- **FR-28** `[Ph-1]` — Rule engine executes IF/THEN AutomationRules. Trigger taxonomy (rules reference data model fields directly and are customer-agnostic in shape):
  - `ItemCreated` — at tracker creation (FR-2): no action, collection not yet started; mid-collection (FR-3 `[Ph-2]`, collection active): fires `StartItemCollection` `[Ph-2]` (FR-3 deferred per DEF-22; mid-collection ItemCreated does not fire in Ph-1)
  - `ItemModified` `[Ph-1]` — sub-triggers:
    - `OwnerReassigned` `[Ph-2]` (per FR-3 / DEF-22 + FR-56 (a) lock — Ph-1 owners are fixed at `setup_milestone` time; owner-change via SP cell-edit is Ph-2 deferred): per FR-2 + FR-88 + `[D-080]` lock — owner-change event fires when EITHER `owner_corp_usa_email` OR `owner_corp_email` changes (HILDA preference rule per FR-9 uses corp_usa_email if set; else corp_email). `owner_corp_id` change alone is NOT an owner-change event per FR-2 + `[D-086]` (it's a free-form text identifier; emails are messaging-channel addresses; no auto-derivation). → `NotifyNewOwner` + `StartItemCollection` for new `(device_id, milestone_id, owner_corp_id)` if collection active
    - `DeadlineMoved`: `Milestone.target_date` changed (per `[D-085]`) → re-arm `DeadlineProximity` evaluation immediately for all items in the milestone
    - `TagsModified` `[Ph-1]`: `item_description` changed (comma-separated tag list per FR-82) → `PropagateTagsToActiveTrackers` — propagates the new tag list to other active items matching `(customer_id, tg_name, item_no)` across all active milestones and devices for that customer; HILDA ops template-YAML edits are not a rule-engine trigger but a config reload (startup load / SIGHUP); operationally, ops can fire the same propagation by editing one representative SP list item which fires this trigger
  - `ItemDeleted` `[Ph-2]` — PM removes item via FR-3 → `CancelOutstanding`
  - `StateChange` — fires on any `delivery_state` change
  - `OwnerStatusConfirmed` — **UPDATED 2026-06-17 — Email-only source per FR-50 outbound-only lock cascade; FR-7 I8 Confirmation skip rule; CustomerJIRA-only path per FR-25 (b) lock cascade**. Explicit owner status message received via **Email only** per FR-23 stream-(b) + FR-12 parsing + FR-24 tag routing (CorporateMessenger removed per FR-50 outbound-only lock 2026-06-17 — messenger is the FR-11 escalation tier-up channel; owner replies via messenger are NOT processed; owner is directed to email in the escalation message body per FR-23 signature contract); maps owner-reported status to `delivery_state`: "done" → evaluate guard (see below), "delayed" → `Delayed`, "blocked" → `Blocked`; **state transition guard for "done"**: **(a) `item_type = Confirmation` SKIPS `OwnerClosed` entirely** per FR-7 I8 unified skip rule — owner-done signal advances `delivery_state` directly to `UnderPMReview` from the item's current pre-PMReview state (skipping `DocumentReceived` + `OwnerClosed`). Two sub-paths: **(a.i) Email-modality Confirmation items** — `OutreachSent → UnderPMReview`; **(a.ii) CustomerJIRA-only Confirmation items** (per FR-7 I4 + FR-9 + FR-25 (b) cascade) — `OutreachSent → UnderPMReview` (HILDA DOES send outbound email reminders per FR-25 (b) lock; recipient is role-collapsed owner-PM identity; outbound body tailored to JIRA modality — "please review open JIRA tickets at `<customer_jira_url>` and reply to confirm milestone completion"; close-intent reply per FR-12 + FR-24 is the trigger). Closing JIRA tickets does NOT auto-advance per FR-25 (b) close-intent path exclusivity. **(b) All other item types** require both conditions to be met when owner confirms "done": (1) `doc_count` reached; (2) all received documents and all their revisions reviewed (for `test_report`: parser result + non-null `llm_review_findings`; for `tech_report`/`waiver`: non-null `llm_review_findings`) — if "done" arrives before condition (1), HILDA notifies the owner that documents are still expected — `delivery_state` stays at its current value (likely `DocumentReceived` if partial docs received), no owner response processed; if "done" arrives before condition (2), state advance is deferred until all reviews complete; when both conditions are met, item advances to `OwnerClosed` (transient) — HILDA immediately forks: `[Ph-1]` single revision per document → `is_final = true` auto-set for rev1 → item advances to `UnderPMReview`; `[Ph-2]` multiple revisions exist for any `doc_id_slug` → `TriggerVersionSelection` (FR-66) fires — owner selects final revision → `is_final = true` → item advances to `UnderPMReview`; if a "done" message arrives while the item is still in `OutreachSent` (no document received yet — applies only to (b) non-Confirmation items), the state is not advanced and a `Premature closure attempt` flag surfaces via `manual_triage_required = true` per FR-12 lock (TPM sees it in SP UI list view per Part 2 read paths). Document receipt alone via PLM or NSD does **not** fire this trigger — see `AttachmentReceived`
  - `LastContactThreshold` — fires when `days_since_last_contact > N` (FR-10)
  - `DeadlineProximity` — fires when `Milestone.target_date - today ≤ N` (FR-11; read from Milestones SP list per `[D-085]`)
  - `AttachmentReceived` — **UPDATED 2026-06-17 — FR-62 + FR-83 source paths added; revert-on-arrival pattern locked per FR-62 + STATUS.md FR-83 follow-up Flag**. Document arrives via any ingest channel: email attachment (FR-12), PLM poll (FR-26), NSD drop (FR-55), **TPM upload (FR-62 `[Ph-2]`)**, OR FR-83 TPM reassignment from default work-item to a real item. **Revert-on-arrival pattern**: if arrival lands on an item currently in `ReadyForSubmission` or `SubmittedToCustomer`, HILDA reverts `delivery_state` to `UnderPMReview` atomically (SP UI engineer's web part performs the revert + clears `pm_approval_at` + `pm_approval_pm_id` for FR-62 path per `[D-068]`; FR-83 path follows same revert pattern per pending FR-83 lock per STATUS.md Flag 2026-06-17). Behaviour on items in earlier states (Open / OutreachSent / DocumentReceived / UnderPMReview) depends on `doc_type`: if `doc_type = test_report`, system checks count of `test_report` rows in the document index for this item — if count reaches `doc_count` (from template, FR-7), → `UpdateState` to `DocumentReceived`; if count < `doc_count`, document is recorded in the index and FR-53 LLM review is triggered but `delivery_state` remains unchanged; supplementary documents (`waiver`, `tech_report`) are always recorded in the index and trigger FR-53 LLM review but do **not** advance `delivery_state`; does **not** set `OwnerClosed`
  - `AIReviewResult` — HILDA LLM review complete per FR-53 → `NotifyPM` surfacing findings in the HILDA-rendered document section per `[D-074]` (writes `llm_review_findings` to document index row; updates `review_status` flag on DeliveryItem to the **4-value enum per FR-53 I8 lock** (`pending` / `complete` / `not_required` / `failed` — `failed` for persistent LLM review failure per FR-53 I8 + FR-59 I4 badge surface; `not_required` when item's `review_required = false`); SP UI displays the flag in the list view per SP_UI_button_actions.md Part 2 read paths); **does not advance `delivery_state`** — LLM review completion satisfies condition (2) of the `OwnerClosed` guard; it is not itself a state transition
  - `PMApproval` — per-item; PM explicitly approves item action via SP UI Approve button per FR-56 (c) → **SP UI engineer's web part writes the atomic 3-field transition** (`delivery_state = ReadyForSubmission` + `pm_approval_at = <now>` + `pm_approval_pm_id = <pm>`) per `[D-068]` impl note 2026-06-12 — HILDA receives the SP-alert and does NOT write `delivery_state` (SP-side write-owner per `[D-068]` lock; corrected 2026-06-17 — supersedes the prior `UpdateState` rule-body wording). HILDA's `PMApproval` rule fires `QueueSubmission` (queue for FR-18 dispatch) + logs `CommunicationLog action_type = pm_approval` with the editor identity per FR-14 attribution pattern. NFR-5 gate at click time on SP UI side.
  - `TrackerCreated` `[Ph-1]` — fires once per tracker at FR-1/FR-2 tracker creation → `InstantiateDefaultWorkItem` (FR-78); `[Ph-2]` additionally fires `TriggerODF` (FR-71) for each TG in the new tracker
  - `MilestoneAllClosed` `[Ph-1]` — milestone-level trigger, not a per-item event; **fires when all `milestone_gating=true` DeliveryItems in a milestone reach `Closed` state** (gating-aware semantic locked 2026-06-13 — aligns with FR-64 enablement model; supersedes the prior "all DeliveryItems reach `Closed`" wording). Non-gating items (`milestone_gating=false`) do NOT block this trigger; per FR-64 rewrite 2026-06-13, the Close All Items bulk action auto-advances non-gating items in non-Closed states to `Closed` so the state machine remains consistent. Default work-item (FR-78) is implicitly `milestone_gating=true` (hardcoded; cannot be set false) — it must reach `Closed` via TPM Mark Closed before this trigger fires. Pathways reaching `Closed`: FR-14 per-item override, FR-64 bulk Close All Items (gating + non-gating cascade), FR-7 TPM-Mark-Closed for `no_customer_upload` items, FR-78 TPM-Mark-Closed for default work-item. Action: → `MilestoneStorageCleanup` (FR-76). **Cleanup-only semantic — does NOT advance any item state on its own** (FR-64's bulk-close is the advance mechanism); automated transition-to-`Closed` per the former FR-22 model remains deferred (DEF-20). The earlier struck-through `[Ph-2]` deferral of this trigger applied only to the state-advance variant; the cleanup variant is Ph-1 (carries FR-76)
  - `CollectionPhaseClosureReached` `[Ph-1]` — milestone-level trigger; fires when all items are in `{ReadyForSubmission, SubmittedToCustomer, Closed}` per FR-74 (items in `Blocked`/`Delayed` keep collection open) → `FinalSweep` + `HaltMilestonePolling`; not equivalent to `MilestoneAllClosed` (that fires later, on all-`Closed`)
  - `UnroutedDocumentAccumulated` `[Ph-2]` — fires when a document is associated with a milestone's default work-item per FR-78 (`unrouted_source_path` set) AND remains unresolved past a configurable threshold → `SendOwnerRoutingQuery` (FR-83 Ph-2 owner outreach upstream of TPM); Ph-1 path is TPM-manual via SP UI (FR-83) without this trigger
  - `CredentialExpired` `[Ph-1]` — fires when a PM credential check fails: either **`CSA-E000`** (pre-flight credential file missing per FR-20 lock 2026-06-17 — dispatch coordinator detects before adapter invocation) or **`CSA-E001`** (adapter-session credential expired per FR-19 lock — detected during adapter run) → `NotifyHildaOps` per FR-75 with PM identifier + carrier_slug (credential material NEVER logged per NFR-2). **Item stays in `ReadyForSubmission`** per FR-20 lock — HILDA does NOT auto-queue or auto-retry; TPM manually re-clicks FR-63 Submit after ops re-encrypts the sops env file per `[D-038]`. Ph-3+ Vault populates `expires_at` for proactive detection; Ph-1/Ph-2 detects only on actual auth failure during dispatch (corrected 2026-06-17 — supersedes prior "queue the dispatching action" wording which contradicted FR-20 lock).

  Rule bodies cited by FR ID: FR-8 (PLM issue creation + initial outreach at Start Collection), FR-9 (per-modality outreach format), FR-10 (reminder + messenger escalation), FR-11 (deadline escalation to owner + PM), FR-16 (test-report parser trigger on attachment), FR-20 (credential-expired submission queue), FR-46 (final/interim classification on test-report receipt), FR-47 (failed-item surface on PM dashboard), FR-53 (AI quality review trigger), FR-71 (Owner Discovery Function on tracker creation), FR-74 (collection phase closure threshold), FR-75 (fault routing to HildaOpsAlert), FR-76 (milestone storage cleanup on all-Closed), FR-78 (default work-item instantiation at tracker creation), FR-82 (tag-catalog propagation on SP UI tag edits), FR-83 (default work-item resolution — TPM + owner outreach), NFR-5 (PM-approval gate before any customer-facing action), FR-18 (queue for customer submission on PM approval).
- **FR-29** `[Ph-1]` — Rule actions are listed below. Action parameters (channel, recipients, template, target state) are rule-instance-specific; new action types are added via configuration without code change.

  **Ph-1 actions:**
  - `SendReminder` — scheduled owner reminder per FR-10
  - `Escalate` — cross-channel escalation via corp messenger per **FR-11** escalation cascade (NOT FR-10; FR-10 = email-only reminder cadence per FR-65 + FR-23 + FR-9 lock cascade). Fires when reminder cadence escalates past email-only thresholds — typically late-stage deadline-proximity rungs where owner has not responded to N successive email reminders. Messenger is reserved for FR-11 escalations per FR-50 + FR-65 lock 2026-06-17. Outbound-only (no inbound from messenger per FR-50). Corrected 2026-06-17 — supersedes prior "per FR-10" wording.
  - `UpdateState` — set `delivery_state` to a specified value via `[D-064]` REST writeback (HILDA-side write-owner discipline). **NOT used for SP-side state transitions** — the PMApproval `delivery_state = ReadyForSubmission` write per `[D-068]` is SP UI engineer's web part atomic 3-field transaction (NOT HILDA via `UpdateState`); HILDA-side state writes are: AttachmentReceived → DocumentReceived (when `doc_count` reached), OwnerStatusConfirmed → OwnerClosed transient + UnderPMReview (per FR-7 state machine), FR-7 I2 Delayed/Blocked entry/exit handling, and other rule-engine-driven transitions. Write-owner discipline parallel to FR-12 / FR-14 / FR-15-extended hub per `[D-064]` REST writeback lock.
  - `StartItemCollection` — idempotent PLM issue creation for an `(device_id, milestone_id, owner_corp_id)` pair (per FR-25 P1 + FR-52 I10 + FR-88 lock) followed by `SendInitialOutreach`; used by `ItemCreated` mid-collection `[Ph-2]` and `OwnerReassigned` `[Ph-2]`. Idempotent: re-firing on same tuple with existing PLM issue is a no-op (detect existing `plm_id`; skip creation).
  - `SendInitialOutreach` — initial owner outreach via **email** per FR-9 lead-in lock + FR-23 outbound + FR-24 tag-bearing subject (no "per-modality" branching — email is the only outbound owner-contact channel per FR-65 modality lock; CustomerJIRA-only items receive email outreach per FR-25 (b) with body tailored to JIRA modality; NSD/SPUI items also receive email outreach for kickoff context). Fired at FR-8 Start Collection per `(device_id, milestone_id, owner_corp_id)` tuple per FR-25 P1.
  - `NotifyNewOwner` `[Ph-2]` — email outreach (per FR-9 + FR-65 email-only lock) to reassigned owner including current item state and delivery path; fired by `OwnerReassigned` Ph-2 trigger
  - `TriggerParser` — invokes the rule-based test-report parser (FR-16) distinct from the LLM review
  - `TriggerAIReview` — invokes the LLM document quality review per FR-53
  - `QueueSubmission` — **no-op marker action** (preserved for backward compatibility / audit trail) — does NOT trigger any HILDA-side queueing or auto-dispatch. Per FR-63 + FR-18 lock 2026-06-17: FR-18 dispatch only fires when TPM clicks FR-63 Submit to Carrier in SP milestone view. The `delivery_state = ReadyForSubmission` (written SP-side by the FR-56 (c) Approve button atomic 3-field write per `[D-068]`) IS the queue — FR-63 enumerates items in that state at click time. This rule action effectively logs `CommunicationLog action_type = pm_approval_recorded` indicating PM approved + item is now SP-side `ReadyForSubmission` + ready for next TPM Submit click; no further HILDA-side action.
  - `NotifyPM` — surfaces a dashboard alert and/or out-of-band notification to the PM/TPM (HILDA-rendered document section per `[D-074]` and/or SP UI list view column flags); no owner-facing outbound
  - `NotifyHildaOps` — routes background processing faults (ZIP extraction errors, classification failures, polling errors, document routing failures, adapter errors per FR-19/FR-25/FR-50 catalogs) to the `HildaOpsAlert` queue per FR-75; mirrored to HILDA-team Slack per FR-50 Slack adapter; distinct from `NotifyPM` which surfaces in the HILDA-rendered document section per `[D-074]` (review findings) or in the SP UI milestone view (column flags) — ops-team-only destination
  - `VerifyDefaultWorkItem` — at FR-8 Start Collection (NOT at tracker creation; SP UI engineer's `setup_milestone` per FR-2 R&R lock creates ALL SP rows including the default work-item per FR-78); HILDA verifies the default work-item row exists in `Deliverables_<customer_id>` for the milestone (`item_type = Default`, system-reserved TG `_unrouted`, `milestone_gating = true` hardcoded, `doc_count = 0`); if missing, HILDA logs `STATUS-W002` orphan to `HildaOpsAlert` per FR-75 (template/setup_milestone gap) but continues — default work-item is essential for FR-86 unrouted-path catchall + FR-78 lifecycle gating. Trigger: `TrackerCreated` rule body invokes this verification. (Renamed from `InstantiateDefaultWorkItem` 2026-06-17 per FR-2 R&R lock cascade — HILDA NEVER creates SP rows; SP UI engineer's `setup_milestone` is sole row-creation path.)
  - `MilestoneStorageCleanup` — per FR-76 (multi-step sweep): **(a) PLM sweep** — enumerates all PLM issues created by HILDA for the milestone's `(device_id, milestone_id, *)` tuples and closes each via `updateIssueStatus(plm_id, status='Closed', metadata={...constant from adapter config...})` per FR-25 P8 Option B lock 2026-06-17; failures log `ITR-E004` (retryable) but do NOT block subsequent steps; **(b) NSD cleanup** — deletes the milestone's NSD1 `internal/<carrier>/<device>/<milestone>/` subtree per FR-86 canonical + FR-76 + HILDA PC local cache; preserves NSD1 owner-inbound flat folder + NSD2 per `[D-013]` impl note. Ordering: PLM sweep BEFORE NSD cleanup (corp PLM and NSD are independent; logical close-then-delete semantic preserved). Fired by `MilestoneAllClosed` trigger.
  - `HaltMilestonePolling` — halts FR-25 (a) CorporatePLM polling + FR-25 (b) CustomerJIRA polling + FR-26 PLM polling + FR-55 NSD monitoring for the milestone per FR-74 once collection phase closes. FR-23 Email Tier 1 IMAP IDLE continues afterwards (always-on; not milestone-scoped).
  - `FinalSweep` — one final poll across all milestone-scoped ingest channels: FR-26 (PLM), FR-55 (NSD), FR-25 (b) (CustomerJIRA), and an FR-23 best-effort drain of any unprocessed email queue items for the milestone. Email Tier 1 IMAP IDLE continues afterwards (always-on; not milestone-scoped) but no new state-machine actions fire for the milestone post-`HaltMilestonePolling`. Per FR-74 collection-phase closure.
  - `ReassignDocumentToWorkItem` — per FR-83 TPM-manual via SP UI (updated 2026-06-17 per `[D-055]` symmetric M:M + FR-85 + FR-86 + FR-62 revert + STATUS.md FR-83 follow-up Flag): (i) updates the `DocumentItemAssociation` row from the default work-item's `delivery_item_id` to the selected real work-item's `delivery_item_id` (per `[D-055]` symmetric M:M model — no primary/secondary distinction; supersedes prior "primary association" framing); (ii) **runs `[D-039]` revision determination** for the resolved `(item, doc_type)` family per FR-86 skip-rule deferral (`[D-039]` was SKIPPED at ingest for default-routed docs; now runs against the resolved family at reassignment time); (iii) **moves the file** in NSD from `internal/<carrier>/<device>/<milestone>/<tg>/_unrouted/<inferred_tg_name>/<original_filename>` (FR-86 `_unrouted` path) to `internal/<carrier>/<device>/<milestone>/<tg>/<item>/<doc_type>/<doc_id_slug>/revN/` (FR-86 `classified` path or `_staged_revision/` if `[D-039]` ambiguous); (iv) **does NOT re-derive `doc_type`** from item_type — `doc_type` was classified at ingest per FR-85 stage 1 and persists (supersedes the prior `re-derive doc_type from item.item_type` framing per `[D-053]` lock); if it was `unresolved` at ingest, TPM resolves via FR-87 step (B) BEFORE reassignment to a non-Default item (alignment invariant enforced); (v) **revert-on-arrival pattern** (cascade with FR-62 revert + STATUS.md FR-83 follow-up Flag 2026-06-17): if the target item is currently in `ReadyForSubmission` or `SubmittedToCustomer`, HILDA reverts `delivery_state` to `UnderPMReview` atomically (SP UI engineer's web part performs the revert + clears `pm_approval_at` + `pm_approval_pm_id` per `[D-068]` clearing discipline); (vi) **does NOT trigger carrier upload** — carrier dispatch is FR-18 + FR-63 click after the item next reaches `ReadyForSubmission` (subject to `no_customer_upload` per FR-80); (vii) PLM upload phase split per FR-13 (Ph-1 at ingest) or FR-62 + FR-68 (Ph-2 deferred); for PLM-source reassigned docs no PLM upload needed (already in PLM; `plm_attachment_id` preserved per FR-26); (viii) logs `CommunicationLog action_type = tpm_reassign_to_workitem`.
  - `PropagateTagsToActiveTrackers` — per FR-82: when `ItemModified.TagsModified` fires, propagates the new `item_description` tag list to all active items matching `(customer_id, tg_name, item_no)` across all active milestones and devices for that customer; idempotent — duplicate tags de-duplicated; SP REST writes per item; audit logged with `action_type: tag_catalog_propagation`

  **Ph-2 actions:**
  - `CancelOutstanding` `[Ph-2]` — cancel all pending reminders and escalations for a removed item and notify owner of removal
  - `NotifyOwnerDocCountPending` `[Ph-1]` (re-tagged 2026-06-17 — was Ph-2; the event-driven behavior is Ph-1 per FR-7 lock; only the mechanism changes from corp messenger to email per FR-50 + FR-65 lock cascade) — **email notification** to owner per FR-9 + FR-65 email-only lock (NOT messenger — messenger is reserved for FR-11 escalations per FR-50 + FR-65 lock 2026-06-17). **Trigger**: `OwnerStatusConfirmed` rule fires with `status = "done"` AND guard condition (1) (`doc_count` reached) NOT met AND `item_type ≠ Confirmation` (i.e., owner sent premature close-intent reply before all expected documents arrived). Distinct from FR-10 periodic reminders (cadence-driven) and FR-11 escalations (escalation-tier driven) — this is event-driven, one-shot per premature close-intent. Body: "You confirmed completion of item X but `<N>` of `<M>` `test_report` documents have been received; please deliver remaining `<M-N>` documents. (No reply needed; deliver via your usual channel.)". Sent via FR-23 outbound + FR-24 tag-bearing subject; recipient is the owner per FR-9 preference rule. **Does NOT update `last_owner_contacted`** (informational reply, not an outreach event) and **does NOT reset FR-10 cadence**. `delivery_state` unchanged. Logged to `CommunicationLog action_type = doc_count_pending_notification`.
  - `TriggerVersionSelection` `[Ph-2]` — initiate version-selection workflow per FR-66 when multiple revisions exist at OwnerClosed time. **Mechanism conflict with FR-50 outbound-only lock 2026-06-17 — pending FR-66 review**: prior framing was "corp messenger" but FR-50 just locked messenger as outbound-only (no inbound processing).
