Either (a) FR-66 needs to use **email** for version selection (HILDA emails owner asking which revision is final; owner replies; FR-12 parses revision selection from reply per FR-24 tag routing) — RECOMMENDED to keep consistent with FR-50; OR (b) FR-50 needs an explicit exception for FR-66 inbound (per-message-type webhook for version selection). Defaults to (a) for FR-66 review unless platform constraints prevent — STATUS.md Flag added 2026-06-17 to track during FR-66 review.
  - `TriggerPLMCleanup` `[Ph-2]` — initiate PLM stale attachment deletion + owner confirmation workflow per FR-67
  - `TriggerODF` `[Ph-2]` — initiate the Owner Discovery Function per FR-71 for the TG group; corp messenger outreach to TG members to discover current owner assignments at tracker creation
  - `SendOwnerRoutingQuery` `[Ph-2]` — per FR-83 upstream of TPM: send corp-messenger query to the originating owner asking which work-item a default-work-item document pertains to; cadence shares `escalation_schedule` AutomationRule; after M unanswered attempts escalates to TPM via `NotifyPM`
- **FR-30** `[Ph-1]` — Rules are scopeable to Global, Customer, or Device level and are customer-agnostic in shape (referencing modality fields, not hard-coded channels); rule files are authored by the HILDA ops team as YAML files under `customizations/rules/` (separate from template schema YAMLs under `customizations/template_schemas/`); directory layout:
  ```
  customizations/rules/
    global/
      defaults.yaml                      ← Global rules; apply to all customers/devices unless overridden
    <customer_id>/
      customer_rules.yaml                ← Customer-scoped overrides
      <device_id>/
        device_rules.yaml                ← Device-scoped overrides (optional; created only when needed)
  ```
  Rule resolution order: Device overrides Customer overrides Global (most-specific wins per rule ID); a customer- or device-scoped file need only contain the rules that differ from the tier above — absent rules fall through to the next tier; rule YAML files are bind-mounted into the HILDA containers per `[D-025]` and are reloaded at startup without redeploy; adding a new customer or device requires creating a new YAML file (ops operation, no code change in `core/`); `[Ph-2]` the Template Schema Ingestor `[D-010]` can generate the initial `customer_rules.yaml` from a customer Excel schema. **Ph-1 PM/TPM override mechanism** (per FR-56 (a) lock 2026-06-12 — corrects original "Postgres item-level records" framing for Ph-1): per-item rule overrides are NOT exposed in SP UI in Ph-1 — TPMs request rule overrides verbally / via ticket to HILDA ops; ops edit `customizations/rules/<customer_id>/per_item_overrides.yaml` (YAML drop-zone format scoping per-item rule param adjustments) which is picked up via SIGHUP reload (see Reload mechanism below). **Ph-2 introduces SP UI inline override** per FR-31 sub-2 Ph-2 framing: PM/TPM edit per-item polling_schedule breakpoints (and additional rule params per FR-31 sub-2 Ph-2 scope) via SP UI; overrides persist as **Postgres item-level records** (NOT written back to YAML); Postgres overrides take precedence over the YAML tiers (Device → Customer → Global) during rule evaluation for that item. **Tier resolution order at evaluation**: (Ph-2) Postgres item-level → (Ph-1) `per_item_overrides.yaml` drop-zone if applicable → Device YAML → Customer YAML → Global YAML — most-specific wins per rule ID. Both FR-31 sub-2 and FR-56 (a) carry this same Ph-1/Ph-2 split; future Ph-1 reviewer should verify FR-31 sub-2 aligns with this lock. **Reload mechanism**: rule YAML changes apply via two paths: **(a) HILDA process restart** (full bootstrap re-reads all rule YAML tiers — used for major updates / verification); **(b) SIGHUP runtime reload** (ops sends `SIGHUP` to HILDA process; rule_engine re-reads all tier YAMLs in-place; no in-flight rule evaluation is interrupted — partial reads use the prior tier snapshot; subsequent evaluations use the new snapshot). Cross-ref: FR-2 (b) template.yaml change semantics + STATUS.md Flag "HILDA SIGHUP handler implementation confirmation" 2026-06-15 — if HILDA's actual reload mechanism is CLI command / HTTP admin endpoint / file watcher instead of SIGHUP, replace the literal "SIGHUP" with the actual mechanism uniformly across FR-2 + FR-30 + FR-56 (a). **`polling_schedule` rule cross-FR consistency** (per FR-23 cascade lock 2026-06-17): the `polling_schedule` AutomationRule (`{days_before_deadline, interval_minutes}` breakpoints) is referenced by 4 FRs: FR-23 (Email Tier 3) + FR-25 (a) CorporatePLM + FR-25 (b) CustomerJIRA + FR-26 PLM + FR-55 NSD. The rule shape is canonical in FR-23; YAML edits to `polling_schedule` breakpoints (Global/Customer/Device tier) flow through to ALL 4 FRs via the shared `polling_schedule` rule ID. **Schema-level changes to the rule shape** (e.g., adding a new field, renaming `days_before_deadline`) require coordinated review across all 4 FRs + the canonical definition in FR-23 — flagged in this FR as a cross-FR change gate. **Scope-id sourcing** (per FR-31 + `[D-077]` lock 2026-06-12 — corrected 2026-06-17 per architect clarification: SP Ph-1 is ONE flat list per customer; NO separate Customers/Devices/Milestones SP lists): the `<customer_id>` and `<device_id>` directory names under `customizations/rules/` correspond to the customer_id + device_id values HILDA reads from **template.yaml at startup** per FR-2 (template.yaml "what to track" manifest at the structural level — primary-key value set defining the universe of (customer, device, milestone) tuples HILDA tracks). NOT sourced from SP at runtime — Ph-1 SP architecture per `[D-077]` flat-table lock is **ONE flat list per customer** (`Deliverables_<customer_id>`); customer-level / device-level / milestone-level fields are denormalized as columns on each DeliveryItem row in that single list. HILDA does NOT read separate Customers / Devices / Milestones SP lists — they don't exist in Ph-1. At SP-alert receive time per FR-84, HILDA resolves the 5-field routing key (customer_id, Model, ProjectID, MinorMilestone, ItemNumber) by reading the relevant denormalized columns from the `Deliverables_<customer_id>` row identified by the alert — same single flat list. HILDA validates at startup that every `<customer_id>` / `<device_id>` directory has a matching tuple in template.yaml; orphaned rule directories (no matching template tuple) log `STATUS-W003` informational to `HildaOpsAlert` per FR-75 (rule will load but doesn't match any active scope; ops should clean up).
- **FR-31** `[Ph-2]` — **DEFERRED TO Ph-2 2026-06-17 per architect lock — supersedes prior [Ph-1] tagging on all 3 sub-capabilities (sub-2 was already partially Ph-2 per FR-56 (a) lock 2026-06-12; sub-1 + sub-3 moved to Ph-2 in this lock; FR-31 now fully Ph-2 in alignment with FR-14 Option B owner override + FR-3 / DEF-22 + per_item_rule_overrides FR-56 (a) deferrals). Rationale: TPM tactical needs in Ph-1 are served by dedicated buttons (Approve / Send Reminder per FR-65 / Mark Closed / Clear Triage Flag / Confirm Carrier Acceptance / Resume from Delayed-Blocked) + cell edits per FR-14 lock — see Ph-1 TPM SP UI capability inventory in STATUS.md. Ph-1 rule changes are ops-mediated via `customizations/rules/<customer_id>/per_item_overrides.yaml` YAML drop-zone + SIGHUP reload per FR-30 I1 lock 2026-06-17. Ph-2 introduces the full rule control panel below.** Rule control panel: PM and TPM can pause, customize, or manually trigger any rule-driven action on any tracker from the SharePoint milestone-view web part (FR-56). **Scope identifiers** (corrected 2026-06-17 per architect lock — `[D-077]` flat-table; NO separate Customers/Devices/Milestones SP lists in Ph-1): rule-override scope is one of `Device | Customer | Global`; `scope_id` for Device-scope = `device_id`; for Customer-scope = `customer_id` — both sourced from **template.yaml at HILDA startup** per FR-2 + `[D-077]` (customer.yaml dropped per `[D-077]` final amendment; template.yaml is the surviving 'what to track' manifest at the structural level). NOT sourced from SP at runtime — Ph-1 SP architecture per `[D-077]` flat-table lock is **ONE flat list per customer** (`Deliverables_<customer_id>`); customer-level / device-level / milestone-level fields are denormalized as columns on each DeliveryItem row in that single list (no separate Customers / Devices / Milestones SP lists exist in Ph-1). **`[Ph-2]` `AutomationRuleOverride` rows** in storage's Postgres carry `item_id + rule_id + override_values` for item-level overrides (per FR-30 I1 lock 2026-06-17 — Ph-1 item-level overrides live in `per_item_overrides.yaml` drop-zone, NOT Postgres). Tier-level (Device / Customer / Global) overrides live exclusively in YAML across both phases; they do NOT have Postgres representation. `scope_id` is sourced from template.yaml at startup per FR-2 + I2 above and is used by rule_engine at evaluation time, NOT stored alongside Postgres item-level overrides. Three sub-capabilities (all Ph-2):
  1. **Pause / resume** `[Ph-2]` — per-item rule pause toggle available on each DeliveryItem row; when paused, all AutomationRule evaluations for that item are suppressed (no reminders, escalations, or deadline triggers fire); a visual `⏸ Rules paused` badge is shown on the item row; pause is recorded in `CommunicationLog` with PM/TPM attribution; PM or TPM may resume at any time — `CommunicationLog` records the resume; a milestone-level **Pause All** / **Resume All** control pauses/resumes every item in the milestone in one operation; `[Ph-2]` pause can also be scoped to a specific rule type (e.g., pause `DeadlineProximity` only) rather than all rules for the item. **`CommunicationLog` action_types**: **(a) per-item pause**: `action_type = item_rules_paused` with PM/TPM Editor identity; **(b) per-item resume**: `action_type = item_rules_resumed`; **(c) milestone-level Pause All**: `action_type = milestone_rules_pause_all` (one log entry per item touched in the milestone, with milestone-scoped batch_id for correlation per `[D-082]` cascade-dedup); **(d) milestone-level Resume All**: `action_type = milestone_rules_resume_all`; **(e) per-rule-type pause**: `action_type = item_rule_type_paused` with `rule_type` field in payload (e.g., `DeadlineProximity`). All entries include the `rules_paused_at` SP column timestamp at the time of action for time-correlation with downstream rule-engine suppression.
  2. **Customize rule parameters** `[Ph-2]` — per-item override of the `polling_schedule` AutomationRule breakpoints (`days_before_deadline`, `interval_minutes`) via an inline edit control on the item row; changes are persisted as **item-level override records in Postgres** (`AutomationRuleOverride` rows; NOT written back to YAML files) and take effect on the next evaluation cycle; Postgres overrides take precedence over all three YAML tiers (Device → Customer → Global) during rule evaluation for that item; Ph-2 also extends to full rule parameter editing UI: PM/TPM can edit `LastContactThreshold` N-day value, `DeadlineProximity` N-day value, and reminder template per item — Postgres override record stores the changed fields alongside the source rule ID and YAML tier it overrides, so the UI can display "overridden from global default" or "overridden from customer rule". **Tier resolution order at evaluation** (per FR-30 lock): Postgres item-level → `per_item_overrides.yaml` drop-zone if applicable (Ph-1 carryover) → Device YAML → Customer YAML → Global YAML — most-specific wins per rule ID.
  3. **Manually trigger any action** `[Ph-2]` — a **Trigger Action** control on each item row presents a dropdown of applicable FR-29 actions for that item's current `delivery_state` (Option (b) Ph-2 lock 2026-06-17 per architect — Ph-1 dropdown is empty / not exposed; manual re-trigger of failed automation in Ph-1 goes via HILDA ops admin CLI or rule_engine direct call). **Dropdown contents (Ph-2)**: **(a) `TriggerAIReview`** — shown only when documents exist + `review_required = true` + (typically) `review_status = failed` per FR-53 I8 lock (re-trigger LLM review for failed cases); **(b) `TriggerParser`** — shown when an unparsed or `parser_failed` `test_report` document exists per FR-16 (re-trigger parser for failed cases). **`QueueSubmission` is NOT exposed in this dropdown** per FR-29 I6 lock 2026-06-17 — it's a no-op marker action (delivery_state IS the queue per FR-63 + FR-18 lock; the natural `UnderPMReview → ReadyForSubmission` advance is via the FR-56 (c) Approve button which writes atomically SP-side per `[D-068]`, not via a HILDA-side trigger). **`ReassignDocumentToWorkItem` is NOT in SP UI** — lives in HILDA-rendered tab per FR-87 + `[D-074]` Variant A (TPM clicks View Documents → HILDA tab → inline buttons on document rows). Selecting a dropdown action enqueues it immediately as a Celery task bypassing normal rule evaluation; the trigger is recorded in `CommunicationLog` with PM/TPM attribution and `trigger_source: manual`. **`SendReminder` via Trigger Action dropdown semantic equivalence** (per FR-65 lock 2026-06-17): the dropdown invocation produces the same effect as the FR-65 dedicated button (which is Ph-1 — FR-65 is NOT deferred) — same email-only modality (PLM is tracking-only; messenger is FR-11 escalation-only); same write-owner discipline (SP UI engineer writes `last_reminder_triggered_at` trigger; HILDA writes side-effects `last_owner_contacted` + `reminder_count` + `Open → OutreachSent` transition via `[D-064]` REST); same FR-10 cadence-reset; same Delayed/Blocked semantics (dispatch fires but no auto-exit from admin state); same default-work-item exclusion (suppress when `item_type = Default`). FR-65 is the dedicated single-click Ph-1 surface for the most-common ad-hoc trigger; the FR-31 dropdown (Ph-2) surfaces it alongside other action triggers but produces an identical state-machine effect. Per `CommunicationLog`: action_type = `send_reminder_manual_trigger` (distinct from FR-65's `send_reminder_button_click` only by source attribution; same downstream rule body).

### Credentials

- **FR-51** `[Ph-1]` — **UPDATED 2026-06-17 per architect lock — 4 distinct identity-model patterns; HILDA stores credentials for only 2 of them; cascade updates to FR-25 P6 + FR-50 in this commit**. Ph-1/Ph-2 `credential_service` provides credential resolution for HILDA's adapter ecosystem. **Four identity-model patterns** govern credential handling (locked 2026-06-17 per architect): **(a) Service-account sops file** — HILDA service identity (BOT account); single sops-encrypted file per service at `customizations/credentials/<service_slug>.env.sops`; **HILDA stores credential**. Ph-1 use: **Email SMTP** (BOT email account per FR-23 + `[D-038]` — `hilda-team-mailbox.env.sops`). **(b) Per-identity sops file** — individual person (HUMAN account: PM or HILDA-OPS member); one sops-encrypted file per `(person, system)` tuple at `customizations/credentials/<system_class>/<identity_id>/<system_slug>.env.sops`; **HILDA stores credential**. Ph-1 use: **Customer adapter** (Google Drive PM-credentialed per FR-19 — `<pm_id>/<carrier_slug>.env.sops`); **Customer JIRA** (PM-or-OPS individual per FR-25 (b) — `customer_jira/<account_id>/<customer_id>.env.sops`). **(c) Gateway-mediated no-key** — corp-internal gateway handles API authentication; **HILDA does NOT store any credential**; HILDA's adapter just calls gateway HTTP APIs. Ph-1 use: **Corp messenger** (BOT account; gateway API key managed entirely by corp messenger gateway infrastructure; HILDA's `customizations/messenger/<adapter_slug>_adapter_config.yaml` specifies only the gateway URL + HILDA-bot's gateway-registered identity; HILDA registers with the gateway for both send and receive routing — received messages are NOT state-machine processed per FR-50 outbound-only lock; HILDA does NOT call `get_credential` for messenger). **(d) Gateway-mediated IP-allowlist + specified human id** — corp-internal gateway handles authentication via IP-allowlist + fixed-key infrastructure; **HILDA does NOT store any credential**; HILDA's adapter calls gateway APIs and SPECIFIES the corp human id (the human acting on behalf of HILDA — currently HUMAN; future could be BOT) as a parameter in API calls. Ph-1 use: **Corp PLM** (gateway handles auth; HILDA's `customizations/issue_tracker/<corp_plm_slug>_adapter_config.yaml` specifies only the gateway URL + the corp human id HILDA uses in its API calls; HILDA does NOT call `get_credential` for corp PLM). **API surface** (for patterns (a) + (b) only): `get_credential(identity: CredentialIdentity, system_type: str) -> Credential` where `CredentialIdentity` is a tagged union: **`ServiceAccount(service_slug: str)`** — resolves to pattern (a) file path; **`PerPerson(identity_id: str)`** where `identity_id` is the PM-id or HILDA-OPS-member-id — resolves to pattern (b) file path. The `system_type` parameter (e.g., `"google_drive"`, `"customer_jira"`, `"smtp"`) selects which credential within the file. Returns `Credential` (an opaque type that does NOT support `__str__` / `__repr__` that exposes the secret per NFR-2 scrubbing discipline). Raises `CredentialNotConfigured` (`CSA-E000` family per FR-20 lock) when the expected file is missing. Patterns (c) + (d) adapters do NOT call `get_credential` — they call their respective corp gateway APIs directly with gateway-handled auth. **Decryption timing** (per FR-19 / FR-25 / FR-50 lock cascade 2026-06-17): credentials are decrypted **on-demand at adapter instantiation time** (NOT at HILDA process startup). On-demand decryption: (i) reduces in-memory credential exposure (inactive PMs' credentials never enter memory in a HILDA process that doesn't use them); (ii) supports per-credential rotation without HILDA restart (re-instantiating the adapter picks up new credentials post-sops re-encrypt); (iii) parallel to the lazy-load pattern in adapter pools. **Process-memory residence**: decrypted credentials live in the calling adapter's instance scope and are NOT cached in a process-global store; adapter destruction releases the decrypted material. **No disk persistence**: decrypted plaintext is NEVER written to disk per NFR-2. **Credential-failure error code catalog** (cross-reference for ops triage; only patterns (a) + (b) have credential-class errors; patterns (c) + (d) have gateway-class errors which are NOT credential failures): **(a) FR-23 SMTP** (pattern (a)): `EML-E001` (SMTP credential expired); **(b) FR-19 customer adapter** (pattern (b)): `CSA-E000` (pre-flight file missing per FR-20) / `CSA-E001` (session-expired per FR-19); **(c) FR-25 (b) customer JIRA** (pattern (b)): `ITR-E003` (per-person credential expired). **NOT credential errors** (gateway-class — separate semantic): **(d) FR-25 (a) corp PLM** (pattern (d)): `ITR-E002` reframed 2026-06-17 as "corp PLM gateway error" (HTTP error from gateway — covers gateway-side auth/connectivity/IP-allowlist failures; NOT a HILDA-side credential issue); **(e) FR-50 corp messenger** (pattern (c)): `MSG-E001` reframed 2026-06-17 as "corp messenger gateway error" (HTTP error from gateway — covers gateway-side auth/connectivity failures; NOT a HILDA-side credential issue). All credential-failure events (patterns (a) + (b)) surface to `HildaOpsAlert` per FR-75 with **identity identifier (pm_id / service_slug) + system_type — NEVER the credential material per NFR-2**. All `retryable = false` (require ops intervention to re-encrypt sops env). Gateway-class errors (patterns (c) + (d)) similarly surface to `HildaOpsAlert` but with gateway URL + HTTP status code (no credential material involved). **NFR-2 compliance (no credential material in logs)**: per NFR-2, decrypted credential plaintext is NEVER written to: **(i)** any HILDA-internal log file (`CommunicationLog`, `HildaOpsAlert`, per-module debug logs); **(ii)** any SharePoint list column (HILDA NEVER writes credential material to SP per FR-2 R&R lock + NFR-2); **(iii)** any dashboard render (FR-57 / FR-59 / FR-60 / FR-61 / FR-87 / FR-62 surfaces NEVER include credentials); **(iv)** any persistent disk location (NSD, Postgres, HILDA PC local cache); **(v)** any stack trace / error message surfaced to TPM (adapter error renderers MUST scrub credential fields before surfacing per FR-19 / FR-25 / FR-50 individual locks). Violations are caught at code-review per the `[D-038]` deployment discipline + by automated linters scanning for known credential patterns in log statements (Ph-3+ Vault integration adds runtime credential-material redaction at the logger boundary; Ph-1/Ph-2 relies on developer discipline + code review). **Anchor history**: `[D-019]` v1 lock (initial sops-encrypted env file decision); `[D-026]` v2 amendment (deployment-mechanism refinements); `[D-038]` v3 amendment (canonical path conventions + 4-pattern identity-model discipline locked 2026-06-17). Ph-3+: PM registration UI, Vault integration, OAuth2 refresh, per-credential `expires_at` for proactive detection of credential expiry — out of Ph-1/Ph-2 scope.

- ~~**FR-33**~~ — *(deferred 2026-05-04 → DEF-14: Vault/AES-256 centralised secrets store — Ph-3)*
- ~~**FR-34**~~ — *(deferred 2026-05-04 → DEF-14: per-request in-memory decryption boundary — Ph-3)*
- ~~**FR-35**~~ — *(deferred 2026-05-04 → DEF-14: secrets store pod isolation + mTLS — Ph-3)*
- ~~**FR-36**~~ — *(deferred 2026-05-04 → DEF-14: OAuth2 health monitor + token refresh — Ph-3)*
- ~~**FR-37**~~ — *(deferred 2026-05-04 → DEF-14: PM credential revocation UI — Ph-3)*
- ~~**FR-38**~~ — *(deferred 2026-05-04 → DEF-14: auto-association + re-association on PM reassignment — Ph-3)*

### Templates & three-tier configuration

- **FR-39** `[Ph-1]` — **UPDATED 2026-06-17 — template path verification pending per STATUS Flag; SIGHUP reload workflow per FR-2 cascade; FR-2 R&R lock explicit cross-ref; Ph-1 TPM SP UI capability inventory cross-ref**. Customer templates are authored and maintained by the **HILDA ops team** as YAML files; templates are **not** exposed to TPM for direct editing. **Template file location**: per FR-40 single-template structure (verification pending — STATUS.md Flag '`customizations/template_schemas/` path verification' 2026-06-17). Authoritative path is `<canonical_template_path_TBD>` to be locked during architecture-phase template.yaml location decision. Original wording `customizations/template_schemas/<customer_id>/` (Option A per `[D-042]` leaning) may or may not match the current FR-40 lock per `[D-077]` final amendment (`customer.yaml` DROPPED entirely); verify against architecture-phase resolution. Independent of `customizations/rules/` (rule YAMLs canonical) per FR-30 lock 2026-06-17. **Template change workflow** (per FR-2 SIGHUP cascade + `[D-025]` bind-mount): (a) ops edits the YAML file; (b) ops `git commit` + push the change; (c) bind-mount picks up the change at the file-system level per `[D-025]`; (d) **ops sends `SIGHUP` to the HILDA process** (per FR-2 line 54 — HILDA reads template.yaml ONLY at startup and on SIGHUP); (e) HILDA re-reads template.yaml, refreshing its in-memory primary-key tuple set + structural metadata; (f) the next SP UI engineer `setup_milestone` invocation per FR-2 R&R lock uses the updated tuples to create new milestone rows. **No HILDA process restart required for template.yaml changes** (only SIGHUP). Cross-ref: STATUS.md Flag 'HILDA SIGHUP handler implementation confirmation' 2026-06-15 — if HILDA's actual reload mechanism is CLI command / HTTP admin endpoint / file watcher instead of SIGHUP, replace 'SIGHUP' uniformly across FR-2 + FR-30 + FR-39 + FR-56 (a). **Setup_milestone role** (per FR-2 R&R lock): in Ph-1 the row set is fixed at `setup_milestone` time — **SP UI engineer's `setup_milestone` task is the sole SP row-creation path**; HILDA NEVER creates SP rows per `[D-077]` final amendment. `setup_milestone` reads the template.yaml tuples (via HILDA-side template resolution or SP UI engineer's own parsing — verify during architecture phase) and creates the corresponding `Deliverables_<customer_id>` rows per FR-5 + the FR-78 default work-item per milestone. Template changes only manifest as new SP rows when ops triggers `setup_milestone` for a new milestone after HILDA's SIGHUP reload (see workflow above). **TPM day-to-day adjustments to live trackers**: per the Ph-1 TPM SP UI capability inventory locked 2026-06-17 in STATUS.md (during FR-31 review), TPM has: (a) limited cell edits per FR-14 lock (`review_required` / `email_cc_list` / `comment` / `tracking_modality` / `manual_triage_required` clear / `delivery_state` for 2 reserved use cases); (b) per-row buttons (Approve / Send Reminder per FR-65 / Mark Closed / Clear Triage Flag / Confirm Carrier Acceptance / Resume from Delayed-Blocked); (c) milestone-level buttons (Start Collection / Submit to Carrier / Close All Items / Refresh / Download Package). **Add/remove items deferred Ph-2** via FR-3 / DEF-22; **owner change deferred Ph-2** via FR-3 / DEF-22 + FR-14 Option B; **rule pause/customize/trigger deferred Ph-2** via FR-31. In Ph-1, structural changes (template-level edits) require ops template.yaml workflow per above. `[Ph-2]` the HILDA ops team may additionally use the Template Schema Ingestor `[D-010]` to generate the initial YAML template from a customer-provided Excel schema (deferred — DEF-15, consistent with FR-1 path b); `[Ph-4]` TPM self-service template authoring wizard (DEF-11) is the long-term target; YAML schema definition, file-based loader implementation, and ops workflow to be formalised during architecture — see `[D-042]`.
- **FR-40** `[Ph-1]` — Customer templates define standard milestones (with DeliveryItems grouped by `tg_name`) and device list with per-device metadata. **Single YAML file per customer at `customizations/template_schemas/<customer_id>/template.yaml`** — milestones at customer level, NOT under device (work-items per carrier don't change per device). **SP-side mapping**: `customizations/sharepoint_config/customers/<customer_id>.yaml` per `[D-065]` defines the **canonical → SP-internal column name** mapping for the per-customer `Deliverables_<customer_id>` Work Items list AND the global `Milestones` list (one mapping file per customer; mappings cover every canonical field HILDA uses — customer-, device-, milestone-, TG-, work-item-level). Per `[D-065]` three-tier name model: HILDA-canonical → SP-internal → SP display. **SP architecture** (per `[D-083]`): HILDA's runtime SP coupling has two SP lists per customer scope:
  - **`Deliverables_<customer_id>`** — per-customer Work Items list (one row per DeliveryItem; ~60 columns).
  - **`Milestones`** — single global list (one row per milestone across all customers; 15 columns; scoped by `carrier` column).
  - **`Projects`** — existing global list reused; `project_id → TPM` (= `assigned_pm_id`) lookup.

  **SP write target** (per `[D-083]`): SP UI engineer's `setup_milestone` task is the sole SP row-creation path per FR-2 R&R lock; HILDA NEVER creates SP rows. The YAML hierarchy below is the logical structural authority. SP storage layout per `[D-083]`:
  - **`Deliverables_<customer_id>`** rows carry per-DeliveryItem fields PLUS denormalized customer / device / milestone identity columns (`customer_id`, `project_id`, `project_model`, `milestone_id`, `milestone_name`) for SP-alert routing per FR-84.
  - **`Milestones`** row (one per milestone; global list) carries milestone-level state (`status`, `target_date`, `download_package_*`, milestone-level button timestamps) — NOT denormalized on Deliverables rows.
  - **`Projects`** list (global; reused) provides `project_id → TPM` (= `assigned_pm_id`) lookup; `assigned_pm_id` is NOT denormalized on Deliverables rows.

  **Hierarchical structure** (template.yaml):

  ```yaml
  <customer_id>:                              # e.g., VZW — YAML map key; `carrier` column on Milestones row + `customer_id` denormalized on Deliverables rows
    customer_jira_url: <default>              # HILDA-only (template.yaml at startup per `[D-083]`); NOT in SP per FR-25 (b) lock
    # ---- Milestone definitions (shared across all devices for this customer) ----
    <milestone_id>:                           # e.g., LE-2 — Milestones row `Id` + denormalized on Deliverables rows as `milestone_id`
      milestone_name: <default>               # Milestones row `Title`; denormalized on Deliverables rows as `milestone_name`
      status: <default>                       # Milestones row `status` column (4-value enum per FR-6); HILDA transitions via `[D-064]` REST writeback
      target_date: <default>                  # Milestones row `target_date` column; sole authoritative deadline per `[D-085]`; HILDA reads at runtime for FR-11 DeadlineProximity + polling_schedule evaluations; NO per-item denormalization
      <work-item-1>:                          # work-items shared across all devices in this customer/milestone — each becomes a Deliverables_<customer_id> row per (device, work-item) pair
        item_name: <default>                  # Deliverables row `Title`
        item_no: <int>                        # per-milestone work-item number; same value across devices for the same logical work-item
        item_path_id: <default>               # NSD path component per FR-13
        milestone_gating: <bool>              # Deliverables row `milestone_gating` Boolean
        owner_corp_usa_email: <default>       # free-form text per `[D-086]`
        owner_corp_email: <default>           # free-form text per `[D-086]`
        owner_corp_id: <default>              # free-form text per `[D-086]`
        owner_name: <default>                 # free-form text per `[D-086]`
        tg_name: <string>                     # TG membership; hardcoded enum LIST per SP UI engineer per `[D-083]`
        doc_count: <int>
        review_required: <bool>
        tracking_modality: [<modality>, ...]
        customer_delivery_modality: <modality>
        customer_delivery_info: <string>      # e.g., target Google Drive URL
        no_customer_upload: <bool>
        item_description: <tag-list>          # FR-82 (ops-only edit)
        email_cc_list: [<email>, ...]
        force_tracking_enabled: <bool>        # per FR-81 effective tracking_enabled override
        handset / tablet / wearable / ir / osmr / rmr / hmr_smr: <bool>   # 7 form-factor flags per `[D-084]`
        # Denormalized TG fields per `[D-051]` — same TG metadata repeats across all items sharing the same tg_name in the milestone.
        # All emails + identifiers free-form text per `[D-086]` (no AD resolution / Person-Group SP fields / auto-derivation).
        # TG fields dropped: `tracking_modality_tg`, `default_cc_list`, `tracking_enabled` (per FR-56 (a)); `tg_owner_corp_email` (never existed per `[D-086]`).
        tg_path_id: <default>
        tg_email_group_alias: <email>         # free-form text per `[D-086]`
        tg_owner_corp_usa_email: <email>      # free-form text per `[D-086]`
        tg_owner_corp_id: <id>                # free-form text per `[D-086]`
        tg_owner_name: <display>              # free-form text per `[D-086]`
        ingress_nsd: none|nsd1|nsd2
        folder_routing_enabled: <bool>
        corp_id_list: [<id>, ...]
      <work-item-2>: ...
    <milestone_id_2>: ...
    # ---- Device list (per-device metadata) ----
    <device_id>:                              # e.g., SM-S901U — `project_model` (= Model column) on Milestones row + denormalized on Deliverables rows
      # `assigned_pm_id` lookup at runtime via Projects SP list (`project_id → TPM`); NOT in template.yaml
      # `project_id` SP-side numeric assigned at `setup_milestone` time; NOT in template.yaml
    <device_id_2>: ...
  ```
  No Deliverable intermediate level (anchors `[D-028]`). `[Ph-3+]` template versioning (`template_version` at top of `template.yaml`) deferred — Ph-1/Ph-2 neither HILDA nor SP UI engineer consumes a version field. **Three-tier name model invariant** (per `[D-065]` + `[D-046]`): template.yaml uses **HILDA-canonical field names** (matching `core/src/template_schema/models.py` Pydantic field names) — NOT SP-internal column names. HILDA reads template.yaml directly using canonical names; no translation layer needed. Translation from HILDA-canonical → SP-internal column names (which include SharePoint's 32-char internal-name truncations such as `download_package_request_timesta` for `download_package_request_timestamp`) is owned by `customizations/sharepoint_config/customers/<customer_id>.yaml` per `[D-065]`. SP UI engineer reads BOTH files at `setup_milestone` time (template.yaml for VALUES; sharepoint_config.yaml for the mapping). Authority file for SP-side schema is **`docs/sp_ui_engineer/SP_lists_authoritative.xlsx`**. **`project_id` is NOT in template.yaml** — SP UI engineer assigns at `setup_milestone` time as the Milestones-row `project_id` column AND denormalized on Deliverables rows; HILDA reads from these denormalized columns at runtime per FR-84 5-field routing key resolution. **`assigned_pm_id` is NOT in template.yaml** — looked up at runtime via the Projects SP list (`project_id → TPM`). **`customer_jira_url` is NOT in SP** — kept in template.yaml; HILDA reads at startup per FR-25 (b) lock. **Work-items live at customer/milestone level**, NOT under device — same work-item set applies to every (device, milestone) combination at `setup_milestone` time per "work-items per carrier don't change per device" rule; same logical work-item shares the same `item_no` across devices in a milestone per `[D-083]`. **TG-field repetition discipline**: TG metadata fields are denormalized per-work-item but MUST be identical across all items sharing the same `tg_name` within a milestone — HILDA validates at template load and raises `TSC-W005` on divergence. **All SP email / identifier columns are STR free-form text** per `[D-086]` — no corp AD resolution, no Person-Group SP fields, no auto-derivation; HILDA's owner-email preference rule (per FR-9 + `[D-080]`) survives (use `owner_corp_usa_email` if set; else `owner_corp_email`); only the AD-validation + auto-derivation aspects are removed. Exact YAML schema formalised during architecture — see `[D-042]`.
- **FR-41** `[Ph-1]` — Configuration overrides apply at three runtime tiers — Global / Customer / Device — without code change or redeploy; onboarding a new customer = new YAML template file under `customizations/template_schemas/<customer_id>/` + new SP config YAML under `customizations/sharepoint_config/` (ops operation, bind-mount update per `[D-025]`, no container rebuild or redeploy); onboarding a new device model = new or updated YAML template file for that customer (ops operation).

### Audit & runtime diagnostics

- **FR-42** `[Ph-1]` — Every external action (email send, message post, issue create/update, customer-system call, credential retrieval / refresh / use) is recorded in `CommunicationLog` with attribution to the originating PM, target system, action type, and DeliveryItem reference; credential material is never logged.
- **FR-49** `[Ph-1]` — Every functional module exposes a `--diagnostic` mode runnable in production (`python -m core.src.<module>.<module>_cli --diagnostic`) that emits a compact RPT report of the module's runtime state without restarting the service — usable by ops to inspect a live deployment and shareable in chat for joint diagnosis (anchors `[D-002]` `[D-005]`).
- ~~**FR-43** — Every functional module emits compact RPT / MET / FIX / QC reports per `[D-002]` containing only counts, status flags, and bounded enum tokens — no proprietary content (test report fragments, tech report prose, waiver text, customer feedback, R&D reply prose, customer-system payloads, or PM credentials).~~ (moved 2026-05-01: reclassified as NFR-17 — chat-mediated collaboration invariant.)
- ~~**FR-44** — Every service / module failure raises a registered error code from the central `error_codes.py` registry in the format `{MODULE}-{E|W}{NNN}` per `[D-002]`.~~ (moved 2026-05-01: reclassified as NFR-18 — chat-mediated collaboration invariant.)
- ~~**FR-45** — Every functional module ships `<module>_cli.py` with `--diagnostic` (emits compact reports) and, for side-effect-bearing modules, `--mock` / `--dry-run`; every UI / web-facing module ships a mock web harness exercising it without production SharePoint access per `[D-005]`.~~ (split 2026-05-01: runtime `--diagnostic` for ops + RPT emission → FR-49; dev/test `--mock` / `--dry-run` + mock web harness → NFR-19.)

## Non-functional

### Data sensitivity & boundary

- **NFR-1** — All HILDA services run on-premises; no public-cloud LLM and no SaaS LLM calls (anchors `[D-007]`).
- **NFR-2** — Compact reports, error messages, and logs that leave the on-prem environment contain no proprietary content (anchors `[D-002]`); negative tests verify the invariant for every artifact type.

### Credential & security

- **NFR-3** — Per-PM credential isolation — each PM's credentials are stored under their own path in the secrets store; Ph-1 and Ph-2 secrets store = sops-encrypted `.env` files partitioned by PM (e.g. `/etc/hilda/pm_<pm_id>.env`) per `[D-038]`; no cross-PM credential access by the application or by ops; HashiCorp Vault or equivalent centralised secrets management service is a Ph-3+ target (anchors DEF-14).
- **NFR-4** — Credential material is encrypted at rest via `sops` (AES-256-GCM, `age`-based key per `[D-038]`) — `.env` files on the host are sops-encrypted; the credential service decrypts at startup into process memory; the age private key at `/etc/hilda/age.key` (`chmod 400`, HILDA service user only) is the only plaintext secret on the filesystem; in transit, all external communications (SharePoint, PLM, customer JIRA, email server) use TLS; per-request in-memory decryption boundary (~~FR-34~~), secrets store pod isolation (~~FR-35~~), and service-to-service mTLS are Ph-3 MicroK8s targets per `[D-021]`, `[D-026]`, and DEF-14 — Ph-1/Ph-2 use a single startup-time decrypt into process memory.

### PM approval & accountability

- **NFR-5** — No customer-facing outbound action — submission, post-to-customer-comment, customer email, customer adapter call — is executed without an explicit PM-approval signal that is recorded in `CommunicationLog`.
- **NFR-6** — Every external action is attributable to a specific PM (no service-account actions); `CommunicationLog` is append-only and complete.

### SharePoint constraint

- **NFR-7** — SharePoint deployment-specific values (site URLs, list internal names, lookup field IDs, library paths) live exclusively in `customizations/sharepoint_config/` and are loaded at startup; `core/` contains no hard-coded SharePoint instance values (anchors `[D-004]`).
- **NFR-8** — SharePoint integration uses the SharePoint REST API + on-prem AD auth (NTLM) against SharePoint 2017; integration scope is List CRUD + classic web parts only — binary owner deliverables are stored in the HILDA NSD internal tree (`\\NSD1\internal\<carrier>\<device>\<milestone>\` per FR-13) as source of truth for in-progress work (FR-13, `[D-040]`, `[D-041]`); PLM receives copies per phase: Ph-1 immediate upload for Email and NSD ingest sources; Ph-2 deferred until post-dispatch via FR-68; CorporatePLM-sourced documents are already in PLM and are never re-uploaded; PLM is source of truth for submitted deliverables only (post-dispatch); HILDA-generated internal artifacts (QC reports, diagnostics) are stored on the shared network drive `outbound/` per FR-13; neither owner deliverables nor HILDA-generated artifacts are stored in SharePoint Document Libraries (anchors `[D-006]`).

### Latency & reliability

- **NFR-9** — DeliveryItem state changes propagate from owner action to PM dashboard within one poll interval + processing time; webhook / push is preferred and delivers within 60 s; for polled channels (Email FR-23, PLM FR-26, NSD FR-55), propagation latency equals the effective deadline-tiered `polling_schedule` interval at the time of the action — far from deadline the interval is longer (acceptable; no active submission pressure); near the deadline (≤ 1 day tier) the interval is 5–15 min target; the 60 s guarantee applies only to webhook/push-delivered events. **Architecture design notes — must address during architecture phase:**
  1. **Async LLM tasks** — FR-52 tier-2, FR-12 path(c), FR-53, FR-54 must execute as Celery background tasks and must never block a state-change event; the state transition (DocumentReceived, OwnerStatusConfirmed) fires and propagates within the polling-interval SLA; the LLM result (AIReviewResult) arrives as a subsequent async event and updates the HILDA-rendered document section per `[D-074]` independently (review findings surface in the HILDA tab; the SP UI review_status flag updates via the standard `[D-064]` writeback); architecture must enforce this decoupling at the task-dispatch boundary.
  2. **Submission assembly progress** — FR-18 package assembly (NSD download + carrier upload) is minutes-scale for large milestones, not sub-60 s; SP UI must show immediate "in progress" feedback on Submit to Carrier click; progress granularity = per-file (`files_done / total_files × 100`, split across download phase and upload phase); progress state written to Redis by the Celery task; HILDA API exposes `GET /milestone/{id}/submission/progress`; SP web part polls at 5 s intervals until completion; individual document downloads (FR-61) stream directly from NSD with `Content-Length` set — browser handles per-file progress natively; bulk document download (Ph-2+) uses async ZIP assembly + dashboard notification with one-time download link.
  3. **Polling channel latency** — Email (FR-23), PLM (FR-26), NSD (FR-55) share a single `polling_schedule` AutomationRule; the effective interval for a milestone is the shortest tier applicable across any of its active items; polling ceases per FR-74 collection phase closure; propagation latency from owner action to PM dashboard is bounded by the effective tier interval — not a flat fixed value.
- **NFR-10** — All external service calls — Email (FR-23), PLM/IssueTracker (FR-25/FR-26), NSD SMB (FR-13/FR-55), corp messenger (FR-50), CustomerJIRA (FR-25), on-prem LLM (hilda-llm-gateway / FR-53), SharePoint REST (NFR-8), and carrier portal adapter (FR-18/FR-19) — must never fail silently; every failure produces a registered error code per `[D-002]` and routes per FR-75; retry policy by failure class: **(a) Transient** (network timeout, 5xx, service momentarily down) — exponential backoff with jitter: base 30 s, max 30 min, max 5 retries; after max retries exhausted raise error code and route per FR-75; **(b) Rate-limited** (429/throttled) — respect `Retry-After` header if present; otherwise same exponential backoff as (a); **(c) Auth failure — transient** (AD/NTLM unreachable) — exponential backoff as per (a); **(d) Non-recoverable** (400, 404, data corruption, permanent 403) — no retry; raise registered error code immediately; Email Service polls or receives 24/7; polling interval for Email, PLM, and NSD is deadline-tiered per the shared `polling_schedule` AutomationRule (FR-23, FR-26, FR-55); auth credential failures route per NFR-11.
- **NFR-11** — When a PM credential is expired or missing, the dependent automation step is queued (not lost) and the PM is alerted via the dashboard and an out-of-band channel; the queue-and-alert pattern (step queued, not lost, human alerted) generalises to all failure classes requiring human resolution: **(a) PM credential expired/missing** — step queued, PM alerted (this NFR); **(b) document classification permanently ambiguous** (`staged/` hold per FR-52) — item remains `DocumentReceived`, PM flagged on dashboard for manual resolution; **(c) on-prem LLM gateway down** — FR-53 review tasks queued, `HildaOpsAlert` fired; items with `review_required = true` and `review_status = pending` remain blocked at `OwnerClosed` guard condition (2) until LLM recovers — TPM may unblock by setting `review_required = false` via FR-14; **(d) version-selection timeout** `[Ph-2 FR-66]` — after configurable max-retry corp messenger attempts without owner response, PM dashboard flag for manual version override via FR-14; **(e) FR-68 PLM sync loop non-convergence** — after configurable max-retry cap, PM dashboard flag + `HildaOpsAlert`; **(f) Celery beat missed schedules on restart** — beat resumes from current time; missed fires not replayed; acceptable for reminder/escalation rules (next scheduled fire covers it); time-critical one-shot events must be Celery tasks, not beat schedules.

### Adapter pattern & build-time data boundary

- **NFR-12** — The dev LLM has no access to proprietary REST API specs (`[D-003]`), proprietary customer-template Excel schemas (`[D-010]`), or proprietary historical test reports (`[D-011]`); all three classes of input are processed exclusively by their on-prem Ingestors / Profiler using an open-source LLM.
- **NFR-13** — Generated proprietary adapters, per-customer template artifacts, and per-customer test-report parsers live exclusively under `customizations/`; `core/` contains only the typed Protocols, the Ingestors / Profiler themselves, and public-vendor adapters (anchors `[D-001]` `[D-003]` `[D-010]` `[D-011]`).

### Extensibility

- **NFR-14** — The data model supports adding new item types, tracking modalities, customer delivery modalities, and delivery states via configuration without schema migrations (anchors §3.3 extensibility intent).

### High availability

- **NFR-15** — Ph-1 and Ph-2 run on bare-metal Docker Compose (per `[D-026]`) — one HILDA application image launched as four containers (hilda-api, hilda-worker, hilda-beat, hilda-llm-gateway) plus postgres and redis = 6 containers total; all containers configured with `restart: unless-stopped`; HA replicas and MicroK8s deployment are Ph-3 targets per `[D-021]`, `[D-026]`, and `[D-043]`; Temporal Workers are deferred to Ph-3 per `[D-022]`; Ph-1/Ph-2 accept single-instance restart-recovery semantics — in-flight tasks may fail and retry on container restart; concurrent-replica HA is not provided.

### Shared network drive boundary

- **NFR-16** — Reads from the shared network drive (FR-13) go exclusively through the HILDA-mediated download endpoint, which authenticates the requestor via on-prem AD; Ph-1 grants access to any authenticated corp AD user — per-DeliveryItem ACL is deferred to DEF-18; writes go exclusively as the dedicated `hilda-svc` AD service account from the HILDA host's SMB mount; direct UNC paths are not exposed to PMs and are not embedded in any HTML rendered by `core/`.

### Chat-mediated collaboration invariants

- **NFR-17** — Every functional module emits compact RPT / MET / FIX / QC reports per `[D-002]` containing only counts, status flags, and bounded enum tokens — no proprietary content (test report fragments, tech report prose, waiver text, customer feedback, R&D reply prose, customer-system payloads, or PM credentials); negative tests verify the invariant for every artifact type.
- **NFR-18** — Every service / module failure raises a registered error code from the central `error_codes.py` registry in the format `{MODULE}-{E|W}{NNN}` per `[D-002]`; codes are stable across deployments and serve as the keys for runtime alerts, runbooks, and chat-shared diagnostics.

### Test interface invariants

- **NFR-19** — Every functional module that performs side-effect operations ships `--mock` and `--dry-run` test modes routing to fixtures / null-sinks without external IO; every UI / web-facing module ships a mock web harness exercising it against mock SharePoint List data without production-environment access (anchors `[D-005]`).

### Task idempotency & recovery

- **NFR-21** — **HILDA-SP runtime coupling discipline** (extracted from FR-2 per `[D-077]` / `[D-071]`). HILDA's Linux service layer runtime SP coupling is constrained as follows: **(a) Read scope = N per-carrier SP lists** (one per customer; `Deliverables_<customer_id>`) — HILDA reads each list via REST `$filter` on column values; no PK navigation, no cross-list joins. Users + PMCredentials SP lists are NOT used at runtime (User identity is denormalized onto each work-item row per FR-88; PMCredentials live in HILDA-local `credential_service` per `[D-019]`). **(b) Write scope = same N per-carrier lists, in-place column updates only** — HILDA writes to specific row columns on already-existing rows in `Deliverables_<customer_id>` (per `[D-064]` REST writeback). Milestone-scoped writes (e.g., `Milestone.status` per FR-6, `download_package_*` per FR-73) are duplicated across all rows in the milestone — HILDA updates each row's column to the same value in a multi-row write. **(c) HILDA NEVER creates SP rows** — at any time, under any condition. SP row creation is exclusively SP UI engineer's `setup_milestone` task. HILDA = state transitions + audit-field writes on already-existing rows; never `SpCrud.create_item`. **(d) HILDA does NOT mirror SP rows in its own Postgres per `[D-071]`** — the `storage` module owns ONLY `DocumentIndexRow` + `DocumentItemAssociation` + `CommunicationLog` + `AutomationRuleOverride` + `TGFolderRoutingRow` + `TagCatalogRow`. Runtime DeliveryItem queries always go through `sharepoint_integration.SpCrud.get_items` (with REST `$filter`) to SP — SP is the canonical store for all entity state. **(e) Source-of-truth pattern**: each `Deliverables_<customer_id>` list is the canonical store for everything HILDA tracks for that customer at runtime; HILDA does NOT maintain a YAML cache for customer/device/milestone data (no `customer.yaml` file). Column-value edits via ops SP UI or TPM SP UI → SP-alert fires → HILDA invalidates row-scoped cache → next alert refetches. **(f) Cache discipline**: per-(customer, device, milestone) row fetches are cached for the duration of a single SP-alert dispatch (~5–30 s) to amortize batched per-item alerts within the same milestone (mechanism per FR-84 resolution path). **(g) Write amplification + cascade dedup**: milestone-scoped writes touch N rows (one per work-item in the milestone); the inverse-direction read pattern (SP UI engineer's web part writing the same milestone-level column to N rows on a button click) also produces N SP-alerts to HILDA — HILDA deduplicates via FR-11 / `[D-082]` cascade-dedup pattern (process the first alert; ignore the rest within the burst). (Ph-2 plan: N SP lists with FK chains per `[D-077]`.) Anchors: `[D-064]` (HILDA→SP REST writeback), `[D-071]` (caller-resolves discipline), `[D-073]` (SP UI engineer manual provisioning), `[D-077]` (per-carrier flat-list coupling); `[D-019]` (PMCredentials in credential_service).

- **NFR-20** — All Celery tasks are idempotent — re-executing a task after a worker crash, Redis restart, or network partition must produce the same result as executing it once; requirements: **(a) `acks_late = True`** on all tasks — broker re-queues the task before acknowledgement so a worker crash cannot silently drop an in-flight task; **(b) all external writes check "already done"** before acting using a stable natural key: PLM issue creation checks `plm_id` already set on the DeliveryItem; SP List updates check current `delivery_state` before overwriting; document index writes use `(delivery_item_id, doc_type, doc_id_slug, rev_number)` as the natural key; email sends check `CommunicationLog` for a matching `(batch_id, action_type)` entry; **(c) Redis AOF persistence required** — without append-only file persistence the Celery broker queue is lost on Redis crash, causing all in-flight tasks to be silently dropped; AOF must be enabled on the Redis container in the Docker Compose configuration; **(d) all retry attempts emit a registered `WFL-W` warning code** per `[D-002]` so retry storms are visible in Prometheus dashboards (per `[D-023]`) and do not accumulate silently.

### Version selection, PLM sync, and portal structure

- **FR-66** `[Ph-2]` — When the `OwnerClosed` transition fires (FR-7 / FR-28) and multiple revisions exist for any `doc_id_slug` under a DeliveryItem, HILDA triggers the corp messenger version-selection workflow as the transient fork from `OwnerClosed`: HILDA sends the owner a corp messenger message listing all revisions for the conflicting `doc_id_slug(s)` (rev number, `original_filename`, upload timestamp) and asks the owner to select the final version; the owner's reply (by revision number or filename) is parsed and the selected revision's `is_final` flag is set to `true` in the document index; all other revisions for that `doc_id_slug` have `is_final` set to `false`; after all version selections are confirmed, the DeliveryItem advances from `OwnerClosed` (transient) to `UnderPMReview`; if the owner does not respond within a configurable timeout, the PM is notified on the dashboard and the item remains in `OwnerClosed` transient hold pending resolution; TPM may manually set `is_final` directly from the `UnderPMReview` SP UI (FR-56) as an override path; `[Ph-1]` only `rev1` exists per document — `is_final = true` is auto-set for rev1 of each document and the item advances directly to `UnderPMReview` without any owner selection step.

- **FR-67** `[Ph-2]` — When HILDA's post-dispatch deferred PLM upload (triggered by FR-18 dispatch → `SubmittedToCustomer`) uploads the `is_final` revision to PLM and detects that a stale attachment for the same `doc_id_slug` already exists in the PLM issue, HILDA initiates PLM stale file deletion; stale attachments arise in two scenarios: **(a) re-submission** — a prior dispatch deferred-uploaded an earlier `is_final` revision (e.g. `rev2`); TPM subsequently uploaded `rev3` via FR-62, re-approved and re-dispatched; the current deferred upload uploads `rev3` and `rev2` is now stale; **(b) CorporatePLM modality** — the owner uploaded the document directly to PLM; HILDA's deferred upload of the `is_final` revision creates a duplicate — the owner's original PLM attachment is stale; in both cases HILDA sends the owner a corp messenger message listing the stale attachment(s) and requests confirmation of deletion; upon owner confirmation, HILDA calls `deleteAttachment` on the IssueTracker adapter for each stale attachment and records the deletion in `CommunicationLog`; **`deleteAttachment` failure** — if `deleteAttachment` returns an error (e.g. HILDA's service account is not the file owner and lacks delete permission on the PLM system), the failure is flagged on the PM dashboard via FR-56 as `PLM stale attachment — delete failed: <filename>` and also logged to `HildaOpsAlert` (FR-75); this flag is visible to TPM regardless of the item's `delivery_state`; TPM must manually delete the stale attachment directly in PLM; **timeout** — if the owner does not confirm within a configurable timeout, the PM is notified and the item is flagged on the PM dashboard for manual resolution; `deleteAttachment` is a new operation added to the `IssueTracker` Protocol (see `[D-008]`) surfacing the PLM system's native attachment-delete capability.

- **FR-68** `[Ph-1]` — After a milestone's submission package is dispatched to the customer portal (FR-18), HILDA runs **two sync verification loops** for each DeliveryItem in the milestone: **(A) PLM-NSD sync** (existing): `[Ph-1]` HILDA polls the `(device, milestone, owner)` PLM issue per FR-5 plm_id grouping + FR-8 step 2 to verify that `rev1` (the single revision in Ph-1; `is_final = true` auto-set) is present in PLM as a current attachment; if not present, HILDA uploads `rev1` from the NSD classified path to PLM via the IssueTracker adapter and re-verifies; `[Ph-2]` approved revision is the `is_final = true` revision selected via FR-66; if not present in PLM, HILDA uploads the approved revision from NSD and re-verifies; if stale attachments (non-final revisions) remain in PLM, FR-67 stale deletion is triggered. **(B) PLM-Carrier hash sync** (anchors `[D-054]`): for every individual file uploaded to the carrier portal, HILDA recomputes `file_hash` and verifies that hash exists in PLM — either as a top-level attachment OR as a member of a zip attachment on PLM (extracted+hashed at verification time); non-match emits `ITR-W003 — PLM-Carrier hash mismatch on file '{filename}' item '{item_id}'`; PM dashboard surfaces for manual reconciliation. **Both phases**: verification result (pass / fail / pending) is written to `CommunicationLog` and surfaced on the PM dashboard. Both verification loops run as Celery tasks per `(milestone_id, hilda_dispatch_run_id)` at a configurable interval and stop when all items are verified or a maximum retry count is reached. Items with `no_customer_upload = true` are excluded from FR-68 sync entirely (no PLM or carrier upload occurred to verify).

- **FR-69** `[Ph-1]` — Each customer's carrier portal submission structure is defined in `customizations/<customer_id>/portal_structure.yaml`; this file is authored by PM team leads at customer onboarding and loaded by the customer adapter at assembly time (FR-18); the `portal_structure.yaml` schema is defined in `core/src/customer_adapter/MODULE.md`; the file lives in `customizations/` (customer-specific, not in `core/`) and is never read by the dev LLM directly; changes to portal structure require updating this file only — no code change in `core/`; the schema includes: **(a) folder / hierarchy layout** — carrier-side folder path or issue-field mappings per `doc_type` (e.g., Google Drive subfolder names, Jira attachment fields, FTP directory paths); **(b) `file_overwrite_policy: overwrite | rename`** — `overwrite` for carriers where uploading a file with an existing name replaces it (some Google Drive configs, SFTP with replace semantics); `rename` for carriers where previously uploaded files cannot be deleted or replaced (Jira attachments, most FTP sites, custom portals) — HILDA uploads the revised file with a distinct name derived from `revision_file_suffix_pattern`; **(c) `revision_file_suffix_pattern`** — used only when `file_overwrite_policy = rename`; a pattern applied to `original_filename` to produce the carrier-side filename for revised submissions; supports `{N}` (revision number) and `{timestamp}` (ISO date) tokens — e.g., `_rev{N}` yields `testreport_rev2.pdf`; `_{timestamp}` yields `testreport_20260519.pdf`; the `original_filename` in the HILDA document index is never modified — only the carrier-upload filename is affected.

- **FR-70** `[Ph-1]` — Each DeliveryItem has an `item_completion_pct` computed field (0–100 integer) representing progress towards document delivery and review completion. **`[Ph-1]` formula (document review progress):** `(count of test_report doc_id_slugs for this item whose rev1 row has non-null llm_review_findings) / doc_count × 100`; `doc_count` (from the template, per FR-7) is the denominator — it represents the expected number of test reports and is fixed at tracker creation; example: `doc_count = 3`, two test reports received and reviewed → `item_completion_pct = 67`; all three received and reviewed → `item_completion_pct = 100`; supplementary documents (`tech_report`, `waiver`) do not contribute to `item_completion_pct` — they are outside the `doc_count` definition. **`[Ph-2]` formula (test-case pass rate):** `(count of test cases whose status ≠ failed across the latest revision of all received test reports for this item) / (total test cases across the latest revision of all received test reports for this item) × 100` — numerator includes all non-failed outcomes (passed, non-applicable, not-supported, waived, and any other non-failed status value); if no test reports have been received, `item_completion_pct = 0`; this formula uses the parser result data produced by FR-16 and reflects actual test-case outcomes rather than document-review completion. **`Confirmation` items:** `item_completion_pct = 100` when `delivery_state ∈ {OwnerClosed, UnderPMReview, ReadyForSubmission, SubmittedToCustomer, Closed}`; `item_completion_pct = 0` before `OwnerClosed` (no documents expected — completion is signalled by owner confirmation). `item_completion_pct` is recomputed on every new document index write (new `doc_id_slug`, new revision, or new `llm_review_findings` write) and on every `delivery_state` change for `Confirmation` items. The field is persisted on the `DeliveryItem` SharePoint List row and is available in the milestone view (FR-56) for PM monitoring. Milestone-level `milestone_completion_pct` (FR-6) is the average of its child DeliveryItems' `item_completion_pct` values.

### Owner Discovery

- **FR-71** `[Ph-2]` — **REWRITTEN 2026-06-12 per FR-88 owner identity model** (specific field names replace generic `tg_owner`; TG fields are denormalized read-only mirrors on delivery-item rows per `[D-051]` impl note 2026-06-12). The Owner Discovery Function (ODF) fires automatically at tracker creation for each unique `tg_name` group in the milestone **where `tg_email_group_alias` is not set**; TGs with `tg_email_group_alias` set are self-organizing — ODF is suppressed entirely for those TGs; per-item owner field overrides by TPM (FR-14) are stored for PLM and NSD purposes but are not applied to email outreach for alias-enabled TGs; for non-alias TGs: **ODF sends one corp messenger message per TG group**, **routed to `tg_owner_corp_id`** (the corp messenger gateway's primary delivery identifier; sourced from customer YAML at tracker creation per FR-2 — denormalized read-only mirror on each delivery-item row in the TG per `[D-051]` impl note), **personalized with `tg_owner_name` in salutation** (e.g., "Dear `<tg_owner_name>`,") — `tg_owner_name` is the free-form text display name typed by TPM per FR-88 + `[D-086]`. **TG-owner identity overrides**: in Ph-1, ops edits `customizations/template_schemas/<customer_id>/template.yaml` (TG-coordinator fields denormalized per-work-item per FR-40 2026-06-14 rewrite — change every work-item sharing the affected `tg_name`); HILDA propagates via tracker rewriting delivery-item rows for the affected TG; SP-side TG editing UI deferred to Ph-2 per `customizations/sharepoint_config/MODULE.md` 2026-06-12. The message lists all items in the TG grouped by their currently assigned per-item owner (`owner_corp_id` + `owner_name` free-form display name per `[D-086]`), showing each distinct owner's identifier and their associated items; when any item in the TG has its per-item `owner_corp_id == tg_owner_corp_id`, the message explicitly notes this and asks the TG owner to confirm they will submit those deliverables themselves; **response format** — two valid reply types, matched case-insensitively: (a) `confirmed` — no changes; all current item owners in this TG stand as-is (including any items where `owner_corp_id == tg_owner_corp_id`); (b) one or more `old_corp_id → new_corp_id` lines — for each line, HILDA reassigns all items in **this TG only** currently owned by `old_corp_id` to `new_corp_id`; an engineer does not work across TGs so reassignment scope is always within the TG group; matching accepts both `corp_id` and full email (corp_usa_email or corp_email per FR-88 preference rule), case-insensitive; multiple reassignment lines are processed independently; **after parsing response:** HILDA updates the per-item owner identity fields on all affected DeliveryItems (TG owner's response provides the new (corp_usa_email + corp_email + corp_id + name) per FR-88 model; HILDA writes back to SP delivery-item rows per `[D-064]`; no AD lookup per `[D-086]`), records each change in `CommunicationLog` with the TG owner's `tg_owner_corp_id` and the old→new mapping, and notifies PM with a per-TG change summary; a flag is surfaced on the PM dashboard for any TG where `owner_corp_id == tg_owner_corp_id` for at least one item — informational only, not a blocker; the TG owner is a fully valid delivery engineer for those items and participates in the normal outreach and document-tracking flow; **timeout:** if the TG owner does not respond within a configurable period, PM is flagged on the dashboard; tracker proceeds with template default owners; **SP UI:** the milestone view (FR-56) displays ODF status per TG group before Start Collection — `Confirmed`, `Updated (N engineers reassigned)`, `Timed out — using defaults`, or `Pending`; TPM may proceed with Start Collection regardless of ODF status; inbound ODF response parsing uses the corp messenger inbound infrastructure per FR-54.

### ZIP Ingestion

- **FR-72** `[Ph-2]` — **ZIP receipt and storage:** when a `.zip` file is received via any ingest channel (Email, NSD1, NSD2, PLM-source), HILDA stores the raw archive as an **audit-only artifact** in the NSD1 internal tree at `\\NSD1\internal\<carrier>\<device>\<milestone>\<tg_path_id>\_zip_audit\<original_zip_filename>.zip`, co-located with its extracted contents at `_zip_audit/<original_zip_filename>/<extracted-tree>/` (anchors `[D-050]` impl note 2026-05-28). `original_zip_filename` is preserved as received. **ZIP files are never uploaded to PLM or carrier portal** — they exist purely as HILDA-side audit/provenance artifacts (anchors `[D-054]`). For PLM-source zips, the zip stays on PLM unchanged (HILDA never modifies PLM); HILDA extracts locally for routing + carrier upload + classified-tree write for audit consistency. **Extraction:** HILDA extracts all files from the ZIP recursively (nested zips are extracted in turn until no zips remain); for quality review purposes (FR-53), the following file types are eligible for AI review: `.xlsx`, `.xlsm`, `.xls`, `.pdf`, `.doc`, `.docx`, `.ppt`, `.pptx`; files of other types are stored and submitted to the carrier portal as-is but are not passed to the AI review pipeline — logged and added to the `HildaOpsAlert` queue as an informational note; there is no restriction on file type for carrier portal submission — every individual file extracted from a ZIP is submitted regardless of type. **Routing extracted files:** each extracted file is individually processed via FR-52's 5-step routing pipeline as if it arrived as a standalone attachment; every document produced from ZIP extraction records two additional fields in the HILDA document index: `from_zip: true` and `source_zip_filename: <original_zip_filename>` (ZIP filename as received from owner); extracted files are written to `<tg_path_id>/<item_path_id>/<doc_type_slug>/<doc_id_slug>/revN/` in the NSD1 internal tree per FR-13; for files that fall to D-039 Step 2 (LLM new-vs-revision classification), Ph-2 LLM resolves. **Version tree on individual files only** — zip-level versioning is irrelevant; `[D-039]` + `[D-048]` apply per extracted individual file; multiple zips containing files with overlapping names are processed independently. **Same-filename detection across ZIPs:** if a file within an incoming ZIP has an `original_filename` matching an existing document index entry for the same `(delivery_item_id, doc_type)`, HILDA emits `EML-W006 — duplicate filename '{filename}' across zips '{zip_a}' '{zip_b}' in TG '{tg}'; manual triage` for PM/TPM review; treatment is per D-039 pipeline in FR-52 (hash match → duplicate; slug match → revision; LLM-ambiguous → staged). **Unresolved routing:** if any extracted file cannot be routed to a DeliveryItem via FR-52's first four steps, it lands at FR-52 step 5 (staged → associated with milestone's default work-item per FR-78); `DocumentIndexRow.unrouted_source_path` captures the originating context (e.g., `<zip_filename>/<nested-folder-path>`); TPM resolves per FR-83. The ZIP archive remains in `_zip_audit/` regardless of whether all, some, or no files are successfully routed. All background processing faults (extraction errors, classification failures) route to `HildaOpsAlert` per FR-75; fine-grain routing to distinct TPM/support-team destinations is deferred: DEF-21. **Carrier submission**: only individual extracted files are submitted to the carrier portal (per FR-18 + FR-77 — zip files are never uploaded); `from_zip: true` documents follow the same FR-77 Type-2 routing as standalone files. **TG3-style fixed zip-internal folder structure**: TGs whose zips have fixed routable folder names (`folder_routing_enabled = True`) use the outermost folder name inside the zip as input to FR-52 step 3 source-folder template; see FR-52.

### Submission Package Download

- **FR-73** `[Ph-1]` — The SP milestone view (FR-56) surfaces a **Download Package** action available to PM and TPM; enabled when all DeliveryItems in the milestone are in `ReadyForSubmission` or `SubmittedToCustomer` state and at least one item is in `ReadyForSubmission` — identical guard to FR-63. The assembled file is named `<carrier_slug>_<device_id>_<milestone_slug>_package.zip`. Assembly failure is shown in the SP milestone view as UI feedback (PM-triggered action — result always surfaced in UI) and also logged to `HildaOpsAlert` for support team investigation (FR-75; DEF-21).

  **Two-click async flow**:
  - **(i) Click 1 — request**: PM/TPM clicks **Download Package** action in SP milestone view → button is wired to modify an SP list field per FR-84 (e.g., set `download_package_request_timestamp = NOW()`) → SP fires alert email → HILDA's `email_service` consumes via IMAP IDLE / short-poll → `sp_alert_parser` extracts the action verb + field deltas → dispatches `download_package` Celery task to `hilda-worker` (per `[D-021]` / `[D-022]`). SP UI immediately updates the same row to show "Package preparing..." status (replaces the Download Package button with a progress indicator).
  - **(ii) Async assembly** (`hilda-worker`, no user interaction): the Celery task iterates qualifying items per the Package content selection rule below, reads individual files from NSD1 classified path, constructs the folder hierarchy per FR-77 Type-2 routing, creates the `.zip` archive with internal folder structure replicating the carrier portal layout, and stores the zip at a HILDA-PC temporary cache with a scoped download token. **Per-click regeneration**: each click of Download Package triggers a fresh assembly; previous cached zip is discarded; ensures fresh milestone state on every download (no caching across clicks).
  - **(iii) Completion write-back**: when assembly completes, HILDA writes back to SP via SP REST API + NTLM per `[D-006]` (outbound is always allowed) — updates SP list fields on the milestone row: `download_package_url = https://hilda-proxy.corp/dl/<scoped_token>`, `download_package_status = "ready"`, `download_package_generated_at = NOW()`.
  - **(iv) SP UI detects completion via focus-aware refresh** (per `[D-074]`): HILDA's write-back in step (iii) updates `download_package_status = "ready"` and `download_package_url` on the single Milestones SP list row for the milestone (per `[D-083]`); SP web part re-reads the Milestones row on **focus-aware refresh** (tab/window focus-gain triggers SP-side re-fetch from SP — no JS polling of HILDA endpoints); when `download_package_status = "ready"` is observed on next refresh, the web part replaces the "Preparing..." indicator with a clickable **Download Now** link (which is the `download_package_url`). PM/TPM may force-refresh the SP tab if they prefer not to wait for focus-gain.
  - **(v) Click 2 — download**: PM/TPM clicks **Download Now** link → browser navigates to the scoped URL (routed through IT-admin's corp-side reverse proxy per FR-84) → HILDA's download endpoint authenticates the requestor via on-prem AD per NFR-16 → endpoint serves the cached `.zip` with `Content-Disposition: attachment` per FR-61 → browser downloads to the TPM's Downloads folder.
  - **(vi) Cache lifecycle**: the cached `.zip` is **deleted immediately after successful download** (file streamed to browser → deleted on stream completion); FR-76 `MilestoneStorageCleanup` provides a backstop for any cached zips that never completed download (e.g., TPM never clicked the link).
  - **(vii) Post-download** (no SP interaction): TPM manually extracts the `.zip` via Windows Explorer's "Extract All", 7-Zip, or any archive tool → folder hierarchy unfolds on local PC matching the carrier portal layout. The browser does not perform extraction — it only delivers the `.zip` to the TPM's Downloads folder; TPM-side extraction is a manual step.

  **Package mirrors FR-18 carrier-portal submission** (anchors `[D-053]`, `[D-054]`): individual files only (NO audit zips); folder structure per FR-77 Type-2 routing — matches the carrier portal target layout exactly. The zip's **internal folder hierarchy replicates the carrier portal layout** — `<target_folder>/<matched_source_folder>/<subtree>/<filename>` exactly as it is/would be on the carrier portal. **Package content selection** — scoped to `ReadyForSubmission` items where `item_type ≠ Confirmation` AND `no_customer_upload = false`: `[Ph-1]` all individual files at NSD1 classified path `<tg_path_id>/<item_path_id>/<doc_type_slug>/<doc_id_slug>/rev1/`; `[Ph-2]` individual files where `is_final = true` only — same selection rule as FR-18. **Excluded**: audit zips from `_zip_audit/` (PM accesses individual zip archives via FR-57 download endpoint if direct audit needed); default work-item documents (per FR-78 no-upload condition). **No state change** — downloaded package is for PM review and pre-submission verification only; state advancement and carrier portal submission remain exclusively via FR-63 / FR-18.


### Collection Phase Closure
- **FR-74** `[Ph-1]` — **Collection phase closure:** HILDA evaluates the milestone's collection status after every DeliveryItem state change; collection is considered closed when all DeliveryItems **except the default work-item** (FR-78) in the milestone are in `{ReadyForSubmission, SubmittedToCustomer, Closed}` — the default work-item is excluded from this threshold check because it never reaches `ReadyForSubmission`/`SubmittedToCustomer` (no carrier submission per FR-78); the default work-item's terminal state (`Closed`) is reached separately via the TPM-Mark-Closed path on the default work-item itself once all its associated documents are resolved (see FR-78); items in `Blocked` or `Delayed` state keep collection open — polling remains active for those items until they also reach the threshold. **On threshold crossing — final sweep:** before halting, HILDA performs one final sweep across all ingest channels for the milestone: (a) **NSD** — one final poll of all `inbound/` folders for the milestone's items; (b) **PLM** — one final poll of all PLM issues for the milestone; (c) **Email** — any queued or unprocessed emails for the milestone's `BATCH-<id>` are processed to completion; all files detected in the final sweep are ingested via normal flows (FR-52, FR-55, FR-26, FR-72). **Polling halt:** after the final sweep completes, all polling for the milestone stops — FR-26 PLM polling, FR-55 NSD monitoring, and FR-25 CustomerJIRA polling all cease; inbound emails for the milestone's `BATCH-<id>` are silently ignored after the final sweep; any NSD drops or PLM uploads after this point are not processed and generate no owner notification; ignored inbound activity is recorded in `CommunicationLog` with `action_type: IgnoredPostClosure` for audit purposes only. **Closure is permanent:** collection closure does not reverse even if a DeliveryItem reverts below `ReadyForSubmission` (e.g. FR-62 upload on a `SubmittedToCustomer` item reverts it to `UnderPMReview`); SP UI (FR-62) is the only accepted upload channel after closure; TPM is responsible for all further document uploads.

### Fault Handling

### Milestone Storage Cleanup

- **FR-76** `[Ph-1]` — **Milestone storage cleanup on `MilestoneAllClosed`:** HILDA monitors the `Closed` state transition on every DeliveryItem; **fires when `MilestoneAllClosed` (FR-28) condition is met — all `milestone_gating=true` DeliveryItems in the milestone reach `Closed` state (gating-aware semantic locked 2026-06-13; default work-item is implicitly gating per FR-78). Non-gating items don't gate cleanup but are auto-advanced to `Closed` by FR-64 part 2 cascade so the state machine stays consistent.** Pathways reaching `Closed`: FR-14 per-item override, FR-64 bulk Close All Items (gating + non-gating cascade), FR-7/FR-80 TPM-Mark-Closed for `no_customer_upload` items, FR-78 TPM-Mark-Closed for default work-item. On condition met, HILDA triggers a permanent storage cleanup for that milestone. **Cleanup scope** (anchors `[D-013]` + `[D-050]` impl notes 2026-05-28): **(a) NSD1 internal tree** — the full milestone subtree `\\NSD1\internal\<carrier>\<device>\<milestone>\` is deleted, including all TG group folders (`<tg_path_id>/_zip_audit/`, classified `<item>/<doc_type>/<doc_id_slug>/revN/`, staged `<item>/<doc_type>/staged/`, outbound `<item>/outbound/`); **(b) HILDA PC local cache** — temporary files downloaded during submission assembly (FR-18) or package download (FR-73) for this milestone are deleted. **NOT deleted**: **(c) NSD1 owner-inbound flat folder** (`\\NSD1\<carrier>\<device>\<milestone>\<Folder-N>\`) — preserved; owners may continue dropping documents post-closure (HILDA ignores them per FR-74 collection halt + `CommunicationLog action_type: IgnoredPostClosure`, but the folder itself stays); **(d) NSD2** — never touched by HILDA per `[D-013]` impl note 2026-05-28 (NSD2 is read-only from HILDA's perspective; originals stay forever, no archival). Deletion logged to `CommunicationLog` with `action_type: milestone_storage_cleanup` and the count of files and bytes deleted; PM dashboard notifies TPM cleanup is complete. **TPM advisory at close action:** the SP UI (FR-14, FR-64) displays an informational note at the point of closing items: *"Closing all items will permanently delete NSD storage for this milestone. Document download links (FR-61) will return 404 after cleanup — download any needed documents before proceeding. Only close items after the carrier has confirmed technical acceptance of the milestone submission."*; advisory only — does not block the close action.

### Fault Handling

- **FR-75** `[Ph-1]` — **Fault routing principle:** all faults and processing errors in HILDA route as follows: **(a) PM-triggered actions** — any SP UI button click (Start Collection, Submit to Carrier, Download Package, Approve, Upload, etc.) always surfaces its result (success or failure) in the SP UI to the PM immediately on completion; the same result is also logged to the `HildaOpsAlert` queue for support team investigation; **(b) background processing faults** — faults arising from background jobs (ZIP extraction errors, document routing failures, classification failures, polling errors, email processing errors) route to `HildaOpsAlert` only; no PM notification is generated for background faults; in Ph-1 and Ph-2 all faults — regardless of type — are routed to a single `HildaOpsAlert` queue; fine-grain routing to distinct support-team vs TPM/PM-dashboard destinations is deferred to Ph-3 (DEF-21); cited by FR-72, FR-73.

### Customer portal routing, default work-item, per-item flags

- **FR-77** `[Ph-1]` — **Document → Customer portal target-folder routing (Type-2 pipeline).** `<carrier>` segment in upload paths = `customer_id`; sourced from customer YAML at HILDA startup per [D-077] (same source as FR-13 NSD path; NOT from Customers SP list — HILDA's runtime SP coupling is Milestones + DeliveryItems only).
