Once a document is routed to a work-item via FR-52, HILDA determines the carrier portal upload destination via a 2-step pipeline: (1) **Folder-based** (TG opt-in via `folder_routing_enabled=True`): outermost source folder (NSD or zip-internal) substring-matched against TG's `folder_routing.yaml` entries; if matched, entry's `target_folder` (full carrier-side path) used; (2) **Work-item fallback**: if folder routing didn't fire OR TG has no folder routing template, use `work_item_routing` section keyed by resolved work-item-id → `target_folder`. **Upload destination**: `<carrier>/<target_folder>/<matched_source_folder>/<subtree>/<filename>` where `<target_folder>` is full carrier path from template (may be nested), `<matched_source_folder>` is outermost source folder name preserved verbatim (including any noise like `audio(done)`), `<subtree>` is everything below the matched source folder preserved as-is. Step 2 (no source folder context) produces `<carrier>/<target_folder>/<filename>`. **No-upload condition**: documents associated only with default work-item (FR-78) cannot be uploaded — TPM must resolve to real work-item via FR-83. Template structure at `customizations/template_schemas/<customer_id>/folder_routing.yaml`:
  ```yaml
  <tg-name>:
    folder_routing:
      <source-folder-substring>:
        work_item: <work-item-id>            # for FR-52 step 3 (optional)
        target_folder: <full-carrier-path>   # for FR-77 step 1 (optional)
    work_item_routing:
      <work-item-id>: <full-carrier-path>    # for FR-77 step 2 fallback
  ```
  **Template-incomplete failure case** — work-item resolved but no `work_item_routing` entry exists for it (template gap; ops hasn't mapped this work-item to a carrier target folder): HILDA raises `EML-W007 — target folder not mapped for work-item '{id}'; manual triage required` and surfaces on PM/TPM dashboard. Document is held in NSD classified path; carrier portal upload is suppressed until ops updates the customer's `folder_routing.yaml` to add the missing `work_item_routing` entry. PLM upload still fires per the standard path (PLM is unaffected by carrier-target-folder mapping).

  **FR-69 portal_structure.yaml retained** for carrier mechanics (overwrite policy, suffix patterns) applied at dispatch time after FR-77 path determination. Two YAMLs with distinct concerns. Anchors `[D-053]`.

- **FR-78** `[Ph-1]` — **Default work-item per milestone (auto-instantiated).** Every milestone instantiated at tracker creation (per FR-2 R&R lock — SP UI engineer's `setup_milestone` task creates the SP rows; HILDA does NOT create rows) includes an auto-instantiated default work-item as the milestone's last DeliveryItem (`sort_order = max+1`; not editable; cannot be deleted). Default work-item: `item_type = Default`; belongs to system-reserved TG named `"_unrouted"`; `doc_count = 0`; `review_required = false` (cannot be set true); **`milestone_gating = true` (hardcoded by HILDA at instantiation; cannot be set false; YAML template's `milestone_gating` value is ignored for the default work-item — added 2026-06-13 per `MilestoneAllClosed` gating-aware semantic);** accepts documents of any of the 5 `doc_type` values per FR-86 (classification per FR-85 still runs at ingest for downstream FR-83 reassignment quality; `[D-039]` revision determination is SKIPPED at ingest when routing lands on Default — deferred to FR-83 reassignment time per FR-86 storage matrix); does NOT participate in carrier submission — files associated only with default work-item cannot be uploaded to carrier portal per FR-77; **does NOT gate `ReadyForSubmission` of other items** in the milestone — other items proceed to submission independently; **does NOT count toward FR-74 collection-phase-closure threshold** (default work-item is excluded from the `{ReadyForSubmission, SubmittedToCustomer, Closed}` check); **DOES gate full milestone closure** (the `MilestoneAllClosed` trigger per FR-28 + `MilestoneStorageCleanup` action per FR-29) — the default work-item must reach `Closed` before milestone-wide cleanup fires. **How default work-item reaches `Closed`**: once all its associated documents have been resolved (reassigned to real work-items via FR-83 or accepted as legacy per FR-83's TPM workflow), TPM clicks **Mark Closed** on the default work-item row in SP UI; HILDA enforces the guard *"no remaining documents associated with this default work-item via `DocumentItemAssociation`"* at click time → default work-item advances to `Closed`. **Template structure** under `default_work_item:` block per milestone:
  ```yaml
  <milestone-name>:
    default_work_item:
      item_name: "Unrouted Documents"          # optional; default if absent
      item_description: ""                      # tags not applicable (no routing)
      owner: null                               # optional; default null
  ```
  **Template fallback**: if the customer template does not define a `default_work_item:` block for a milestone, HILDA auto-instantiates with system defaults: `item_name = "Unrouted Documents"`, `item_description = ""`, `owner = null`. Per `[D-085]`, the default work-item inherits `Milestone.target_date` from the Milestones SP list at rule-evaluation time (no per-item date column). Documents failing FR-52 routing (step 5) associated via multi-item table (FR-79); `DocumentIndexRow.unrouted_source_path` captures originating context (NSD parent path or zip-internal path). Documents remain associated until TPM resolves them via FR-83. Anchors `[D-053]`.

- **FR-79** `[Ph-1]` — **REWRITTEN 2026-06-12 — cleanup of stale primary/secondary association_kind schema reference per storage 2026-06-07 file-centric refactor**. **Multi-item document association.** One document may be associated with multiple work-items (e.g., a test report covering test cases for items I-1 and I-2). HILDA represents via M:M join table `DocumentItemAssociation` in Postgres (storage module) with **composite PK `(file_hash, delivery_item_id)`** — symmetric M:M, **no primary/secondary distinction** (the prior `association_kind: Literal["primary", "secondary"]` field + the duplication of primary association across `DocumentIndexRow` and `DocumentItemAssociation` were both dropped during the 2026-06-07 storage refactor; the refactor moved `DocumentIndexRow` to file-centric model with PK = `file_hash` per `[D-072]` and made `DocumentItemAssociation` carry per-(file, item) physical-storage properties — `local_nsd_path`, `nsd_path_type`, `owner_email`, `plm_id`, `plm_attachment_id`, `upload_timestamp`). One physical file (= one `file_hash` = one `DocumentIndexRow`) may have N `DocumentItemAssociation` rows (one per associated item); per-(file, item) properties live exclusively on the M:M row. **The same file may exist at multiple NSD paths simultaneously** — one physical copy per associated item per `[D-055]`. **Single-milestone invariant**: per FR-79 (Ph-1/Ph-2), all `DocumentItemAssociation` rows for a given `file_hash` share the same `milestone_id` (cross-milestone associations deferred to Ph-3+; storage enforces via STR-E005).

  **When multi-item association fires**: FR-52 step 1 (strict substring on tags) — if the same filename satisfies the tag-set of multiple items, all matched items receive an association entry (each as a separate `DocumentItemAssociation` row with its own `local_nsd_path` to that item's physical copy). Per-item ZIP-extracted destinations may differ (each item gets its own `<item_path_id>/<doc_type>/<doc_id_slug>/revN/<filename>` path per FR-13). **HILDA rendering** (FR-59): document section in HILDA's tab shows the document on each associated item's `/docs/<customer_id>/<item_id>` page with the per-item download link (each item's view of a shared file resolves to that item's own NSD copy per FR-61 path-agnostic resolution).

  **PLM upload — fan-out across distinct (owner_email × plm_id) pairs per FR-79 revised 2026-06-07** (note: `owner_email` here = storage's `DocumentItemAssociation.owner_email` denormalized at association time per FR-88 preference rule — corp_usa_email > corp_email; storage field rename to align with FR-2 split is a separate template_schema/storage cascade tracked in STATUS.md Flags): each item has exactly one owner with one `plm_id` per (device, milestone, owner). The file is uploaded **once per distinct `(owner_email, plm_id)` pair** across all associations — not per item. Two cases: **(a) one owner, N items in same TG**: N rows share the same (owner_email, plm_id) → ONE PLM upload; `plm_attachment_id` + `upload_timestamp` replicated across all N M:M rows for that owner. **(b) two owners in same TG, N items**: N rows have 2 distinct (owner_email, plm_id) pairs → TWO PLM uploads; distinct `plm_attachment_id` + `upload_timestamp` per row (grouped by which owner). This ensures every owner whose item(s) the document touches sees the file in their respective PLM issue without duplicate uploads to the same PLM issue. Storage's `fan_out_plm_associations(file_hash) -> list[PLMFanOutTarget]` returns DISTINCT (owner_email, plm_id) pairs; `issue_tracker` iterates this list once per pair.

  **Carrier portal upload**: file uploaded once per FR-77 per association — per-item target_folder resolution per FR-77 Type-2 routing (different items may route to different carrier-side folder destinations). Anchors `[D-053]`, `[D-055]`, `[D-072]`.

- **FR-80** `[Ph-1]` — **`no_customer_upload` per-item flag.** Each DeliveryItem carries an optional `no_customer_upload: bool = False` field. When `True`: PLM upload suppressed (`plm_attachment_id` stays null); carrier portal upload suppressed (`ReadyForSubmission → SubmittedToCustomer` path doesn't fire). HILDA continues to receive + store documents on NSD per FR-13; FR-9/FR-10 outreach + reminders fire normally; FR-53 review fires per `review_required`. TPM downloads via existing FR-57 endpoint at any time (state-agnostic). Item advances to `Closed` via FR-7 TPM-Mark-Closed manual path (guards enforced). Editable per FR-14 by TPM. Use case: TPM intends to manually dispatch HILDA-collected documents to carrier out-of-band (legacy carriers without portal integration). Anchors `[D-054]`.

- **FR-81** `[Ph-2]` — **`tracking_enabled` per-TG + `force_tracking_enabled` per-item override.** Each TGGroup carries a `tracking_enabled: bool = True` field. **Storage location (revised 2026-06-14 per FR-40 single-template rewrite)**: TG-level `tracking_enabled` lives in `template.yaml` denormalized per work-item (same value repeated across all items sharing the same `tg_name`); HILDA reads from per-item delivery_state row via `[D-051]` impl note denormalization; TPM cannot edit it inline. Supersedes prior 2026-06-13 framing (`tg_groups.yaml` — obsolete per FR-40 rewrite). Per-item `force_tracking_enabled` IS on each delivery-item row per FR-56 (b) and is TPM-editable inline. When `False`: HILDA does NOT poll email/NSD/PLM/messenger for any DeliveryItem in this TG; FR-9 outreach + FR-10 reminders suppressed; documents cannot be ingested through any channel other than FR-62 (TPM SP UI upload — see FR-62 Ph-2 Open-state extension). Items sit in `Open` state until TPM uploads via FR-62; thereafter advances per standard pipeline. Each DeliveryItem may carry optional `force_tracking_enabled: bool | None = None` override: `None` (default) — inherit TG; `True` — override TG-false (this item IS tracked); `False` — same as None when TG is False. **Effective tracking**: `effective_tracking_enabled = item.force_tracking_enabled if item.force_tracking_enabled is not None else tg.tracking_enabled`. **Interaction with FR-80**: a no-tracking TG can have items with any combination of `no_customer_upload`; when `no_customer_upload=false` for a no-tracking item, FR-62-uploaded documents still flow through PLM + carrier portal. Anchors `[D-054]`.

- **FR-82** `[Ph-1]` — **Tag catalog management (HILDA ops ONLY; NOT TPM-editable per 2026-06-12 SP UI engineer review).** Routing tags (FR-52 step 1) are owned exclusively by the HILDA ops team — **TPM does NOT edit `item_description` tags in SP UI** (per FR-56 column model rewrite 2026-06-12: `item_description` is YAML-locked / ops-managed display; routing internals are HILDA architecture concern, not per-instance TPM call). Tags live on each DeliveryItem's `item_description` field (comma-separated list per item) and are sourced from two complementary paths:

  **(a) Customer template (YAML) — bootstrap for new trackers.** HILDA ops author/edit the per-customer template at `customizations/template_schemas/<customer_id>/template.yaml` with a `tag_catalog:` block per milestone:
  ```yaml
  <milestone-name>:
    tag_catalog:
      <item-id>: [<tag-1>, <tag-2>, ...]
  ```
  Template-file edits **apply only to new trackers** instantiated after the edit (FR-1/FR-2); HILDA reloads the template at process startup or via `SIGHUP` admin signal. Template-file edits are **not** a rule-engine trigger and do **not** auto-propagate to active trackers — propagation requires path (b).

  **(b) SP UI `item_description` edit — propagation to active trackers.** When **HILDA ops** edits an existing item's `item_description` field via SP UI (TPM is NOT authorized for this field per FR-56 column model — SP UI engineer's web part renders `item_description` read-only to TPM role; HILDA ops role bypasses that render-time restriction), the SP-alert email per FR-84 fires `ItemModified` → `TagsModified` sub-trigger (FR-28) → `PropagateTagsToActiveTrackers` action (FR-29). HILDA propagates the new tag list to all active items matching `(customer_id, tg_name, item_no)` across all active milestones × devices for that customer (idempotent SP REST writes; duplicate tags de-duplicated). Audit logged to `CommunicationLog` with `action_type: tag_catalog_propagation`.

  **Workflow for HILDA ops to update tags across active trackers**: ops can either (i) edit one representative SP list item via SP UI (ops role) → SP-alert → auto-propagation per (b); or (ii) edit the YAML template + edit one representative SP item — keeps YAML and live state aligned for future trackers.

  **Backup manual path** `[Ph-2]`: TPM clicks "Refresh tags from template" per tracker via SP UI — re-reads YAML + overwrites SP item_descriptions for that tracker only.

  **Tag uniqueness validation** runs at two points: (i) at YAML template load (startup / reload) — HILDA validates tag-sets across items in same `(milestone_id, doc_type)` scope; (ii) at SP-UI propagation time — same validation before `PropagateTagsToActiveTrackers` writes. Overlap raises `TSC-W004 — routing-tag overlap detected for items I-N, I-M on (milestone, doc_type)`. Anchors `[D-053]`.

- **FR-83** `[Ph-1]` — **Default work-item resolution by TPM (Ph-1) + owner outreach upstream (Ph-2).** Documents in milestone's default work-item (FR-78) require resolution before milestone closure. **Scope**: FR-83 covers documents in the `unrouted` NSD path only (per FR-86); documents in `staged-not-classified` and `staged-not-revision-determined` paths (work-item already resolved; doc_type or revision pending) are resolved directly via FR-87 step (B) and/or step (C) — no FR-83 reassignment flow is invoked for those paths. **`[Ph-1]` TPM-manual via SP UI — strict A→B→C order per FR-87**: TPM browses default work-item document list (FR-56) and resolves outstanding ambiguities in strict order — (A) work-item reassignment; (B) doc_type resolution (if `unresolved` OR misaligned with new item_type); (C) revision determination (if `[D-039]` deferred at ingest per FR-86 storage matrix). HILDA: (1) updates document's primary association to selected work-item (FR-79); (2) clears `unrouted_source_path`; (3) evaluates (new item_type, doc_type) pair against FR-86 storage matrix — aligned pairs proceed to `classified` NSD path (after `[D-039]` runs); misaligned pairs land on `staged-not-classified` for further TPM resolution via step (B) per FR-87 (no auto-override of doc_type — supersedes the prior auto-derivation interpretation per `[D-053]` impl note 2026-06-08); (4) re-runs FR-77 Type-2 routing when alignment is reached; (5) uploads to carrier portal (subject to `no_customer_upload`); (6) logs `CommunicationLog` entry with `action_type: tpm_reassign_to_workitem`. **`[Ph-2]` Automated owner outreach upstream of TPM**: before surfacing to TPM, HILDA sends corp-messenger query to originating owner: "Document X received but could not be routed; which item does this pertain to?" Owner replies via messenger structured response with item_id; HILDA performs same reassignment as TPM-manual. After M unanswered attempts (configurable), HILDA escalates to TPM. Cadence shares `escalation_schedule` AutomationRule (mirrors FR-10 pattern). Anchors `[D-053]`.

### SharePoint → HILDA inbound notification channel

- **FR-84** `[Ph-1]` — **SharePoint → HILDA inbound notification channel** (anchors `[D-047]`). SharePoint 2017 → HILDA HTTP is **unconditionally firewall-blocked** by the corp/lab network boundary — SP cannot POST to `hilda-api`; PM browsers cannot directly XHR to `hilda-api`. The **only** inbound notification channel from SP to HILDA is **email** via SP's built-in alert feature on each SP list, configured with the **"Send Alerts for These Changes: Anything changes"** trigger setting. **Two SP list sources** per `[D-083]` 2-list architecture: per-customer `Deliverables_<customer_id>` (Work Items) lists AND the global `Milestones` list. SP UI engineer configures alerts on both. Each list source has its own subject pattern + routing key.

  **Common flow**: (1) PM/TPM action in SP UI (direct list-field edit per FR-14, or button click) → (2) SP alert fires → structured notification email to the HILDA dedicated mailbox (sender `<List Display Name> <sharepoint@<corp-domain>>`; subject pattern per source — see below; structured key:value body; action verb in sub-header: `has been added | modified | deleted`) → (3) HILDA's `email_service` consumes the mailbox via the three-tier inbound path per FR-23 (IMAP IDLE primary → short-poll fallback → deadline-tiered third tier); interactive UX latency is bounded by the IMAP IDLE / short-poll path (~5–15 s end-to-end) → (4) `sp_alert_parser` sub-module under `core/src/email_service/sp_alert_parser.py` deterministically extracts the structured key:value body via rule-based regex (no LLM) and the action verb from the sub-header → (5) the parser dispatches to the per-source routing path below → (6) HILDA dispatches the corresponding Celery task.

  **Source A — Deliverables_<customer_id>** (per-customer Work Items list; one alert source per customer): subject pattern `Alert_Deliverables_<customer_id> - <ItemTitle>` (example: `Alert_Deliverables_MMK - NVIOT - AGPS Test Results` → `MMK` is the `customer_id`). **5-field natural key** routes to a Work Item row: **(i) `customer_id`** extracted from subject suffix → selects the `Deliverables_<customer_id>` SP list; **(ii) `Model`** extracted from body (= `device_id` / `project_model` column on Deliverables row); **(iii) `ProjectID`** extracted from body (= `project_id` column; corp-assigned numeric); **(iv) `MinorMilestone`** extracted from body (= `milestone_id` column); **(v) `ItemNumber`** extracted from body (= `item_no` column). **Resolution**: single `$filter` GET on the Deliverables list returns the work-item row with all columns (denormalized customer/device/milestone identity + work-item-scoped fields + TG-denormalized fields). `Model` + `ProjectID` cross-validated against the row's columns — log `EML-W008` on mismatch but don't fail.

  **Source B — Milestones** (global list shared across all customers): subject pattern `Alert_Milestones - <MilestoneTitle>`. **4-field natural key** routes to a Milestone row: **(i) `carrier`** extracted from body (= `customer_id`); **(ii) `Model`** extracted from body (= `device_id` / `project_model`); **(iii) `ProjectID`** extracted from body (= `project_id`); **(iv) `Id`** extracted from body (Milestones row PK auto-Counter). **Resolution**: single `$filter` GET on the Milestones list returns the milestone row with `status`, `target_date`, milestone-level button timestamps (`milestone_collection_started_at`, `milestone_submission_triggered_at`, `closed_all_items_triggered_at`, `refresh_requested_at`, `download_package_request_timestamp`), and download_package_* HILDA-write fields. `carrier` + `Model` + `ProjectID` cross-validated against the row's columns — log `EML-W008` on mismatch.

  **PM identity resolution** at SP-alert dispatch time (per `[D-083]` Projects lookup): HILDA reads the Projects SP list to resolve `assigned_pm_id` by `project_id` (the Projects list is the canonical `project_id → TPM` lookup; `assigned_pm_id` is NOT denormalized on Deliverables or Milestones rows). HILDA caches the Projects list in memory at startup (SIGHUP-refreshed) — architecture-phase to confirm caching policy. **`customer_jira_url`** is NOT in SP — HILDA reads from template.yaml at startup per FR-25 (b) lock.

  **Milestone-level actions** (Start Collection per FR-8, Download Package per FR-73, Submit to Carrier per FR-63, Close All Items per FR-64) per `[D-083]` write to a **single Milestones row** for the milestone (one column write → one SP-alert fires → one routing dispatch). **No N-row cascade write**; **no `[D-082]` cascade-dedup needed** for milestone-button actions (dedup obsoleted for these cases per `[D-085]` cascade scope reduction). The HILDA-write-back fields (`download_package_url`, `download_package_status`, `download_package_generated_at`) are written to the same single Milestones row via `[D-064]` REST writeback.

  **Outbound write-back from HILDA to SP** uses SP REST API + NTLM per `[D-006]` (outbound is unconstrained). HILDA writes to Deliverables rows (per-item state machine updates) and to the Milestones row (status updates) via `[D-064]` REST. **PM browser access to HILDA** routes through the IT-admin's corp-side reverse proxy (`hilda-proxy.corp`); all such access is top-level browser navigation per `[D-074]`. **SP-page-JS cross-origin XHR to HILDA is blocked**; the JS-fetch path is structurally closed. **Same-origin POST inside HILDA's rendered tab is unrestricted** for HILDA's own endpoints (e.g., FR-87 TPM-resolution buttons per `[D-074]`).

**Routing key immutability requirement**: `ItemNumber` in the SP list must be immutable for the life of a delivery item; if SP UI engineer confirms `ItemNumber` is re-numberable when items are added/removed, an immutable `item_guid` column must be added to the SP list and the routing key extended to `(ProjectID, MinorMilestone, item_guid)` (architecture-phase confirmation per STATUS.md Flags).

**Resolution from routing key**: the 5-field routing key `(customer_id, Model, ProjectID, MinorMilestone, ItemNumber)` resolves a row via two-step lookup — (i) `customer_id` selects the SP list `Deliverables_<customer_id>` (subject suffix encodes this); (ii) `$filter` query on that list — `?$filter=minorMilestone eq '<MinorMilestone>' and item_no eq <ItemNumber>` — returns exactly one row carrying every column HILDA needs (`customer_id`, `customer_jira_url`, `model`, `project_id`, `assigned_pm_id`, milestone-scoped fields, TG-scoped fields, work-item-scoped fields). `Model` and `ProjectID` are cross-validated against the row's `model` + `project_id` columns for routing-key integrity — log `EML-W008` on mismatch but don't fail. All attributes co-exist on the single retrieved row; no cross-list joins. Per-(customer, device, milestone) row reads are cached for the duration of a single SP-alert dispatch (~5–30 s) to amortize batched per-item alerts within the same milestone — first alert in a batch issues the GET; subsequent alerts in the same batch reuse the cached row. Per-tracker total: 1 SP row read per distinct (customer, milestone, item) in an alert batch (sub-second total; negligible compared to IMAP poll cycle). **Milestone-scoped writes** (Start Collection per FR-8, Download Package per FR-73, Submit to Carrier per FR-63, Close All Items per FR-64): SP UI engineer's web part writes the milestone-level column (e.g., `milestone_collection_started_at`) to **every row** in the milestone — N rows × 1 column write = N SP-alerts to HILDA. HILDA deduplicates by detecting the same `(customer_id, MinorMilestone, column_name)` tuple within an alert batch and processing only the first (per FR-11 / `[D-082]` cascade-dedup pattern).

Anchors `[D-047]`, `[D-006]`, `[D-013]`, `[D-021]`.

**Implementation note (2026-06-10 — see `[D-064]`)**: The outbound write-back direction of this FR (HILDA → SP REST) is **the sole HILDA-initiated state writeback channel** per `[D-064]` — there is no other mechanism by which HILDA may push state changes to SP UI surfaces (no SP-inbound email handler from HILDA; no SP-side scheduled jobs polling HILDA; no shared filesystem signaling). All HILDA-side state mutations PMs/TPMs need to see in SP UI flow through `core/src/sharepoint_integration:SpCrud`. SP UI surfaces them via **focus-aware refresh** per SP UI engineer 2026-06-10 (re-fetch on tab focus-gain; no work when backgrounded). `[D-064]` is the outbound sister ADR to `[D-047]` (inbound SP-alert channel) — together they define the complete HILDA ↔ SP channel discipline.

- **FR-85** `[Ph-1]` — **Document `doc_type` classification pipeline** (anchors `[D-053]` impl note 2026-06-08, supersedes the withdrawn 2026-05-28b "1:1 derivation" framing). On document arrival via any ingest channel (email FR-12, NSD FR-55, PLM poll FR-26, SP UI upload FR-62), HILDA processes the document through a **sequential pipeline** (locked 2026-06-16, corrects prior "independent / parallel allowed" framing): **(stage 1) doc_type classification** (this FR) — runs first; **(stage 2) work-item routing** per FR-52 — runs after classification completes; **(stage 3) new-vs-revised version determination** per `[D-039]` — runs after stage 2, gated on `doc_type != unresolved` AND `item_type != Default` per FR-86 storage matrix (deferred to FR-83 reassignment time when gate fails); **(stage 4) NSD path determination** per FR-86 4-path matrix from the `(item_type, doc_type, revision-determination)` tuple. Work-item routing in stage 2 does NOT use `doc_type` as an input (routing per FR-52 uses filename tags / fuzzy match on item_name / folder template / LLM independently of doc_type); sequential ordering is chosen for pipeline cleanliness + to enable the stage 3 `[D-039]` gate (which requires both classification + routing complete). **Review-vs-pipeline invariant** (locked 2026-06-16): document **review** (FR-16 parser + FR-53 LLM review) is a **post-pipeline action** — fires AFTER stages 1–4 complete on documents that land `classified` per FR-86. Review results (`DocumentIndexRow.parser_result` + `llm_review_findings`) do NOT feed back into stage 1 classification, stage 2 routing, or stage 3 revision determination — the pipeline runs to completion independent of any review outcome. Review is therefore advisory + persisted-only; it cannot retroactively change a doc's `doc_type`, work-item assignment, or revision row. Classification (stage 1) proceeds via the following 2-step ladder: **(Step 1) Filename regex match** — runs against per-customer rules loaded from `customizations/<customer_id>/doc_type_filename_rules.yaml` (universal fallback rules in `core/src/email_service/default_doc_type_rules.yaml` inherited if no customer override); patterns cover all 4 actionable doc_types `{test_report, tech_report, waiver, compliance_certification_release_notes}` (note: `compliance_certification_release_notes` is detected by filename regex ONLY — LLM does not classify into this bundle category). Top single-match wins; multi-match falls through to Step 2. **(Step 2) LLM `CLASSIFY_DOC_TYPE`** — fires only when Step 1 fails or multi-matches; candidate set restricted to `{test_report, tech_report, waiver}` (3 values; LLM never returns `compliance_certification_release_notes`); inputs are first-page excerpt + candidate list; returns `(doc_type, confidence)`. Confidence threshold default `0.85` (configurable per customer in `customizations/<customer_id>/doc_type_classifier_config.yaml`); below threshold → doc_type = `unresolved`. The classified `doc_type` is written to `DocumentIndexRow.doc_type` and consumed by stage 2 routing + FR-86 storage matrix. **Confirmation items**: type 1 (`Confirmation`) items do not appear in this workflow — they receive no documents per item type definition; reply paths are governed by FR-9 / FR-10 outreach. Anchors `[D-053]` impl note 2026-06-08, `[D-052]` (LLM backend selection for `CLASSIFY_DOC_TYPE` TaskKind — restored after 2026-05-28b removal).

- **FR-86** `[Ph-1]` — **Document storage matrix + 4-path NSD layout + alignment invariant** (anchors `[D-053]` impl note 2026-06-08, supersedes the prior single-classified-path framing). After FR-52 work-item resolution + FR-85 doc_type classification + conditional `[D-039]` revision determination, HILDA places each inbound document into exactly one of 4 NSD storage paths per the following matrix: **(unrouted)** — `internal/<carrier>/<device>/<milestone>/<inferred_tg_name>/_unrouted/<original_filename>` — when work-item lands on `item_type = Default` (FR-52 step 5 fallback); `<inferred_tg_name>` is the channel-resolved TG (NSD ingress_nsd / tg_email_group_alias / PLM-id reverse-lookup per `[D-060]`) — known at ingest even when work-item routing fails — enables TPM browsing of unrouted docs grouped by TG; doc_type may be any of 5 values; `[D-039]` is SKIPPED at ingest (run at FR-83 reassignment instead per Issue 13 optimization — saves LLM cost on unrouted docs). (Cascade note: `storage/MODULE.md` `NSDPath.internal_default_workitem()` helper signature needs to add `inferred_tg_name` parameter at next architecture-phase cascade.) **(classified)** — `internal/<carrier>/<device>/<milestone>/<tg>/<item>/<doc_type>/<doc_id_slug>/revN/` — when (item_type, doc_type) pair aligns per the alignment invariant AND `[D-039]` revision determination passed. **(staged-not-revision-determined)** — `internal/<carrier>/<device>/<milestone>/<tg>/<item>/<doc_type>/_staged_revision/<original_filename>` — when (item_type, doc_type) pair aligns BUT `[D-039]` revision determination failed (LLM ambiguous per `[D-039]` Step 3); awaits TPM resolution per FR-87 step C. **(staged-not-classified)** — `internal/<carrier>/<device>/<milestone>/<tg>/<item>/_staged_classification/<original_filename>` — when (item_type, doc_type) pair is MISALIGNED per the alignment invariant (e.g., `TEST_TECH_WAIVER_REPORT` item with doc_type = `compliance_certification_release_notes`, or any non-Default item with doc_type = `unresolved`); awaits TPM resolution per FR-87 steps A/B. **Alignment invariant**: `TEST_TECH_WAIVER_REPORT` items hold doc_type ∈ `{test_report, tech_report, waiver}`; `COMPLIANCE_CERTIFICATION_RELEASE_NOTES` items hold doc_type = `compliance_certification_release_notes`; `Default` items hold any of 5 doc_types; `Confirmation` items hold no documents (not in workflow). **`[D-039]` revision determination skip rule**: when work-item resolution lands on `item_type = Default` OR when doc_type = `unresolved`, `[D-039]` is SKIPPED entirely (not run); revision determination is deferred until TPM reassignment / doc_type resolution per FR-87, at which point `[D-039]` runs against the resolved (item, doc_type) family. **`[D-039]` family scope within `compliance_certification_release_notes` doc_type**: revision families are keyed by `(item, doc_type, doc_id_slug)` per existing `[D-039]` spec — filename slug differentiates compliance vs certification vs release_notes within the bundle (no separate `doc_subtype` field; cross-sub-type slug collisions are accepted as edge case). **`DocumentIndexRow` state combinatorics**: doc_type may be set OR `unresolved`; doc_id_slug + rev_number are NULL when `[D-039]` has not yet run (UNRESOLVED state OR Default-routed state OR staged-not-revision-determined state); secondary unique constraint `(milestone_id, doc_id_slug, rev_number)` allows multiple NULL-tuple rows per standard SQL semantics; `file_hash` PK uniquely identifies all rows including UNRESOLVED-state rows.

- **FR-87** `[Ph-1]` — **TPM document resolution in HILDA tab — strict order A → B → C** (anchors `[D-053]` impl note 2026-06-08; **REWRITTEN 2026-06-12 per `[D-074]`** — buttons moved from SP UI to HILDA-rendered document section; see "Original wording" below). For documents in any of the 3 staged NSD paths (`unrouted`, `staged-not-classified`, `staged-not-revision-determined` per FR-86), TPM resolves outstanding ambiguities via inline buttons in HILDA's rendered document section (per FR-59). **Each TPM button click is initiated in HILDA's rendered tab and reaches HILDA backend via same-origin form POST** — no SP-alert round-trip needed since HILDA receives the click directly (HILDA tab is `hilda-proxy.corp`; HILDA-tab JS is same-origin with HILDA's own POST endpoints; corp policy only blocks cross-origin XHR per `[D-074]`). **Per-step UI surface (inline on document row in HILDA's rendered tab per FR-59)**: **(A) Reassign work-item** — applies to documents in the `unrouted` path; TPM views the default work-item document list in HILDA tab (FR-59); document row surfaces a "Reassign to..." dropdown. **Dropdown shortlist (default)** filters by `(milestone_id = source.milestone_id) AND (tg_name = doc.inferred_tg_name) AND (item_type ∈ {TEST_TECH_WAIVER_REPORT, COMPLIANCE_CERTIFICATION_RELEASE_NOTES})` per `[D-060]` impl note 2026-06-08 inferred_tg_name semantic. **"Show all in milestone" toggle** expands the shortlist by dropping the `tg_name` filter (for cross-TG reassignment when TPM determines the channel-resolved `inferred_tg_name` was wrong). **Display per dropdown row**: `item_no` + `item_name` + (when "Show all in milestone" is engaged) `tg_name` — `item_name` alone is ambiguous when multiple items share names across TGs in a milestone. **(B) Resolve doc_type** — applies to documents with `doc_type = unresolved` OR (item_type, doc_type) misalignment; document row surfaces a "Resolve doc_type" dropdown with `{test_report, tech_report, waiver, compliance_certification_release_notes}`. **(C) Resolve revision** — applies to documents in `staged-not-revision-determined` path; document row surfaces "NEW" or "Revision of `<doc_id_slug>`" picker with existing family entries for the resolved (item, doc_type). Step C is gated until Step B is complete (`doc_type` set). The 3 steps may be performed independently in separate HILDA-tab sessions. **End-to-end flow per step**: (1) TPM clicks the inline button/dropdown in HILDA tab → (2) browser does **same-origin form POST** to dashboard endpoint (`/docs/<customer_id>/<item_id>/reassign`, `/docs/<customer_id>/<item_id>/resolve-doc-type`, or `/docs/<customer_id>/<item_id>/resolve-revision`) → (3) **synchronous dashboard validation** of auth, Choice-value membership, the resulting (item_type, doc_type) pair against the FR-86 alignment invariant, target item lookup (Step A — defensive against race: target item deleted between page render and submit), `doc_id_slug` existence (Step C — same defensive race intent: source row deleted concurrently), and FR-87 button token freshness — **note**: target_item / doc_id_slug were picked from server-rendered dropdowns on the same `/docs/<id>` page, so existence at server render time was guaranteed; these sync checks are race-condition defensive, not expected-to-fail in normal flow; **invalid choices are rejected with a form-redisplay error in the HTTP response** (HILDA-tab page re-renders showing the error inline next to the FR-87 button + the dropdown remains pre-populated so TPM can correct without losing state); error codes per dashboard MODULE.md: `DSH-E005` (Step A target item invalid), `DSH-E006` (Step B alignment violation), `DSH-E007` (Step C revision picker mismatch), `STR-E005` (cross-milestone association), `STR-E007` (expired button token); → (4) **synchronous storage write** for the immediate field update (`storage.reassign_document_to_workitem` / `tpm_resolve_doc_type` / `tpm_resolve_revision` per storage MODULE.md FR-87 APIs) — DB row update + NSD file move; sub-second; → (5) **synchronous workflow_engine task enqueue** for the async tail (Step B `[D-039]` LLM re-run for revision determination; `[D-064]` SP writeback for audit columns; review pipeline trigger if `review_required=true` AND the document reaches `classified` post-resolution) — task_id recorded in CommunicationLog; sub-second; **FR-77 carrier-upload is NOT triggered by FR-87 resolution** (carrier upload happens at FR-63 Submit to Carrier time per FR-13 / FR-18, not at per-document classification time); → (6) **dashboard returns 303 redirect to `GET /docs/<customer_id>/<item_id>`** so TPM sees the resolved state in the same HILDA tab WITHOUT blocking on the async tail; net latency target: sync POST handler returns 303 in <500ms; → (7) **ASYNC TAIL** runs in workflow_engine task body: Step B `[D-039]` Step 2 LLM re-run (10-30s typical) → re-runs FR-86 storage matrix → moves NSD file to final path if `[D-039]` resolves (classified or staged_not_revision) → updates `DocumentIndexRow` → writes back to SP via `[D-064]` REST (the audit-column SP fields `tpm_reassignment_target_item_id` / `tpm_resolved_doc_type` / `tpm_revision_resolution` are written as **READ-ONLY AUDIT DISPLAY** showing what TPM resolved + by whom — TPM-editable input semantics removed under FR-87 rewrite 2026-06-12; SP-side schema discipline added in `customizations/sharepoint_config/MODULE.md` 2026-06-12); → (**Progress UX during async tail**, added 2026-06-12): on the 303-redirected `/docs/<customer_id>/<item_id>` page, the document row that was just resolved shows a **🔄 Processing badge** ("Resolution submitted; processing...") read from CommunicationLog query for the pending workflow_engine task_id; TPM can manually refresh `/docs/<customer_id>/<item_id>` (or rely on focus-aware refresh) to see status updates; **on async success**, the badge clears and the document row updates to its resolved state (new `nsd_path_type` + populated `doc_id_slug`/`rev_number`/`doc_type` per resolution) — **state change is the implicit success indicator** (no explicit success banner needed); same-origin polling from HILDA tab to dashboard for status is architecturally allowed per `[D-074]` but Ph-1 keeps it lightweight (static badge until manual refresh) — Ph-2 may add JS-driven status polling if TPM UX warrants. → (8) **Async-tail errors surface to TPM via three channels** (NOT in the original form-submit response — that already returned 303): **(α) primary** — inline badge on the document row on next `/docs/<customer_id>/<item_id>` visit, read from CommunicationLog (e.g., "🔴 [D-039] re-run failed: LLM gateway timeout. Retry?"); **(β) secondary** — top-of-page banner on `/docs/<customer_id>/<item_id>` for the most recent FR-87 action within N minutes ("Last action `tpm_resolve_doc_type` had a downstream error: <msg>. [Retry] [Dismiss]"); **(γ) escalation** — TPM email when async failure is unrecoverable AND > N minutes elapsed since submit, with link back to `/docs/<customer_id>/<item_id>` for retry. Reserved for genuinely-stuck cases. → (9) TPM eventually returns to SP tab; sees updated state via focus-aware refresh per `[D-074]` once `[D-064]` writeback completes; if writeback lagged or failed, SP state shows stale until next focus-refresh after backend recovery. **Supersedes** the prior FR-83 step (3) auto-derivation behavior per `[D-053]` impl note 2026-06-08 — TPM reassignment from default work-item to a real item follows this same A → B → C flow rather than auto-overriding doc_type.

  ~~Original wording (struck 2026-06-12 per `[D-074]` — TPM resolution UX moved from SP-side field write to HILDA-tab same-origin form POST): SP UI document resolution — strict order A → B → C... Each TPM action is initiated in SP UI and reaches HILDA backend via the FR-84 SP-alert email channel — SP UI does NOT call hilda-api directly (firewall-blocked per FR-84)... TPM clicks the SP UI button → SP UI writes the value to the SP List row → SP alert fires per FR-84 → email to HILDA mailbox → email_service.sp_alert_parser routes the email to the appropriate FR-87 resolution handler...~~ Rationale for shift: TPM needs to SEE the document content before resolving (especially for step B doc_type pick) — document is in HILDA's rendered tab per FR-59. SP-side field writes were architecturally awkward (per-item SP dialog couldn't surface per-document buttons cleanly when one item has multiple staged documents). HILDA-tab same-origin POST works under corp policy per `[D-074]` (cross-origin XHR is blocked; same-origin POST is unrestricted). SP-alert round-trip for FR-87 is eliminated; SP fields become audit-only display.

### Owner identity model

- **FR-88** `[Ph-1]` — **Owner identity model**. Per-item owner is identified by **three fields, all SP `STR` free-form text** per `[D-086]`: **(a) `owner_corp_usa_email`** (corp-USA email — TPM types whatever resolves; HILDA does NOT validate against corp AD; not a Person/Group SP field); **(b) `owner_corp_email`** (free-form text; typically used for non-USA owners or external collaborators); **(c) `owner_corp_id`** (corp directory identifier; free-form text — TPM types whatever; HILDA does NOT auto-derive from email local-part). A fourth field **`owner_name`** (free-form text; TPM types display name) is the display-rendering source — HILDA does NOT resolve names via AD lookup. **HILDA preference rule for owner outreach**: HILDA uses `owner_corp_usa_email` when set; falls back to `owner_corp_email` when `owner_corp_usa_email` is null. **TG-level owner identity follows the same free-form text discipline** but uses **5 fields** (no `tg_owner_corp_email` — never existed per `[D-086]`): `tg_owner_corp_usa_email` + `tg_owner_corp_id` + `tg_owner_name` + `tg_email_group_alias` + `tg_path_id`. **Owner-change event semantics** `[Ph-2]` (with FR-3 / DEF-22): a write to EITHER `owner_corp_usa_email` OR `owner_corp_email` constitutes an owner change event — fires `OwnerReassigned` rule per FR-28 sub-trigger; HILDA records `CommunicationLog` with `action_type=owner_reassigned`. A write to `owner_corp_id` alone is NOT an owner change event (corp_id is identifier metadata; not principal identity for outreach). A write to `owner_name` alone is NOT an owner change event (display field only). **Ph-1**: owner fields are set at `setup_milestone` time from template.yaml and remain fixed for the milestone's lifetime; no `OwnerReassigned` rule firings; mid-milestone owner changes are out-of-scope until Ph-2. **Cross-references**: FR-2 (tracker creation pre-populates these fields from template.yaml — Ph-1 source of truth); FR-3 `[Ph-2]` (PM reassignment); FR-9 (outreach uses preference rule); FR-28 (`OwnerReassigned` rule trigger conditions per this FR — Ph-2); FR-71 (Owner Discovery Function operates on TG-level identity per this FR); FR-79 (PLM fan-out keys use preference-rule-resolved email).

## Appendix A — Rule Engine: Triggers, Conditions, and Actions

All rules fire via `AutomationRules` evaluated by the rule engine (FR-28 – FR-31). Column definitions: **Trigger** = event type from the FR-28 taxonomy; **Condition** = guard that must hold for the rule to fire; **Actions** = FR-29 action verbs invoked; **State Transition** = resulting `delivery_state` change on the target DeliveryItem; **Ph** = earliest phase in which the trigger/action is active; **FR refs** = authoritative requirements.

> **Note — Start Collection (FR-8):** PM/TPM clicking **Start Collection** in the SharePoint UI (FR-56) is a direct HILDA request, not a rule engine trigger. It creates PLM issues per (device, milestone, owner), calls `SendInitialOutreach` for all open items, and advances all to `OutreachSent`. For items added after Start Collection has already fired, `ItemCreated` mid-collection fires `StartItemCollection` instead (row 2).

> **Note — StateChange (row 6):** `StateChange` fires on any `delivery_state` transition and is an observation point for downstream rules. It does not independently initiate state transitions and has no default action — rules scoped to it are customer-configurable.

### A.1 Rule engine trigger table

| # | Trigger | Condition | Actions | State Transition | Ph | FR refs |
|---|---------|-----------|---------|------------------|----|---------|
| 1 | `ItemCreated` | At tracker creation; collection **not yet started** | *(none — item enters `Open`)* | → `Open` (initial) | Ph-1 | FR-2, FR-8 |
| 2 | `ItemCreated` | Mid-collection; collection **already active** for this milestone | `StartItemCollection` (idempotent PLM issue creation for new `(device, milestone, owner)` tuple per FR-5 + FR-8 step 2) + `SendInitialOutreach` | `Open` → `OutreachSent` | Ph-2 | FR-3, FR-8, FR-28, FR-29 (DEF-22) |
| 3 | `ItemModified` · `OwnerReassigned` | `owner_corp_usa_email` or `owner_corp_email` changed (per FR-2 — `owner_corp_id` change alone does NOT fire); collection active | `NotifyNewOwner`; `StartItemCollection` for new (device, milestone, owner) + `SendInitialOutreach` | `Open` → `OutreachSent` (new owner) | Ph-2 | FR-2, FR-3, FR-9, FR-28, FR-29 (DEF-22) |
| 4 | `ItemModified` · `DeadlineMoved` | `Milestone.target_date` changed by TPM (per `[D-085]`) | Re-arm `DeadlineProximity` evaluation immediately for all items in the milestone | *(no state change)* | Ph-1 | FR-14, FR-28 |
| 5 | `ItemDeleted` | PM removes DeliveryItem via FR-3 | `CancelOutstanding` — cancel all pending reminders and escalations; notify owner of removal | Item removed from tracker | Ph-2 | FR-3, FR-28, FR-29 |
| 6 | `StateChange` | Any `delivery_state` transition occurs | *(observation point — no default action; downstream rules may be scoped here)* | Varies | Ph-1 | FR-28 |
| 7 | `OwnerStatusConfirmed` | Owner reports **"done"** via `Email` [Ph-1] or `CorporateMessenger` [Ph-2]; AND (`item_type = Confirmation` from `OutreachSent`) OR (both conditions met: `doc_count` reached AND (`review_required = false` OR all documents/revisions reviewed)); `[Ph-1]` single revision per document | `UpdateState` → `OwnerClosed` (transient); auto-set `is_final = true` for rev1 of each document; `UpdateState` → `UnderPMReview` | `OwnerClosed` (transient) → `UnderPMReview` | Ph-1 | FR-7, FR-28 |
| 7a | `OwnerStatusConfirmed` | Owner reports **"done"**; `item_type ≠ Confirmation`; condition (1) NOT met (`doc_count` not reached) | `NotifyOwnerDocCountPending` (corp messenger notification: documents still expected; no response processed); `delivery_state` stays `DocumentReceived` | *(no state change)* | Ph-2 | FR-7, FR-28, FR-29 |
| 7b | `OwnerStatusConfirmed` `[Ph-2]` | Row 7 fires; multiple revisions exist for any `doc_id_slug` (OwnerClosed transient fork) | `TriggerVersionSelection` (FR-66 corp messenger version-selection workflow); `is_final = true` set on selected revision for each `doc_id_slug`; `UpdateState` | → `UnderPMReview` (after owner confirms all version selections) | Ph-2 | FR-7, FR-28, FR-29, FR-66 |
| 8 | `OwnerStatusConfirmed` | Owner reports **"done"**; `item_type ≠ Confirmation` AND `delivery_state = OutreachSent` (premature — no document received yet) | `NotifyPM` (`Premature closure attempt` flag on PM dashboard) | *(no state change)* | Ph-1 | FR-7, FR-28 |
| 9 | `OwnerStatusConfirmed` | Owner reports **"delayed"** via status-capable modality; any pre-approval state | `UpdateState` | → `Delayed` (transient; PM or owner can reverse) | Ph-1 | FR-7, FR-28 |
| 10 | `OwnerStatusConfirmed` | Owner reports **"blocked"** via status-capable modality; any pre-approval state | `UpdateState` | → `Blocked` (transient; PM or owner can reverse) | Ph-1 | FR-7, FR-28 |
| 11 | `OwnerStatusConfirmed` | `tracking_modality includes CustomerJIRA`; HILDA poll detects JIRA closure (no owner message required) | `UpdateState` | → `OwnerClosed` | Ph-1 | FR-7, FR-25, FR-28 |
| 12 | `LastContactThreshold` | `days_since_last_contact > N` (N configurable per rule); item not `Closed` | `SendReminder` (via item's status-capable modality) | *(no state change)* | Ph-1 | FR-10, FR-28 |
| 13 | `LastContactThreshold` | `days_since_last_contact > N`; `reminder_count ≥ M` (M configurable) and no owner response (no `last_updated` change after M reminders) | `Escalate` via corp messenger cross-channel; if owner has multiple open items in same milestone, messenger message aggregates all into one | *(no state change)* | Ph-1 | FR-10, FR-28, FR-29 |
| 14 | `DeadlineProximity` | `Milestone.target_date - today ≤ N` (N configurable per rule; per `[D-085]`); item not `Closed` | `Escalate` (owner + PM via configured channel) | *(no state change)* | Ph-1 | FR-11, FR-28 |
| 15 | `AttachmentReceived` | `doc_type = test_report`; count of `test_report` rows in document index for this item **< `doc_count`** (more test reports still expected) | `TriggerParser` (FR-16 rule-based parser — always fires); `TriggerAIReview` (FR-53 LLM review — fires only when `review_required = true`); document recorded in index | *(no `delivery_state` change)* | Ph-1 | FR-7, FR-16, FR-28, FR-52, FR-53, FR-55 |
| 16 | `AttachmentReceived` | `doc_type = test_report`; count of `test_report` rows in document index **reaches `doc_count`** | `TriggerParser` (FR-16 — always fires); `TriggerAIReview` (FR-53 — fires only when `review_required = true`); `UpdateState` | → `DocumentReceived` | Ph-1 | FR-7, FR-16, FR-28, FR-52, FR-53, FR-55 |
| 17 | `AttachmentReceived` | `doc_type ∈ {waiver, tech_report}` (supplementary documents) | `TriggerAIReview` (FR-53 LLM review — fires only when `review_required = true`); document recorded in index | *(no `delivery_state` change)* | Ph-1 | FR-7, FR-28, FR-53 |
| 18 | `AIReviewResult` | HILDA LLM quality review complete per FR-53; fires only when `review_required = true` | `NotifyPM` (findings surfaced to PM dashboard; `llm_review_findings` written to document index; `review_status` updated to `complete` on DeliveryItem); **no state change** — LLM review completion satisfies `OwnerClosed` guard condition (2) when `review_required = true`; when `review_required = false`, no `AIReviewResult` fires — condition (2) is vacuously satisfied | *(no delivery_state change)* | Ph-1 | FR-28, FR-53 |
| 19 | `PMApproval` | TPM explicitly approves per-item from `UnderPMReview` SP UI (FR-56); NFR-5 PM-approval gate | `QueueSubmission`; `UpdateState` | `UnderPMReview` → `ReadyForSubmission` | Ph-1 | FR-28, FR-29, FR-56, NFR-5 |
| ~~20~~ | ~~`MilestoneAllClosed`~~ | ~~All DeliveryItems in milestone reach `OwnerClosed`~~ | ~~`NotifyPM` (FR-22); queue submission assembly (FR-18)~~ | *(deferred: DEF-20 — FR-22 deferred; submission is manual via FR-63 SP action)* | ~~Ph-2~~ | FR-22, FR-28 |

### A.2 Direct SharePoint UI actions (not rule-engine triggers)

These are PM/TPM-initiated milestone-level actions that call HILDA directly, bypassing the rule engine. They can fire rule engine events as a side effect (e.g., Start Collection fires `SendInitialOutreach` which fires `ItemCreated` mid-collection for new items, etc.).

| Action | Enabled when | HILDA operations | State transition | Ph | FR refs |
|--------|-------------|------------------|-----------------|----|---------|
| **Start Collection** | Milestone has not yet been kicked off | Create PLM issues per (device, milestone, owner) (idempotent); `SendInitialOutreach` for all open items per FR-9 | All open items: `Open` → `OutreachSent` | Ph-1 | FR-8, FR-9, FR-56 |
| **Submit to Carrier** | All items = `ReadyForSubmission` or `SubmittedToCustomer`; ≥1 item = `ReadyForSubmission` | Assemble delta package from `ReadyForSubmission` items only (FR-18); NFR-5 gate with delta summary; dispatch per customer delivery modality | `ReadyForSubmission` items → `SubmittedToCustomer`; `SubmittedToCustomer` items unchanged | Ph-1 | FR-18, FR-63, NFR-5 |
| **Close All Items** | All items `SubmittedToCustomer` or `Closed`; ≥1 item `SubmittedToCustomer` | Set all `SubmittedToCustomer` items to `Closed`; log `CommunicationLog action_type: bulk_close` with PM/TPM attribution | `SubmittedToCustomer` items → `Closed`; already `Closed` items unchanged | Ph-1 | FR-64 |

### A.3 Delivery state machine

Valid `delivery_state` transitions per DeliveryItem:

```
Open
 └─[Start Collection / StartItemCollection]──────────────────────► OutreachSent
                                                                         │
                              ┌──────────────────────────────────────────┤
                              │ OwnerStatusConfirmed "done"               │ AttachmentReceived;
                              │ (Confirmation item only)                  │ doc_count reached
                              │                                           ▼
                              │                                   DocumentReceived
                              │                                           │
                              │                                           │ AIReviewResult
                              │                                           │ (no state change — updates
                              │                                           │  llm_review_findings only)
                              │                                           │
                              │                           OwnerStatusConfirmed "done"
                              │                           (both conditions met: doc_count
                              │                            reached AND (review_required=false
                              │                            OR all docs reviewed))
                              │                                           │
                              ▼                                           ▼
                         OwnerClosed ◄─────────────────────────── OwnerClosed
                         (transient)                               (transient)
                              │
                    ┌─────────┴────────────────────────┐
                    │ [Ph-1] single rev per doc         │ [Ph-2] multiple revisions
                    │ is_final=true auto-set            │ for any doc_id_slug
                    ▼                                   ▼
             UnderPMReview ◄────── FR-66 version selection ──── (owner selects via
             (TPM review gate)      (corp messenger)              corp messenger)

Delayed  ◄──[OwnerStatusConfirmed "delayed"; any pre-approval state]
Blocked  ◄──[OwnerStatusConfirmed "blocked"; any pre-approval state]
Delayed / Blocked ──[PM or owner reversal]──► (prior active state)

UnderPMReview ──[PMApproval — TPM approves in SP UI]─────────────► ReadyForSubmission   [Ph-1]
ReadyForSubmission ──[Submit to Carrier (FR-63)]─────────────────► SubmittedToCustomer  [Ph-1]
SubmittedToCustomer ──[FR-14 per-item or FR-64 bulk manual override]► Closed               (automated FR-22 deferred: DEF-20)
```

**Guards:**
- `Confirmation` items (`item_type = Confirmation`): skip `DocumentReceived` and `UnderPMReview`; `OwnerStatusConfirmed "done"` from `OutreachSent` is valid; `OwnerClosed` transient → `UnderPMReview` immediately (no documents, no `is_final` fork needed).
- All other item types: `OwnerStatusConfirmed "done"` from `OutreachSent` is **not** valid — triggers `NotifyPM` (`Premature closure attempt`) instead.
- `CustomerJIRA` items: reach `OwnerClosed` via HILDA detecting JIRA closure; no owner message required; transient fork applies normally.
- `Delayed` and `Blocked` are transient overlay states — the item re-enters its prior active state when the PM or owner reverses.
- `AIReviewResult` does **not** produce a state transition — it updates `llm_review_findings` in the document index and notifies the PM, satisfying the `OwnerClosed` guard condition (2) when all revisions are reviewed (`review_required = true` items only). When `review_required = false` on an item, guard condition (2) is vacuously satisfied — no `AIReviewResult` is awaited and the item advances to `OwnerClosed` immediately once condition (1) is met and the owner confirms "done".
- `Closed` is reachable via FR-14 manual per-item `delivery_state` override or FR-64 bulk Close All Items action; automated transition via FR-22 is deferred (DEF-20); Milestone → `Complete` when all items are `Closed`.

## Appendix B — FRs Requiring LLM Calls

> **Constraint (applies to every row):** All LLM calls — runtime and build/onboarding-time — must use on-premises open-source models. No public-cloud or SaaS LLM calls in any phase (NFR-1, `[D-007]`).

Two categories of LLM use exist in HILDA: **runtime LLM** (called during live pipeline execution) and **build/onboarding-time LLM** (called once per customer or adapter onboarding to generate code/config artifacts; not called during normal operation).

### B.1 Runtime LLM — Phase 1

| FR | LLM role | Inputs | Output / effect | Notes |
|----|----------|--------|-----------------|-------|
| **FR-12** path (c) | Free-text email reply classification | Email body text + `BATCH-<id>` DeliveryItem batch context | Item assignment, status extraction (`delivery_state` update) | LLM is fallback — rule-based parsing (path a) runs first; LLM fires only when rule-based confidence is below threshold; when the email also contains attachments, FR-52 document routing is **fused** into this same LLM call (one pass, shared context) — anchors `[D-034]` |
| **FR-52** step 4 (`ROUTE_ATTACHMENT` TaskKind) | Document-to-work-item routing | Filename + first-page text excerpt + candidate items within TG scope | `delivery_item_id` + confidence (doc_type classified per FR-85 stage 1, runs before this step) | LLM is fallback in 5-step pipeline per FR-52 amendment 2026-05-28 — fires only when strict-substring on `item_description` tags (step 1) + fuzzy match on `item_name` (step 2) + source-folder → work-item template (step 3) all fail; not invoked when fused into FR-12 path (c) call; on low confidence cascades to step 5 (staged → default work-item per FR-78); `CLASSIFY_DOC_TYPE` TaskKind restored per `[D-053]` impl note 2026-06-08 (FR-85) — doc_type classification runs first in the FR-85 sequential pipeline before FR-52 work-item routing per FR-85 lock 2026-06-16 |
| **FR-85** step 2 (`CLASSIFY_DOC_TYPE` TaskKind) | Document type classification | Filename + first-page excerpt + candidate set `{test_report, tech_report, waiver}` | `(doc_type, confidence)`; below threshold → `unresolved` | LLM fires only when filename regex (FR-85 step 1) fails or multi-matches; restored per `[D-053]` impl note 2026-06-08 (supersedes withdrawn 2026-05-28b 1:1 derivation framing); independent of FR-52 work-item routing — both feed FR-86 storage matrix |
| **FR-53** | Document quality review | Document content (test report / tech report / waiver) + per-customer quality checklist generated by the Test Report Profiler `[D-011]` | `llm_review_findings` written to document index row; `review_status` flag updated on DeliveryItem | Fires for all three `doc_type` values on every ingest channel (email FR-52, NSD FR-55, PLM poll FR-26, SP UI upload FR-62); findings surfaced in HILDA-rendered document section per `[D-074]` |

### B.2 Runtime LLM — Phase 2 additions

| FR | LLM role | Inputs | Output / effect | Notes |
|----|----------|--------|-----------------|-------|
| **FR-17** | Revision document quality re-review | Revised document (revN) + same per-customer quality checklist | Updated `llm_review_findings` written to document index row for `rev_number = N` | Prior revision findings are preserved in their own rows and never overwritten; invoked for all three revision sub-paths (email, NSD, PLM) |
| **FR-17** (D-039 Step 2 / `CLASSIFY_DOC` TaskKind) | New-vs-revision document classification | New document first-page excerpt + list of existing `(doc_id_slug, prior_first_page_excerpt)` candidates for same `(delivery_item_id, doc_type)` | Verdict ∈ {REVISION, NEW_DOCUMENT}; `revision_of: doc_id_slug` populated when REVISION; confidence score | LLM fires only on revision detection (Ph-2) when filename-derived slug doesn't match existing entries AND prior entries exist; Ph-1 first-receipt path never invokes Step 2 LLM (no prior docs to compare against); below-confidence → D-039 Step 3 `staged/` for PM/TPM triage; anchors `[D-039]` + `[D-052]` impl note 2026-05-28b |
| **FR-54** | Inbound messenger message classification | Inbound messenger text + owner's open DeliveryItems across milestones | Status (`OwnerClosed / Delayed / Blocked`) + reason; auto-applied above confidence threshold | Below-threshold cases go to PM dashboard as `Messenger reply — Manual triage`; flags grouped by milestone when owner has items across multiple milestones |

### B.3 Build / onboarding-time LLM

These LLM calls run **once per onboarding event** (new API spec, new customer template, new test-report corpus). They produce code/config artifacts committed to `customizations/`. Normal pipeline operation does not call these LLMs.

| Module | LLM role | Inputs | Output artifacts | Phase introduced | FR / decision refs |
|--------|----------|--------|-----------------|------------------|--------------------|
| **API Spec Ingestor** | Adapter code generation | Proprietary REST API spec (format TBD per `[D-015]`) | IssueTracker / Messenger Protocol adapter module under `customizations/<system>/<vendor>_adapter.py` | Ph-1 (corp PLM adapter + proprietary messenger adapter are Ph-1 first exercises) | `[D-003]`, `[D-008]`, `[D-009]`, FR-25, FR-50 |
| **Template Schema Ingestor** | Schema artifact generation | Customer Excel template schema | Per-customer Pydantic validators, Excel parsers, SharePoint List column mappings, initial `customer_rules.yaml` under `customizations/rules/<customer_id>/` (per FR-30) | Ph-2 (deferred alongside FR-1 path b, DEF-15) | `[D-010]`, FR-1, FR-39 |
| **Test Report Document Profiler** | Parser and checklist generation | Historical test report corpus (per-customer) | Per-customer test-report parser (consumed by FR-16), per-customer quality checklist (consumed by FR-53) | Ph-1 (parser + checklist are pre-requisites for FR-16 and FR-53) | `[D-011]`, FR-16, FR-46, FR-53 |

| **FR-64** | Close All Items SP action | Milestone-scoped bulk close; enabled when all items `SubmittedToCustomer` or `Closed` with ≥1 `SubmittedToCustomer`; sets all `SubmittedToCustomer` items to `Closed`; `CommunicationLog action_type: bulk_close`; automated FR-22 transition remains deferred (DEF-20) |
| **FR-69** | Customer portal directory structure | `customizations/<customer_id>/portal_structure.yaml` defines carrier folder/hierarchy layout, `file_overwrite_policy (overwrite \| rename)`, and `revision_file_suffix_pattern` (used when rename); loaded at assembly time (FR-18); schema in `core/src/customer_adapter/MODULE.md` |
| **FR-70** | Per-item `item_completion_pct` | Ph-1: test reports where (review complete OR `review_required = false`) / `doc_count` × 100 — a received test report counts as "done" for pct purposes when its review is complete or review is not required; Ph-2: non-failed test cases (status ≠ failed) / total test cases across latest revisions × 100; Confirmation items: 100 at OwnerClosed, 0 before; persisted on DeliveryItem SharePoint List row |
| **FR-74** | Collection phase closure | Milestone-level closure threshold: all items in {ReadyForSubmission, SubmittedToCustomer, Closed}; Blocked/Delayed keep polling active; final sweep (NSD, PLM, Email) before halt; FR-26/FR-55/FR-25 polling stops; post-closure inbound silently ignored; CommunicationLog audit; closure permanent — SP UI only after threshold |
| **FR-68** | PLM–NSD sync verification loop | Post-dispatch: poll PLM to verify rev1 present (Ph-1) or is_final revision (Ph-2); upload from NSD if missing; Ph-2: trigger FR-67 for stale attachments; result surfaced on PM dashboard |
| **FR-75** | Fault routing principle | PM-triggered actions: result surfaced in SP UI + logged to `HildaOpsAlert`; background faults: `HildaOpsAlert` only, no PM notification; single queue Ph-1/Ph-2 — fine-grain routing deferred: DEF-21 |
| **FR-76** | Milestone storage cleanup | Triggered when all items reach `Closed`; deletes HILDA internal NSD tree + owner inbound tree for milestone + HILDA PC local cache; logged to `CommunicationLog`; TPM notified; SP UI advisory shown at FR-14/FR-64 close action explicitly warning that document download links (FR-61) will return 404 after cleanup — TPM should download any needed documents before closing all items |
| **FR-77** | Document → carrier portal target-folder routing (Type-2) | 2-step pipeline: (1) source-folder → target-folder template (TG opt-in); (2) work-item → target-folder fallback; upload destination preserves source folder structure: `<carrier>/<target_folder>/<matched_source_folder>/<subtree>/<filename>`; no-upload when item is default work-item; consolidated `folder_routing.yaml` per TG (work_item_routing + folder_routing sections); FR-69 `portal_structure.yaml` retained for carrier mechanics |
| **FR-78** | Default work-item per milestone | Auto-instantiated at tracker creation; `item_type = Default`; tg_name = `_unrouted`; sort_order = max+1; not editable; not deletable; doc_count=0; review_required=false (immutable); **`milestone_gating=true` (hardcoded, immutable per 2026-06-13)**; accumulates unrouted documents (FR-52 step 5); does NOT gate carrier submission of other items; **EXCLUDED from FR-74 collection-phase-closure threshold**; **DOES gate `MilestoneAllClosed` (FR-28) and therefore FR-76 storage cleanup** — milestone cleanup cannot fire while default work-item has unresolved documents (reached `Closed` via TPM Mark Closed once all associated docs resolved via FR-83) |
| **FR-79** | Multi-item document association | **REVISED 2026-06-12 per storage 2026-06-07 file-centric refactor**: M:M join table `DocumentItemAssociation` with composite PK `(file_hash, delivery_item_id)` — **symmetric M:M, no primary/secondary distinction** (prior `association_kind: primary \| secondary` model dropped). One file_hash + N item associations; same file may exist at N NSD paths (one per item) per `[D-055]`. Fires on FR-52 step 1 when filename satisfies tag-set of multiple items. PLM upload fan-out: ONCE per distinct `(owner_email, plm_id)` pair across associations (not per item) — case (a) one owner N items = 1 upload; case (b) N owners = N uploads. Carrier portal upload: per-item per-association via per-item target_folder per FR-77 |
| **FR-80** | `no_customer_upload` per-item flag | Symmetric suppression — when True, BOTH PLM upload AND carrier portal upload disabled (independent of ingest channel); HILDA still receives + stores; FR-9/FR-10 outreach still fires; FR-53 review still fires per `review_required`; item advances to `Closed` via FR-7 TPM-Mark-Closed manual path (guards enforced); editable via FR-14; use case: TPM manually dispatches HILDA-collected docs out-of-band |
| **FR-82** | Tag catalog management (HILDA ops) | Ops-owned tags per (customer, milestone) in customer template `tag_catalog:` block; new tags auto-propagated to active trackers via SP REST writes (idempotent); audit logged with `action_type: tag_catalog_propagation`; tag uniqueness validation at template load (overlap raises `TSC-W004` warning, not fail); backup manual path (Ph-2): TPM "Refresh tags from template" per tracker |
| **FR-83** | Default work-item resolution (TPM-manual) | Ph-1: TPM resolves A→B→C in **HILDA tab** per FR-87 (moved from SP UI 2026-06-12 per `[D-074]`): (A) reassign to work-item via inline dropdown on document row; (B) resolve doc_type if `unresolved` or misaligned; (C) resolve revision if `[D-039]` deferred. Same-origin form POST to dashboard endpoints; HILDA updates `DocumentItemAssociation` (symmetric M:M per FR-79), clears `unrouted_source_path`, re-evaluates FR-86 storage matrix, moves NSD file to final path; `CommunicationLog` audit with `action_type: tpm_reassign_to_workitem` |
| **FR-73** | Submission package download *(amended 2026-05-28)* | **Package mirrors FR-18 carrier submission**: individual files only (NO audit zips); folder structure per FR-77 Type-2 routing (matches carrier portal layout); recursive folder structure preserved on TPM-local PC download; updated paths per `[D-013]` + `[D-050]` impl notes (no `un-resolved-zip\`, no per-item zip-store) — see FR-73 amendment in 2026-05-28 amendments subsection |
| **FR-76** | Milestone storage cleanup *(amended 2026-05-28)* | Updated paths per `[D-013]` + `[D-050]` impl notes: deletes NSD1 `internal/` milestone subtree + HILDA PC local cache; preserves NSD1 owner-inbound flat folder + NSD2 (read-only constraint) — see FR-76 amendment in 2026-05-28 amendments subsection |

| **FR-83** | Default work-item resolution *(extends Ph-1)* | Ph-2: automated owner outreach upstream of TPM via corp messenger ("Document X received but could not be routed; which item?"); owner replies via messenger structured response with item_id; HILDA performs reassignment same as TPM-manual; after M unanswered attempts escalates to TPM; cadence shares `escalation_schedule` AutomationRule (mirrors FR-10) |

| **FR-71** | Owner Discovery Function | At tracker creation: one corp messenger message per TG group to `tg_owner_corp_id` (personalized via `tg_owner_name`); lists all items grouped by current assigned per-item owner; structured reply — `confirmed` (no change) or `old_corp_id → new_corp_id` per-engineer reassignment within TG; TG-coordinator-as-delivery-engineer flag to PM; timeout → PM flag + proceed with defaults; SP UI shows ODF status per TG before Start Collection |
| **FR-72** | ZIP file ingestion | All ZIPs (Email/NSD/PLM-source) → `<tg_path_id>/_zip_audit/<original_zip_filename>.zip` co-located with `_zip_audit/<original_zip_filename>/<extracted-tree>/` (audit-only; never uploaded to PLM/carrier); per `[D-050]` impl note 2026-05-28 (supersedes prior `un-resolved-zip` framing); extraction + per-file FR-52 routing; `from_zip` + `source_zip_filename` index fields; AI-review eligible types: xlsx/xlsm/xls/pdf/doc/docx/ppt/pptx; **only individual extracted files submitted to PLM/carrier** (zips never); same-filename detection → `EML-W006` manual triage; all faults → `HildaOpsAlert` (DEF-21) |
| **FR-73** | Submission package download | Same guard as FR-63; async assembly on HILDA server; **individual files only (NO audit zips)** per FR-13/`[D-050]` (supersedes prior un-resolved-zip framing); folder structure per FR-77 Type-2 routing — matches carrier portal layout; completion via SP focus-aware refresh on any row's `download_package_status` field (column duplicated across all rows in the milestone — NOT JS polling per `[D-074]`); named `<carrier_slug>_<device_id>_<milestone_slug>_package.zip`; failure shown in UI + `HildaOpsAlert`; no state change |

### C.3 Non-functional requirements (all phases)

NFRs are architectural invariants active from Phase 1 onward. None are phase-gated.

| NFR | Area | Summary |
|-----|------|---------|
| **NFR-1** | Data boundary | All HILDA services on-prem; no cloud or SaaS LLM calls |
| **NFR-2** | Data boundary | Compact reports / logs leaving on-prem contain no proprietary content |
| **NFR-3** | Credential security | Per-PM isolation; Ph-1/Ph-2 secrets store = sops `.env` files per `[D-038]`; Vault / centralised secrets service deferred to Ph-3 (DEF-14) |
| **NFR-4** | Credential security | Ph-1/Ph-2: sops AES-256-GCM at rest, startup-time decrypt into process memory; TLS in transit; per-request decrypt + pod isolation + mTLS deferred to Ph-3 (DEF-14) |
| **NFR-5** | PM approval | No customer-facing outbound without explicit PM-approval signal recorded in `CommunicationLog` |
| **NFR-6** | PM accountability | Every external action attributable to a specific PM; `CommunicationLog` append-only |
| **NFR-7** | SharePoint | Deployment-specific SP values in `customizations/sharepoint_config/` only; none in `core/` |
| **NFR-8** | SharePoint | SharePoint REST API + on-prem AD auth (NTLM) against SP 2017; List CRUD + classic web parts only |
| **NFR-9** | Latency | Webhook/push: < 60 s; polled channels: within deadline-tiered poll interval (baseline configurable; ≤1-day tier: 5–15 min target); LLM tasks async (never block state transition); submission assembly progress tracked per-file via Redis |
| **NFR-10** | Reliability | All external service calls never silent-fail; retry tiers: (a) transient → exponential backoff base 30 s / max 30 min / 5 retries; (b) rate-limited → respect Retry-After; (c) non-recoverable → no retry, raise code immediately; auth failures route per NFR-11 |
| **NFR-11** | Reliability | Queue-and-alert pattern for all human-resolution failures: credential (step queued + PM alerted); staged doc (PM dashboard); LLM down (tasks queued + HildaOpsAlert); version-selection timeout (PM dashboard); FR-68 non-convergence (PM dashboard + HildaOpsAlert); beat missed schedules (resume from current time) |
| **NFR-12** | Adapter / build boundary | Dev LLM has no access to proprietary API specs, template schemas, or historical test reports |
| **NFR-13** | Adapter / build boundary | Generated proprietary artifacts under `customizations/` only; `core/` contains Protocols, Ingestors, public-vendor adapters |
| **NFR-14** | Extensibility | New item types, modalities, states extensible via configuration; no schema migrations |
| **NFR-15** | High availability | `restart: unless-stopped` in Docker Compose (Ph-1/Ph-2); HA replicas and MicroK8s deferred to Ph-3 (see `[D-043]`) |
| **NFR-16** | NSD boundary | NSD reads via HILDA-mediated download endpoint only; on-prem AD authentication required; direct UNC paths never exposed; per-DeliveryItem ACL deferred (DEF-18) |
| **NFR-17** | Chat-mediated collaboration | All module RPT / MET / FIX / QC reports contain only counts, flags, bounded enum tokens — no proprietary content |
| **NFR-18** | Chat-mediated collaboration | All failures raise registered error codes from central `error_codes.py` in format `{MODULE}-{E\|W}{NNN}` |
| **NFR-19** | Test interface | Every side-effect module ships `--mock` / `--dry-run`; every UI module ships mock web harness without production SP access |
| **NFR-20** | Task idempotency | All Celery tasks: `acks_late=True`; all external writes check "already done" via natural key; Redis AOF persistence required; all retries emit `WFL-W` warning code |
